\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. By the first step of the proof, we may view
\pi^{1}(X)
as a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
in such a way that the quotient group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})/\pi^{1}(X)
is isomorphic to
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Let
A
be the divisible hull of
\pi^{1}(X)
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
and
B
be a complementary summand of
A
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
. Then
A/\pi^{1}(X)
is a torsion group and
B
is a torsion-free group. Since
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})/\pi^{1}(X)\cong(A/\pi^{1}(X))\oplus B
, it follows that
A/\pi^{1}(X)\cong\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and
B\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathfrak{D}
. We claim that the following statements hold:

•

\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R}))\geq\mathfrak{c}
; indeed,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
is a real linear space, which contains a non-trivial group
\pi^{1}(X)
,

•

\mathop{\rm rank}(A)<\mathfrak{c}
; indeed, by the assumptions of the theorem,

\mathop{\rm rank}(A)=\mathop{\rm rank}(\pi^{1}(X))<\mathfrak{c}.

Thus, since
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})=A\oplus B
and
B\cong\mathfrak{D}
, we have
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathfrak{D})=\mathop{\rm rank}(B)\geq\mathfrak{c}
, as was to be shown.
∎

Chapter 4
Topological-Algebraic Aspects

4.1
Free objects for categories of maps

Our aim in this section is to prove the existence of free objects for certain categories of continuous base point preserving maps from a given pointed space into compact abelian groups. Our results from this section will be applied in the next section, where we show the existence of a free compact abelian group extension for an arbitrary minimal flow.

Let
X
be a pointed space with the base point
z
and
A
be a subgroup of the group
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
of all continuous base point preserving maps
X\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
. Given
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, let
A_{G}
denote the set of all
f\in C_{z}(X,G)
such that
\chi f\in A
for every
\chi\in G^{*}
. Clearly,
A_{G}
is a subgroup of
C_{z}(X,G)
. Moreover, if
G,H\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G,H)
, then
hf\in A_{H}
for every
f\in A_{G}
. Now consider the category
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
defined as follows. The objects of
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
are the elements of
A_{G}
with
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
arbitrary. For
f_{1}\in A_{G_{1}}
and
f_{2}\in A_{G_{2}}
, the set of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}(f_{1},f_{2})
in
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
consists of all
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G_{1},G_{2})
with
hf_{1}=f_{2}
. The composition of morphisms is obtained by restricting that from
\mathsf{CAGp}
. Clearly,
\mathop{\rm id}_{G}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(f,f)
for all
f\in A_{G}
and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
.

Our goal now is to show that
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
is a category with a free object. This is done in Theorem
4.1
below and some examples are given in Example
4.2
. Recall that a free object for
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
consists of a group
G_{A}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and of a map
f_{A}\in A_{G_{A}}
with the following universal property:

•

for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and every
f\in A_{G}
there is a unique
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G_{A},G)
with
hf_{A}=f
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
X
be a pointed space and
A
be a subgroup of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
equipped with the discrete topology. Set
G_{A}=A^{*}
and let
f_{A}\colon X\to G_{A}
be given by the rule
f_{A}(x)\colon A\ni\varphi\mapsto\varphi(x)\in\mathbb{T}^{1}
for every
x\in X
. Then
G_{A}
and
f_{A}
constitute a free object for the category
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
.

Proof.

The group
G_{A}
is compact abelian, being a dual group of a discrete abelian group
A
. The map
f_{A}
is well defined (that is,
f_{A}(x)\in A^{*}
for every
x\in X
), it is base point preserving and continuous. It therefore remains to verify the universality of
f_{A}
. That is, we must show that for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and every
f\in A_{G}
there is a unique
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G_{A},G)
with
hf_{A}=f
. Since the latter condition is an invariant of topological isomorphism with respect to
G
, we may assume that
G=D^{*}
for an appropriate group
D\in\mathsf{DAGp}
. Thus, we need to show that
k^{*}f_{A}=f
for one and only one
k\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,A)
. The uniqueness of
k
is clear, for the equality
k^{*}f_{A}=f
translates into
k(d)(x)=f(x)(d)
for all
d\in D
and
x\in X
, and this in turn is satisfied for at most one function
k\colon D\to A
. We finish the proof by showing that
k
thus defined is a (well defined) morphism of groups.

To see that
k
takes its values in
A
, fix
d\in D
and consider the character
\chi
of
D^{*}
given by
\chi(\Upsilon)=\Upsilon(d)
for every
\Upsilon\in D^{*}
. Then
\chi f=k(d)
. Since
f\in A_{D^{*}}
, it follows that
\chi f\in A
and hence
k(d)\in A
. The fact that
k
is a morphism of groups
D\to A
follows at once from the fact that
f(x)
is a morphism of groups
D\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
for every
x\in X
.
∎

Example 4.2
.

We present three examples of categories
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
and the ranges
G_{A}
of their free objects
f_{A}
. We shall abuse the terminology here and refer to the groups
G_{A}
as the free objects for the categories
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
.

(
\iota
)

Let
X
be a pointed space and
A
be the whole group
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
. Then
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
is the category of all continuous base point preserving maps
X\to G
with
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and the free object
G_{A}
for
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
is the free compact abelian group over
X
. If, for instance, the space
X
is compact connected second countable and non-degenerate then
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
is a real linear space with dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))=\mathop{\rm w}(X)^{\aleph_{0}}=(\aleph_{0})^{\aleph_{0}}=\mathfrak{c}
, and hence there are topological isomorphisms

\begin{split}G_{A}&=A^{*}=\left(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})_{d}\right)^{*}\cong\left(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})_{d}\oplus\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{*}\\
&\cong\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\oplus\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{*}\cong\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}\oplus\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{*}\\
&\cong b\mathbb{R}\times\pi^{1}(X)^{*}.\end{split}

(
\iota\iota
)

Let
X
be a non-degenerate compact connected second countable pointed space and
A
be the subgroup of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
formed by the null-homotopic maps. Then
A
is the identity arc-component of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
and it is naturally isomorphic to
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
. The category
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
consists of the continuous base point preserving null-homotopic maps
X\to G
with
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Similarly to (
\iota
), there are topological isomorphisms

G_{A}=A^{*}\cong\left(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})_{d}\right)^{*}\cong\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\right)^{*}\cong(\mathbb{R}_{d})^{*}\cong b\mathbb{R}.

(
\iota\iota\iota
)

Let
X
be a compact abelian group and
A=X^{*}
be the dual group of
X
. Clearly,
A
is a subgroup of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
. Since the characters of compact abelian groups separate points, we have
A_{G}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(X,G)
for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
is thus the category of the (topological) morphisms
X\to G
with
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. The free object
G_{A}
of
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
is given by the topological isomorphism
G_{A}=A^{*}=X^{**}\cong X
.

Corollary 4.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.1
, the category
\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
is isomorphic to the category
\mathsf{Hom}(G_{A},\mathsf{CAGp})
.

Proof.

We shall define a covariant functor
\mathsf{F}\colon\mathsf{Maps}_{A}\to\mathsf{Hom}(G_{A},\mathsf{CAGp})
and show that it is an isomorphism of categories. Given
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
f\in A_{G}
, we let
\mathsf{F}(f)
be the unique element of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G_{A},G)
with
\mathsf{F}(f)f_{A}=f
. By universality of
f_{A}
, this defines a bijection between
A_{G}
and
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G_{A},G)
. Further, given
G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
,
f_{1}\in A_{G_{1}}
,
f_{2}\in A_{G_{2}}
and
l\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(f_{1},f_{2})
, we set
\mathsf{F}(l)=l\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathsf{F}(f_{1}),\mathsf{F}(f_{2}))
. This definition is correct, for
l\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G_{1},G_{2})
and
lF(f_{1})=F(f_{2})
; indeed,
(lF(f_{1}))f_{A}=lf_{1}=f_{2}=F(f_{2})f_{A}
and hence, by universality of
f_{A}
,
lF(f_{1})=F(f_{2})
. Moreover, we see at once that
\mathsf{F}
defines a bijection between
\mathop{\rm Hom}(f_{1},f_{2})
and
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathsf{F}(f_{1}),\mathsf{F}(f_{2}))
. The functorial properties
\mathsf{F}(l_{2}l_{1})=\mathsf{F}(l_{2})\mathsf{F}(l_{1})
and
\mathsf{F}(\mathop{\rm id}_{f})=\mathop{\rm id}_{\mathsf{F}(f)}
are straightforward and so
\mathsf{F}
is indeed an isomorphism of categories.
∎

4.2
Free group extensions

Our aim in this section is to show that for every minimal flow
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a category with a free object. This free object consists of a group
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and of an extension
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})
, and is universal in the sense that every object from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is obtained as a composition of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
with a unique topological morphism. The existence of a free object in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is interesting from the categorical point of view, for it shows that
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
can be viewed as a category of morphisms of compact abelian groups. However, from the point of view of topological dynamics this fact becomes even more important: the dynamics of every extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is encoded in the dynamics of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

After showing that the free extension
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
of
\mathcal{F}
exists, we compute
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
in the case when
\Gamma
is a simply connected Lie group and describe relationships of
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
to some of the concepts introduced earlier. The existence of a free object for
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
becomes even more valuable to us in Section
4.4
, where we show that a free object for the category of the minimal extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
typically does not exist.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. The
free compact abelian group extension
(briefly, the
free extension
) of
\mathcal{F}
is defined as the free object for the category
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Recall that it consists of a group
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and an extension
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})
with the following universal property:

•

for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
there exists a unique
h_{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
with
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
.

The free extension of
\mathcal{F}
is of course unique up to a natural isomorphism. On occasions we shall take the liberty of abusing the terminology by referring either to
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
or to
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
as the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Equip the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with the discrete topology and define
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
as its Pontryagin dual

\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}=\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)_{d}^{*}.

Further, let
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})
be given by

\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\ni\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x)\in\mathbb{T}^{1}

for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
x\in X
. Then
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
is the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

Fix
z\in X
and set
A=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
A
is a subgroup of
C_{(1,z)}(\Gamma\times X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
. Moreover, under the notation from Section
4.1
, we have
A_{G}=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, for the characters of compact abelian groups separate points. Consequently,
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
and the statement of the theorem thus follows directly from Theorem
4.1
.
∎

Example 4.6
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
simply connected and with
X
compact second countable. Assume that both
\Gamma
and
X
are infinite. We show that there is a topological isomorphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong b\mathbb{R}
.
Indeed, by virtue of Corollary
3.41
and Remark
3.42
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a real linear space with dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathfrak{c}
. Consequently,

\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}=\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)_{d}^{*}\cong\left(\mathbb{R}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\right)_{d}^{*}\cong\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}\right)^{*}=b\mathbb{R}.

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
be the model of the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
constructed in Theorem
4.5
. Then the following statements hold.

(1)

The category
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is isomorphic to the category
\mathsf{Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathsf{CAGp})
.

(2)

Under the identification
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}=(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{**}\cong\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)_{d}
it holds

F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\hskip 11.38109pt\text{and}\hskip 11.38109pt\left(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{*}=\emph{Id}_{\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}.

(3)

Let
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
\kappa\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}
be the quotient morphism,
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}=\kappa\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
z
be the base point of
X
and
\vartheta
be the base point preserving transfer function for
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}
. Then
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}
is the free compact abelian group over
X
with respect to the universal map
\vartheta
.

(4)

For every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
there is an isomorphism of groups

\widetilde{\Phi}_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\ni\mathcal{C}\mapsto\left(\omega_{G}\right)^{-1}\Phi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}=h_{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G\right),

where
\omega_{G}
is the Pontryagin isomorphism
G\to G^{**}
,
\Phi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}=\mathcal{C}^{**}
stands for the morphism dual to
\Phi_{G}(\mathcal{C})=\mathcal{C}^{*}
and the morphism
h_{\mathcal{C}}
is defined by the formula
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
.

Remark 4.8
.

We wish to make the following observations.

•

Under the identification from statement (2),
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
corresponds to
(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp\perp}\subseteq(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{**}=(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
. Consequently, the isomorphism
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
yields an equality
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}
and a topological isomorphism
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{*}=(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{*}
. Under this isomorphism the identity component
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})_{0}
of
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
corresponds to the group
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}
. Consequently, the group
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
is connected if and only if the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free. Similarly,
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
is torsion-free if and only if
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible.

•

Recall that
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}
is topologically isomorphic to the right-hand side of (
2.17
) if the base space
X
of the flow
\mathcal{F}
is compact. If
X
is additionally assumed connected (which is the case if
\Gamma
is connected) then the middle term of the right-hand side of (
2.17
) vanishes.

Proof of Theorem
4.7
.

Statement (1) follows from Theorem
4.1
, Corollary
4.3
and Theorem
4.5
, for
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathsf{Maps}_{A}
with
A=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

In order to verify statement (2), we adopt the following notation. Given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, let
\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}
be the image of
\mathcal{C}
under the Pontryagin isomorphism

\omega_{(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}}\colon(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}\to(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{**}=(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}.

Then
\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
. From this observation and from the properties of the functor
F
we obtain the equivalence of the following conditions for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
:

•

\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}\in F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}
,

•

\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=1
,

•

F(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=1
,

•

F(\mathcal{C})=1
,

•

\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

This verifies the first statement from (2). As for the second statement from (2), for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}(\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}})=\Upsilon_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
, and so the action of
(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
on
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is indeed identical.

We verify statement (3). First observe that
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}=\kappa\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a coboundary and the map
\vartheta
is thus well defined; indeed, we have
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}})=F(\kappa\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=\kappa F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=e
and hence
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}})
. Now let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\xi\colon X\to G
be a continuous base point preserving map; we need to show that there is a unique topological morphism
\sigma\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}\to G
with
\sigma\vartheta=\xi
. By universality of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
, there exists a unique
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
with
h\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
. Since
e=F(\mathop{\rm co}(\xi))=F(h\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=hF(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
h
factors through
\kappa
onto a morphism
\sigma\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}\to G
; that is,
\sigma\kappa=h
. Thus,

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)=h\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\sigma\kappa\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\sigma\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}=\sigma\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta)=\mathop{\rm co}(\sigma\vartheta)

and, since both
\xi
and
\sigma\vartheta
are base point preserving maps, it follows from minimality of
\mathcal{F}
that
\xi=\sigma\vartheta
. To verify the uniqueness of
\sigma
with the latter property assume that
\varrho\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}},G)
also satisfies
\varrho\vartheta=\xi
. Then

\sigma\kappa\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=h\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)=\mathop{\rm co}(\varrho\vartheta)=\varrho\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta)=\varrho\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{c}}=\varrho\kappa\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}

and hence, by universality of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
\sigma\kappa=\varrho\kappa
. Finally, since
\kappa
is an epimorphism, it follows that
\sigma=\varrho
. This finishes the proof of statement (3).

We verify statement (4). Fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Recall that there is an isomorphism of groups

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\ni k\mapsto\left(\omega_{G}\right)^{-1}k^{*}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G).

Since
\Phi_{G}
is an isomorphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
by virtue of Theorem
3.1
, it follows that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{G}
is an isomorphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
. Thus, to finish the proof of (4), it remains to show that
(\omega_{G})^{-1}\mathcal{C}^{**}=h_{\mathcal{C}}
or, equivalently, that
\mathcal{C}^{**}=\omega_{G}h_{\mathcal{C}}
for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. So fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. By universality of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
and by virtue of the identity
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
, the desired equality
\mathcal{C}^{**}=\omega_{G}h_{\mathcal{C}}
is equivalent with
\mathcal{C}^{**}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\omega_{G}\mathcal{C}
. It is the latter equality that we now verify. Observe that both
\mathcal{C}^{**}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\omega_{G}\mathcal{C}
are elements of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G^{**})
.

Fix
\gamma\in\Gamma
,
x\in X
and
\chi\in G^{*}
. Then

\left(\mathcal{C}^{**}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)(\gamma,x)\colon\chi\mapsto\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)(\mathcal{C}^{*}(\chi))=\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)(\chi\mathcal{C})=\chi\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x)

by definition of
\mathcal{C}^{*}
and
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Likewise,

\left(\omega_{G}\mathcal{C}\right)(\gamma,x)\colon\chi\mapsto\omega_{G}(\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x))(\chi)=\chi(\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x))=\chi\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x)

by definition of
\omega_{G}
. This verifies the equality
\mathcal{C}^{**}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\omega_{G}\mathcal{C}
.
∎

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
be the model of the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
constructed in Theorem
4.5
. Then the following statements hold.

(
\iota
)

For
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
let
h_{\mathcal{C}}
be the unique morphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to G
with
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
and let
H_{\mathcal{C}}=\ker(h_{\mathcal{C}})
,
G_{\mathcal{C}}=\mathop{\rm im}(h_{\mathcal{C}})
. Then

(a)

F(\mathcal{C})=h_{\mathcal{C}}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

(b)

(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}=\mathcal{C}^{*}
(under the identification
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
),

(c)

H_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}=\mathop{\rm im}((h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*})=\mathop{\rm im}(\mathcal{C}^{*})
,

(d)

G_{\mathcal{C}}
is the closed subgroup of
G
generated by the values of
\mathcal{C}
.

(
\iota\iota
)

For all
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,

(ii)

F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq H_{\mathcal{C}}
.

(
\iota\iota\iota
)

For all
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(
\alpha
)

\mathcal{C}
is minimal,

(
\beta
)

G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
and
H_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}\cap F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=0
,

(
\gamma
)

G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
and
H_{\mathcal{C}}+F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Proof.

We begin by verifying (
\iota
). Statement (a) follows immediately from the definition of
h_{\mathcal{C}}
and from the properties of the functor
F
. To verify statement (b), observe that
(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}=\mathcal{C}^{*}
by definition of
h_{\mathcal{C}}
. Moreover, under the identification
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
equals the identity on
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
by statement (2) of Theorem
4.7
. Thus, it follows that
(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}=\mathcal{C}^{*}
. The first equality in (c) follows by a simple computation
H_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}=\ker(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{\perp}=\mathop{\rm im}((h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*})
and the second equality follows from (b). Let
V_{\mathcal{C}}
be the closed subgroup of
G
generated by the values of
\mathcal{C}
. Clearly,
\chi\in G^{*}
belongs to
V_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}
if and only if
\mathcal{C}^{*}(\chi)=\chi\mathcal{C}=1
. Thus, by virtue of (b),
V_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}=\ker(\mathcal{C}^{*})=\ker((h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*})=\mathop{\rm im}(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{\perp}=G_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}
. Since
V_{\mathcal{C}}
and
G_{\mathcal{C}}
are closed subgroups of
G
by definition, it follows that
G_{\mathcal{C}}=V_{\mathcal{C}}
. This verifies statement (d).

Statement (
\iota\iota
) follows at once from part (a) of statement (
\iota
) and from Theorem
3.7
(2).

We finish the proof of the proposition by verifying statement (
\iota\iota\iota
). By part (a) of statement (
\iota
), we have
F(\mathcal{C})^{\perp}=(h_{\mathcal{C}}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}))^{\perp}=((h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*})^{-1}(F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp})
. Thus, by part (c) of statement (
\iota
), the following conditions are equivalent:

•

\mathcal{C}
is minimal,

•

F(\mathcal{C})=G
,

•

((h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*})^{-1}(F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp})=0
,

•

(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}
is a monomorphism and
\mathop{\rm im}((h_{\mathcal{C}})^{*})\cap F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=0
,

•

h_{\mathcal{C}}
is an epimorphism and
H_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}\cap F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=0
,

•

G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
and
H_{\mathcal{C}}^{\perp}\cap F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=0
.

This verifies the equivalence of (
\alpha
) and (
\beta
). The equivalence of (
\beta
) and (
\gamma
) follows from basic properties of the annihilator mechanism.
∎

4.3
Divisibility and torsion-freeness

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. In this section we study two important properties of the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
(
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
), namely their torsion-freeness and divisibility. First, we relate the torsion-freeness of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
to the topological(-algebraic) properties of
X
and
\Gamma
. Then we relate the divisibility of the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
to the divisibility of the single group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and show how it is reflected in the existence of lifts of extensions across various types of epimorphisms within
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. We also gain from these results some useful information about the free extension
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathcal{F}
.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Then the following statements are equivalent:

(i)

\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
is connected,

(ii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free,

(iii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is torsion-free for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
,

(iv)

the space
X
is connected and the group
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups.

Remark 4.11
.

Let us mention the following facts.

•

In connection with (iv) observe that a group
\Gamma
may posses a non-trivial finite quotient group and still not possess a non-trivial finite
abelian
quotient group. For instance, a finite non-abelian simple group (say, the alternating group
A_{5}
) has no proper normal subgroups and hence it possesses no abelian quotient groups other than the trivial one. Of course, our interest is in infinite topological transformation groups, but our remark now applies for instance to
\Gamma=\mathbb{R}\times A_{5}
.

•

A discrete abelian group
\Gamma
possesses no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups if and only if it is divisible. This occurs if and only if its dual group
\Gamma^{*}
is torsion-free.

•

The two conditions from (iv) are automatically satisfied if the group
\Gamma
is connected, which is the situation of our main interest.

Proof of Theorem
4.10
.

The equivalence of (i) and (ii) is an immediate consequence of the definition of
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Implication (iii)
\Rightarrow
(ii) is clear. The converse implication follows from Theorem
3.1
, which yields

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))&\cong\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\text{tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))\\
&=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\text{tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))=0\end{split}

for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
.

To show that (iv)
\Rightarrow
(ii), assume that
X
is connected and
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups. Fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
\mathcal{C}^{k}=1
. Then
\mathcal{C}
takes its values in
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
. By connectedness of
X
, the mapping
l\colon\Gamma\ni\gamma\mapsto\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x)\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}
does not depend on the choice of
x\in X
and from the cocycle identity for
\mathcal{C}
it follows that
l
is a morphism of topological groups. It is clearly a quotient morphism onto its image. Since the group
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups by our assumptions, it follows that
l=1
and hence
\mathcal{C}=1
. Thus, indeed,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a torsion-free group.

We finish the proof by showing that (ii)
\Rightarrow
(iv). Assume that condition (iv) is not satisfied and consider first the case of
X
not connected. Fix a continuous map
\xi\colon X\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
with
\xi(X)=\{\pm 1\}
. Then, by minimality of
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
is a non-trivial element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)^{2}=1
and hence
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\neq\emptyset
. Now consider the case when
\Gamma
has a non-trivial finite abelian quotient group. Then
\Gamma
factors onto
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
for some
k\geq 2
and the quasi-coboundary induced by the underlying epimorphism
\Gamma\to\mathbb{Z}_{k}
is a non-trivial element of
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. This verifies the desired implication.
∎

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free,

(2)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible,

(3)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected,

(4)

the objects of
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
lift across the finite-to-one epimorphisms from
\mathsf{CAGp}
,

(5)

the objects of
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
lift across all epimorphisms from
\mathsf{CAGp}
.

(6)

for every every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and every
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
, the canonical sequence

0\longrightarrow H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\longrightarrow}}G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}G/H\longrightarrow 0

gives rise to the short exact sequence of abelian groups

0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G/H)\longrightarrow 0.

Remark 4.13
.

The assumption of connectedness of
G
in condition (3) is in general not redundant, for otherwise the morphisms
\kappa_{k}\colon G\to G
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
) would not be surjective. However, if we restrict ourselves to the connected topological transformation groups in the base (which is the situation that interests us most) then condition (3) holds for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Indeed, in such case all extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
take their values in
G_{0}
and
\kappa_{k}(G_{0})=G_{0}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof of Theorem
4.12
.

The equivalence of (1) and (2) follows immediately from the definition of
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Further, implications (5)
\Rightarrow
(4), (3)
\Rightarrow
(2) and (4)
\Rightarrow
(2) hold obviously. Also, (5) implies (3), since the endomorphisms
\kappa_{k}
of
G
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
) are epimorphisms for every connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Thus, in order to prove the equivalence of (1)–(5), it suffices now to show that (2) implies (5). So assume that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. Fix
G,H\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, an epimorphism
p\colon H\to G
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Then
\mathcal{C}^{*}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and
p^{*}\colon G^{*}\to H^{*}
is a monomorphism. By divisibility of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
\mathcal{C}^{*}
extends through
p^{*}
to a morphism
h\colon H^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; that is,
hp^{*}=\mathcal{C}^{*}
. By virtue of Theorem
3.1
,
h=\mathcal{D}^{*}
for an appropriate
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
. Then
\mathcal{D}^{*}p^{*}=\mathcal{C}^{*}
, which translates into
p\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}
. This verifies condition (5).

We finish the proof by verifying the equivalence (2)
\Leftrightarrow
(6). First, assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and fix
G,H
as in (6). Then
\mathop{\rm Ext}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
and statement (6) thus follows from the exactness of (
3.3
) in Corollary
3.4
. Conversely, assume that condition (6) holds. We show that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible by verifying the identity
\mathop{\rm Ext}(A,\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
for every
A\in\mathsf{DAGp}
. So fix
A\in\mathsf{DAGp}
and set
H=A^{*}
. Clearly,
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
for an appropriate torus
G
. Since the group
G^{*}
is free abelian, it follows that
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
and so the exact sequence (
3.3
) takes the form

0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G/H)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow 0.

(4.1)

By virtue of condition (6),
\widehat{p}
is an epimorphism and hence, by exactness of the sequence (
4.1
),
\mathop{\rm Ext}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
. Thus,
\mathop{\rm Ext}(A,\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
, as was to be shown.
∎

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free and connected,

(b)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and torsion-free,

(c)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
,

(d)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible and torsion-free for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
,

(e)

the objects of
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
lift uniquely across the epimorphisms from
\mathsf{CAGp}
with finite kernels,

(f)

the objects of
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
lift uniquely across the epimorphisms from
\mathsf{CAGp}
with totally disconnected kernels.

Remark 4.15
.

In connection with condition (b), recall the following facts. If
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
is a minimal flow whose acting group
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups and whose base space
X
is connected then the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free, see Theorem
4.10
. If
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
is simply connected and
X
is compact then
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible, see Remark
3.39
.

Proof of Theorem
4.14
.

First, the equivalence of (a) and (b) follows immediately from the definition of
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Further, implications (f)
\Rightarrow
(e) and (d)
\Rightarrow
(c) are obvious. Also, (b) follows from (e) at once, for the endomorphisms
\kappa_{k}
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
) of
\mathbb{T}^{1}
have finite kernels. To finish the proof, it is sufficient to verify implications (b)
\Rightarrow
(d), (c)
\Rightarrow
(b) and (b)
\Rightarrow
(f).

We show that (d) follows from (b). So assume that (b) holds and fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free, it follows that so is
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Further, since
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free and divisible, all the morphisms
\kappa_{k}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
) are isomorphisms and hence the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is divisible. Thus,
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is both divisible and torsion-free and so, by Theorem
3.1
, it follows that so is
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. This verifies condition (d).

We show that (b) follows from (c). So assume that all the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
(
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
) are divisible; we show that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and torsion-free. The divisibility of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is immediate. To see that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is also torsion-free, fix an integer
k\geq 2
. Then, by virtue of Theorem
3.1
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{k})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Since
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{k})
is divisible by the assumptions, it follows that
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
. This verifies condition (b).

We finish the proof of the theorem by showing that (f) follows from (b). So assume that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is both divisible and torsion-free. Fix an epimorphism
p\colon H\to G
in
\mathsf{CAGp}
with a totally disconnected kernel and an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
; we wish to show that
p\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}
holds for a unique extension
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
. The existence of such
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
follows from the implication (2)
\Rightarrow
(5) from Theorem
4.12
. To verify the uniqueness part, assume that
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
are such that
p\mathcal{D}_{i}=\mathcal{C}
for
i=1,2
. Then
\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{-1}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\ker(p))
. Since
\ker(p)\in\mathsf{CAGp}
is totally disconnected, its dual group
\ker(p)^{*}
is a torsion group and hence
\chi(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{-1})\in\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
for every
\chi\in H^{*}
. By virtue of (b), the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free and hence
\chi(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{-1})=1
for every
\chi\in H^{*}
. Thus,
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}_{2}
, as was to be shown.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free and divisible. Then the following statements hold.

(i)

For every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and every
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
totally disconnected, the quotient morphism
p\colon G\to G/H
induces an isomorphism of groups

\widehat{p}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G/H).

The isomorphism is topological, provided
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
is connected and
X
is compact.

(ii)

For every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected, the projective resolution
(
2.18
)
of
G
induces isomorphisms of groups

\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{\dim(G)}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathfrak{P}(G))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{\mathfrak{p}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G).

The isomorphisms are topological, provided
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
is connected and
X
is compact.

(iii)

For every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected, the maximal toral quotient sequence
(
2.13
)
of
G
induces isomorphisms of groups

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{q}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}(G))\cong(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\dim(G)}.

The isomorphisms are topological, provided
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
is connected and
X
is compact.

(iv)

Assume that both
\Gamma
and
X
are locally compact second countable and that
X
is non-degenerate. Then there are an isomorphism of abelian groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{R}
and a topological isomorphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong b\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

We begin by verifying statement (i). First, the morphism
\widehat{p}
is an isomorphism by virtue of statement (f) from Theorem
4.14
. Further, let
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
be connected and
X
be compact. Then both
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G/H)
are topological groups with the topology of u.c.s. convergence and
\widehat{p}
is a bijective morphism of topological groups. We show that
\widehat{p}
is in fact a topological isomorphism by showing that it is open at the identity. So let
\mathcal{U}
be an identity neighbourhood in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. By our assumptions on
\Gamma
and
X
, there exist a compact connected set
1\in C\subseteq\Gamma
and an identity neighbourhood
V
in
G
such that
\mathcal{U}\supseteq[C\times X;V]
. Let
K\subseteq V
be an open subgroup of
H
. The quotient group
F=H/K
is then finite. Further, for every
f\in F
, fix
h_{f}\in f
. Since the cosets
K+h_{f}
(
f\in F
) are compact, there is an identity neighbourhood
V^{\prime}
in
G
with
V^{\prime}+K\subseteq V
and with the sets
V^{\prime}+K+h_{f}
(
f\in F
) mutually disjoint.

We claim that
\widehat{p}(\mathcal{U})\supseteq[C\times X;p(V^{\prime})]
; since
[C\times X;p(V^{\prime})]
is clearly an identity neighbourhood in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G/H)
, this will finish the proof of (i). So let
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G/H)
be an extension with
\mathcal{D}(C\times X)\subseteq p(V^{\prime})
and let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
be the lift of
\mathcal{D}
across
p
; we show that
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{U}
. Then

\mathcal{C}(C\times X)\subseteq p^{-1}p(V^{\prime})=V^{\prime}+H=\bigcup_{f\in F}(V^{\prime}+K+h_{f}).

Since the set
C\times X
is connected, the sets
V^{\prime}+K+h_{f}
(
f\in F
) are open and mutually disjoint, and
e\in\mathcal{C}(C\times X)\cap(V^{\prime}+K+h_{0})\neq\emptyset
, it follows that

\mathcal{C}(C\times X)\subseteq V^{\prime}+K+h_{0}=V^{\prime}+K\subseteq V.

Thus,
\mathcal{C}\in[C\times X;V]\subseteq\mathcal{U}
, as was to be shown.

We verify statement (ii). The fact that
\widehat{\mathfrak{p}}
is a (topological) isomorphism follows from statement (i), for the kernel
\mathfrak{K}(G)
of
\mathfrak{p}
is totally disconnected. We verify the other isomorphism from (ii). By definition of
\mathfrak{P}(G)
, there are topological isomorphisms

\mathfrak{P}(G)=(\mathbb{Q}\otimes G^{*})^{*}\cong\left(\mathbb{Q}^{\left(\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})\right)}\right)^{*}=\left(\mathbb{Q}^{\left(\dim(G)\right)}\right)^{*}\cong\left(\mathbb{Q}^{*}\right)^{\dim(G)}.

Moreover, since
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Q}^{*}
, the part of statement (ii) already proved yields an isomorphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{Q}^{*})\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{1})=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
(the isomorphism is in fact topological, provided
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
is connected and
X
is compact). It follows that there are isomorphisms of abelian groups

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathfrak{P}(G))\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\left((\mathbb{Q}^{*})^{\dim(G)}\right)\cong\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{Q}^{*})\right)^{\dim(G)}\cong\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{\dim(G)}.

If
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
is connected and
X
is compact then all the three isomorphisms above are topological. This finishes the proof of (ii).

We verify statement (iii). The fact that
\widehat{q}
is a (topological) isomorphism follows from statement (i), for the kernel
\mathbb{K}(G)
of
q
is totally disconnected. The other (topological) isomorphism from (iii) follows from (topological) isomorphisms

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}(G))\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})}\right)=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{\dim(G)}\right)\cong\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{\dim(G)}.

We finish the proof of the proposition by verifying statement (iv). The group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, being divisible and torsion-free, is a rational linear space. Write
\mathfrak{k}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. By our assumptions on
\Gamma
and
X
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a Polish group with the topology of u.c.s. convergence and hence its cardinality is at most
\mathfrak{c}
. Consequently,

\mathfrak{k}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\leq\mathop{\rm card}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\leq\mathfrak{c}.

(4.2)

Further, by our assumptions on
X
,
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\neq 0
. Therefore,
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))\geq 1
and hence
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Since the space
X
is connected by virtue of Theorem
4.10
,
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains an isomorphic copy of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
. Consequently,

\mathfrak{k}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\geq\mathop{\rm rank}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))=\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))\geq\mathfrak{c}.

(4.3)

From (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) we infer that
\mathfrak{k}=\mathfrak{c}
. This yields isomorphisms of groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{k})}=\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\cong\mathbb{R}
and topological isomorphisms
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{*}\cong(\mathbb{R}_{d})^{*}\cong b\mathbb{R}
.
∎

4.4
The non-existence of free minimal extensions

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. In Section
4.2
we have seen that the category
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
always possesses a free object. Our aim in this section is to show that this is not the case for the subcategory of
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
formed by the minimal extensions of
\mathcal{F}
. The main results of this section are Theorems
4.23
and
4.27
. The first of them states that if both
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
are non-trivial then a free object for the category of the minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
does not exist. (This applies, for instance, to flows satisfying one of the three assumptions listed at the beginning of Section
6.1
in Chapter
6
.) The second theorem states that every minimal extension
\mathcal{C}
from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is an epimorphic image of a minimal extension
\beth
, which is maximal in the sense that it can not be expressed as a non-isomorphic epimorphic image of another minimal extension
\beth^{\prime}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a minimal flow. Throughout this whole section the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is assumed to carry the discrete topology. We use notation
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
for the model of the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
constructed in Section
4.2
. Given
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, we let
h_{\mathcal{C}}
stand for the unique morphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to G
with
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
. We also write
H_{\mathcal{C}}=\ker(h_{\mathcal{C}})
and
G_{\mathcal{C}}=\mathop{\rm im}(h_{\mathcal{C}})
. Recall from Proposition
4.9
(
\iota
) that
G_{\mathcal{C}}
is the closed subgroup of
G
generated by the values of
\mathcal{C}
. Consequently, if
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
h
is a morphism
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
then
G_{\mathcal{D}}=h(G_{\mathcal{C}})
. Finally, given a subgroup
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, we use the symbol
p_{\Lambda}
to denote the quotient morphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}
.

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
be its free extension. Then the following statements hold.

(1)

For every subgroup
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, the values of
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
generate a dense subgroup of
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}
; that is,
G_{p_{\Lambda\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}
. In particular,
G_{\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

(2)

Given a group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with
G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
, there is a unique subgroup
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
such that
\mathcal{C}
is isomorphic to
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Proof.

We begin by verifying the identity
G_{\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
. To this end, we need to show that for every
\Upsilon\in(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
,
\Upsilon\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
implies
\Upsilon=1
. First, denote by
\omega
the Pontryagin isomorphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{**}=(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
corresponding to
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Now, fix
\Upsilon\in(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
with
\Upsilon\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
and write
\mathcal{C}=\omega^{-1}(\Upsilon)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
x\in X
,

\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x)=\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)(\mathcal{C})=\Upsilon(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x))=\Upsilon\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)=1.

Thus,
\mathcal{C}=1
and hence
\Upsilon=1
. Now we turn to the proof of the main statement from (1). So fix a subgroup
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then

G_{p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}=p_{\Lambda}(G_{\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}})=p_{\Lambda}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp},

as was to be shown.

Now fix
G
and
\mathcal{C}
as in (2). First we check the existence part of statement (2). Let
h_{\mathcal{C}}
be the morphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to G
with
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
. We claim that
h_{\mathcal{C}}
is an epimorphism; indeed, by virtue of (1),

G=G_{\mathcal{C}}=G_{h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}=h_{\mathcal{C}}(G_{\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}})=h_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}).

Consequently, there exist a subgroup
\Lambda
of the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and a topological isomorphism
\varphi\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}\to G
such that
\varphi p_{\Lambda}=h_{\mathcal{C}}
. Then
\varphi(p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
, whence it follows that
\varphi
is an isomorphism
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathcal{C}
.

Now we check the uniqueness part of statement (2). So let
\Lambda
satisfy the conclusion of (2) and fix an underlying isomorphism
\varphi\colon p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathcal{C}
. Then
(\varphi p_{\Lambda})\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}=h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
, and so
\varphi p_{\Lambda}
and
h_{\mathcal{C}}
agree on the set of all values of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. By virtue of (1), this means that
\varphi p_{\Lambda}=h_{\mathcal{C}}
on the whole
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Since
\varphi
is an isomorphism by the assumptions, we obtain
\Lambda^{\perp}=\ker(p_{\Lambda})=\ker(h_{\mathcal{C}})
. Thus,
\Lambda=\omega^{-1}(\Lambda^{\perp\perp})=\omega^{-1}(\ker(h_{\mathcal{C}})^{\perp})
. This verifies the uniqueness part of (2).
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Then the following statements hold.

(i)

Given groups
H,K\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
,
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(K)
with
G_{\mathcal{C}}=H
and
G_{\mathcal{D}}=K
, the set
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
is either a singleton or empty. In the first case the corresponding morphism from
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
is an epimorphism.

(ii)

Given subgroups
\Lambda
and
\Xi
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, the set
\mathop{\rm Hom}(p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}},p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
is non-empty if and only if
\Xi\subseteq\Lambda
.

Remark 4.19
.

Recall that if
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is minimal then
G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
. Consequently, statement (i) from Lemma
4.18
, as well as statement (2) from Lemma
4.17
, applies to all minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.18
.

Fix
H
,
K
,
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
as in (i) and assume that
\varphi
is a morphism
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
. Consider the morphisms
h_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to H
and
h_{\mathcal{D}}\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to K
with
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
and
h_{\mathcal{D}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{D}
, respectively. Then
(\varphi h_{\mathcal{C}})\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\varphi\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}=h_{\mathcal{D}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
and hence, by Lemma
4.17
(1),
\varphi h_{\mathcal{C}}=h_{\mathcal{D}}
. Since both
h_{\mathcal{C}}
and
h_{\mathcal{D}}
are epimorphisms by our assumptions on
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, it follows that such a morphism
\varphi
is at most one and, if it exists, it is in fact an epimorphism. This verifies statement (i).

Now fix subgroups
\Lambda
and
\Xi
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. First we verify the “if” part of statement (ii). So assume that
\Xi\subseteq\Lambda
. Then
\Lambda^{\perp}\subseteq\Xi^{\perp}
and so there is a morphism
\varphi\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}\to\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Xi^{\perp}
with
\varphi p_{\Lambda}=p_{\Xi}
. Consequently,
\varphi(p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
and hence
\varphi
is a desired morphism
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\to p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Now we turn to proving the “only if” part of statement (ii). So let
\varphi
be a morphism of extensions
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\to p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
\varphi
is a morphism of topological groups
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}\to\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Xi^{\perp}
with
(\varphi p_{\Lambda})\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Thus, by Lemma
4.17
(1),
\varphi p_{\Lambda}=p_{\Xi}
. This shows that
\Lambda^{\perp}\subseteq\Xi^{\perp}
and hence
\Xi\subseteq\Lambda
.
∎

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow,
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
be the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
and
\Lambda
be a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp})
is minimal if and only if
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.17
(1),
G_{p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}
. Moreover,
H_{p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}}=\ker(p_{\Lambda})=\Lambda^{\perp}
and, by the first part of Remark
4.8
,
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}
. Consequently, it follows from Proposition
4.9
(
\iota\iota\iota
) that the following conditions are equivalent:

•

p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
is minimal,

•

(H_{p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}})^{\perp}\cap F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=1
,

•

\Lambda^{\perp\perp}\cap(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp\perp}=1

•

\Lambda\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
.

∎

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a minimal flow,
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}})
. We say that
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}})
is a
free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
if the following condition holds:

(
m
)

for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and every minimal extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
there exists
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}},G)
with
q\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=\mathcal{C}
.

Observe that the morphism
q
from (
m
) is unique by Lemma
4.18
(i). Consequently, if a free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
does exist then it is unique up to an isomorphism. We shall therefore speak of
the
free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

the free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
exists,

(b)

there is a largest subgroup
\Omega
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\Omega\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
.

Remark 4.22
.

Assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free; that is,
X
is connected and
\Gamma
has no finite abelian quotient groups. Then a subgroup
\Omega
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
satisfies
\Omega\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
if and only if it is a subgroup of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.21
.

We show that (b) follows from (a). So assume that
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}})
is the free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
and denote by
h
the unique morphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}
with
h\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}
. By Lemma
4.17
(2), we may assume, without loss of generality, that
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Omega^{\perp}
and
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=p_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
for an appropriate subgroup
\Omega
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. We show that
\Omega
fulfills condition (b). First, by virtue of Lemma
4.20
, the minimality of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=p_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
yields
\Omega\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
. Further, fix
\Lambda\mathrel{\lhd}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
; we show that
\Lambda\subseteq\Omega
. By virtue of Lemma
4.20
,
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp})
is a minimal extension. Since
p_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
is the free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
, we have
\mathop{\rm Hom}(p_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}},p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\neq\emptyset
. Thus, by Lemma
4.18
(ii),
\Lambda\subseteq\Omega
.

We show that (a) follows from (b). So let
\Omega
be as in (b). Set
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Omega^{\perp}
and
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=p_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
; we show that
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}},\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}})
is the free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
. First, the extension
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}
is minimal by Lemma
4.20
. Further, fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and a minimal extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
; we need to find a morphism
q\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}\to G
with
q\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}=\mathcal{C}
. By Lemma
4.17
(2), we may assume, without loss of generality, that
G=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}
and
\mathcal{C}=p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
for an appropriate subgroup
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. By virtue of Lemma
4.20
,
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
and hence
\Lambda\subseteq\Omega
by our choice of
\Omega
. Lemma
4.18
(ii) then yields a desired morphism
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}}\to p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
.
∎

Theorem 4.23
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with both
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
non-trivial. Then the free minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
does not exist.

Remark 4.24
.

The assumptions of the theorem are satisfied, provided the flow
\mathcal{F}
fulfills one of the three assumptions listed at the beginning of Section
6.1
in Chapter
6
. First, in all the three situations
X
is a non-trivial compact space and so the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
is non-trivial. Second, it follows from Theorems
6.3
,
6.5
and
6.6
that the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is also non-trivial.

Proof of Theorem
4.23
.

We proceed by contradiction, assuming that
\mathcal{F}
does possess the free minimal extension. By virtue of Lemma
4.21
, there is a largest subgroup
\Omega
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with the property
\Omega\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
. Fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1
and
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1
. Then the subgroups
\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle
and
\langle\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}\rangle
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
generated by
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}
, respectively, are contained in the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, and hence they intersect the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at the identity. Thus, by definition of
\Omega
, both
\langle\mathcal{C}\rangle
and
\langle\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}\rangle
are contained in
\Omega
. Consequently,
\mathcal{D}=(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D})\mathcal{C}^{-1}\in\Omega\cap(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1)
, which contradicts the definition of
\Omega
.
∎

Lemma 4.25
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow,
\beth
be a minimal extension from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\Lambda
be the subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
isomorphic to
\beth
. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(
\alpha
)

every minimal extension
\beth^{\prime}
from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\beth^{\prime},\beth)\neq\emptyset
is isomorphic to
\beth
,

(
\beta
)

\Lambda
is maximal (with respect to inclusion) among the subgroups of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
intersecting
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at the identity.

Remark 4.26
.

We wish to mention the following facts.

•

Condition (
\alpha
) can be reformulated by saying that
\beth
is a maximal object in the category of the minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

•

By Lemma
4.17
(2), the isomorphism classes of the extensions
\beth
satisfying (
\alpha
) are in a one-to-one correspondence with the subgroups
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
satisfying (
\beta
). A group
\Lambda
satisfying (
\beta
) is not unique in general and so two extensions
\beth
satisfying (
\alpha
) need not be isomorphic; see Example
4.29
below.

•

Let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
be subgroups of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
satisfying (
\beta
) and let
\beth_{i}=p_{\Lambda_{i}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda_{i}^{\perp})
for
i=1,2
. As mentioned above,
\beth_{1},\beth_{2}
need not be isomorphic in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. As a matter of fact, it is possible that the groups
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}
,
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}
are not isomorphic in
\mathsf{CAGp}
; see Example
4.30
below.

Proof of Lemma
4.25
.

Before turning to the proof oberve that by Lemma
4.17
(2), there is indeed a unique subgroup
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
such that
\beth
is isomorphic to
p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Also, since
\beth
is minimal,
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
by virtue of Lemma
4.20
.

We show that (
\beta
) follows from (
\alpha
). So assume that (
\alpha
) holds and fix a subgroup
\Xi
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\Xi\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
and
\Lambda\subseteq\Xi
. Then the extension
\beth^{\prime}=p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
is minimal by Lemma
4.20
. Furthermore, Lemma
4.18
(ii) yields
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\beth^{\prime},\beth)\neq\emptyset
. Consequently,
\beth^{\prime}
and
\beth
are isomorphic in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
by virtue of (
\alpha
), and Lemma
4.18
(ii) thus yields
\Lambda=\Xi
. This verifies (
\beta
).

We show that (
\alpha
) follows from (
\beta
). So assume that (
\beta
) holds and fix a minimal extension
\beth^{\prime}
from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\beth^{\prime},\beth)\neq\emptyset
. By Lemma
4.17
(2),
\beth^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
for an appropriate subgroup
\Xi
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Moreover, Lemmas
4.20
and
4.18
yield
\Xi\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
and
\Lambda\subseteq\Xi
. Thus, by applying (
\beta
), we obtain
\Xi=\Lambda
. Consequently, we have isomorphisms of extensions
\beth^{\prime}\cong p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\beth
. This verifies (
\alpha
).
∎

Theorem 4.27
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
\mathcal{C}
be a minimal extension from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then there is a minimal extension
\beth
in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with the following properties:

(a)

there is an epimorphism
\beth\to\mathcal{C}
,

(b)

every minimal extension
\beth^{\prime}
from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\beth^{\prime},\beth)\neq\emptyset
is isomorphic to
\beth
.

Remark 4.28
.

In view of our discussion from Remark
4.26
we may reformulate the statement of the theorem by saying that in the category of the minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, every object
\mathcal{C}
is an epimorphic image of a maximal object
\beth
.

Proof of Theorem
4.27
.

By Lemma
4.17
(2),
\mathcal{C}
is isomorphic in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
to
p_{\Xi}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
for an appropriate subgroup
\Xi
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Moreover, Lemma
4.20
yields
\Xi\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
. By an elementary argument involving Zorn’s lemma one finds a subgroup
\Lambda\supseteq\Xi
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, which is maximal (with respect to inclusion) among the subgroups of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
intersecting
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at the identity. Now set
\beth=p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
\beth
is a minimal extension of
\mathcal{F}
due to Lemma
4.20
. Further, since
\Xi\subseteq\Lambda
, condition (a) holds by virtue of Lemma
4.18
. Finally, condition (b) follows from Lemma
4.25
by our choice of
\Lambda
.
∎

Example 4.29
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
X
non-degenerate locally compact and assume that
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}\neq 1
. Let
\mathfrak{A}
be the family of all subgroups
\Lambda
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, which are maximal with respect to the condition
\Lambda\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
. By the second part of Remark
4.26
,
\mathfrak{A}
can be identified with the set of all isomorphism classes of the maximal objects in the category of the minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. To demonstrate that there are many such isomorphism classes in general, we show that
\mathop{\rm card}(\mathfrak{A})\geq\mathfrak{c}
under our current assumptions on
\mathcal{F}
.

Fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1
. For
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
let
\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}}
be an element of
\mathfrak{A}
with
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}}
. (Such a group
\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}}
of course exists by Zorn’s lemma, since
\langle\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}\rangle\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
.) We claim that distinct coboundaries
\mathcal{D}
correspond to distinct groups
\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}}
. To see this, fix
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and assume that
\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}=\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}
. Then

\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\ni\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{-1}=(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}_{1})(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}_{2})^{-1}\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}^{-1}=\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}.

Since
\Lambda_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=1
, it follows that
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}_{2}
. Consequently,

\mathop{\rm card}(\mathfrak{A})\geq\mathop{\rm card}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm card}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1}))\geq\mathfrak{c},

as was to be shown.

Example 4.30
.

In the previous example we have seen that two extensions
\beth
satisfying condition (
\alpha
) from Lemma
4.25
need not be isomorphic in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. In this example we show that it is in fact possible for their sections
F(\beth)=G_{\beth}
to be non-isomorphic in
\mathsf{CAGp}
. To this end, we shall use a result from Chapter
6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}\curvearrowright\mathbb{T}^{2}
be defined as the product action of a minimal continuous flow on
\mathbb{T}^{1}
with the natural action of
\mathbb{T}^{1}
on itself. Clearly,
\mathcal{F}
thus defined is a minimal flow. Choose
z=(1,1)
as the base point for
\mathbb{T}^{2}
and consider the corresponding motion map
\mathcal{F}_{z}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{2}
. The induced morphism
\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}\colon H_{1}^{w}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{1})\to H_{1}^{w}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
is a monomorphism and it takes the form of an inclusion of groups
0\oplus\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
. It follows that the flow
\mathcal{F}
is topologically free and possesses a free cycle. By virtue of (
6.9
) from Theorem
6.14
, the inclusion
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
takes the form of an inclusion of abelian groups

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}.\end{array}

(4.4)

By writing the inclusion
0\subseteq\mathbb{R}
in the form
0\oplus 0\subseteq\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{Q}
and by reorganizing the direct summands from the isomorphism above, we put (
4.4
) into the following form:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}&\oplus&(0\oplus\mathbb{Z})&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}&\oplus&(\mathbb{Q}\oplus\mathbb{Z})&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}.\end{array}

Set
\Lambda_{1}=\mathbb{R}\oplus 0\oplus 0\oplus A_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}=\mathbb{R}\oplus 0\oplus 0\oplus A_{2}
, where
A_{1}=\mathbb{Q}\oplus 0\cong\mathbb{Q}
and
A_{2}=\{(k,k):k\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cong\mathbb{Z}
. For
i=1,2
set
\beth_{i}=p_{\Lambda_{i}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Elementary arguments show that both
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
are maximal among the subgroups of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
intersecting
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at the identity, and hence both
\beth_{1}
and
\beth_{2}
are minimal and satisfy condition (
\alpha
) from Lemma
4.25
. We claim that the groups
G_{\beth_{1}}
and
G_{\beth_{2}}
are not isomorphic in
\mathsf{CAGp}
. Indeed, there are topological isomorphisms

G_{\beth_{1}}=F(\beth_{1})=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda_{1}^{\perp}\cong\Lambda_{1}^{*}\cong b\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Q}^{*}

and

G_{\beth_{2}}=F(\beth_{2})=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda_{2}^{\perp}\cong\Lambda_{2}^{*}\cong b\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{1}.

These two groups are not topologically isomorphic, for the first one is torsion-free, while the other one is not.

Remark 4.31
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
torsion-free. As has been shown in the preceding example, two distinct extensions
\beth
satisfying condition (
\alpha
) from Lemma
4.25
may have non-isomorphic sections
F(\beth)=G_{\beth}
. We would like to show now that, after all, there does exist an invariant for such extensions
\beth
, namely the topological dimension of the section
\dim(G_{\beth})
. So fix
\beth
satisfying (
\alpha
) from Lemma
4.25
and let
\Lambda
be the subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\beth\cong p_{\Lambda}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
in
\mathop{\rm\mathsf{CAGpZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. By Lemma
4.25
,
\Lambda
is maximal among the subgroups of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
intersecting
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at the identity. Consequently, the divisible hull
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
takes the form of a direct sum of abelian groups (and hence also of rational linear spaces)
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\Lambda\right)
. Thus,

\begin{split}\dim\left(G_{\beth}\right)&=\dim\left(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}/\Lambda^{\perp}\right)=\dim\left(\Lambda^{*}\right)=\mathop{\rm rank}(\Lambda)=\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\Lambda)\\
&=\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}\left((\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})/(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\right)\end{split}

does not depend on the choice of
\beth
.

4.5
Cohomology classes as group morphisms

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. In Theorem
3.1
from Section
3.1
we have seen that for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
can be viewed as the group of morphisms
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. For cohomology groups such an isomorphism fails to hold. Though the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
can be viewed as a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, the extent to which these two groups differ is represented by the group of extensions
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))
; this is proved in Theorems
4.32
and
4.36
. In fact, as we show in Theorem
4.39
, the obstruction to an isomorphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
lies in the existence of extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathcal{F}
with totally disconnected sections
F(\mathcal{C})
. This also suggests that there might be a connection between the existence of such extensions
\mathcal{C}
on one side and the first cohomotopy group
\pi^{1}(X)
of
X
on the other side. This is indeed the case, as we shall demonstrate later in Chapter
5
.

Theorem 4.32
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow,
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
\pi
be the canonical quotient morphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then the map

\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\ni\mathcal{C}\mapsto\pi\mathcal{C}^{*}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})

(4.5)

is a topological isomorphism onto its image, provided the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
are equipped with the ext-topology and the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
carries the topology of point-wise convergence.

Remark 4.33
.

We wish to add the following remarks.

•

In order to simplify notation, we are identifying an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with its cohomology class in the definition of
\Psi_{G}
. We shall do so also in the sequel in situations when no misunderstanding can arise.

•

We shall see in the proof of the theorem that the morphism
\Psi_{G}
can also be defined formally as a unique morphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
with
\Psi_{G}\pi_{G}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\Phi_{G}
.

•

The theorem provides us with the following inclusions of topological groups for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
:

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{G^{*}}.

Since
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a discrete group by Remark
3.35
, it follows that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is always totally disconnected.

•

The image
