\mathop{\rm im}(\Psi_{G})
of
\Psi_{G}
may or may not be closed in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, depending on a particular situation. See, for instance, Theorem
6.28
in Chapter
6
.

Proof of Theorem
4.32
.

Consider the isomorphism
\Phi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
defined in Theorem
3.1
and the morphism

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})

derived from
\pi
. We claim that

\ker(\pi_{G})=\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\ker(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\Phi_{G}).

Indeed, the first equality is an immediate consequence of the definition of
\pi_{G}
, whereas the second equality follows from the equivalence of the following statements for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
:

•

\mathcal{C}\in\ker(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\Phi_{G})
,

•

\pi\mathcal{C}^{*}=1
,

•

\chi\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for every
\chi\in G^{*}
,

•

\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

(For the equivalence of the third and the fourth statement, see Corollary
3.8
.) It follows that there exists a unique morphism of groups

\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})

with
\Psi_{G}\pi_{G}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi_{G})\Phi_{G}
and that
\Psi_{G}
is in fact a monomorphism. One verifies at once that
\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})=\pi\mathcal{C}^{*}
for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Now we show that
\Psi_{G}
is a topological isomorphism onto its image. First we verify the continuity of
\Psi_{G}
. So assume that a convergent net
\mathcal{C}_{i}\to e
is given in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Then
F(\mathcal{C}_{i})\to e
in
2^{G}
and hence
F(\mathcal{C}^{*}_{i}(\chi))=F(\chi\mathcal{C}_{i})=\chi F(\mathcal{C}_{i})\to 1
in
2^{\mathbb{T}^{1}}
for every
\chi\in G^{*}
. This yields convergence
\pi\mathcal{C}^{*}_{i}\to e
in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and verifies the continuity of
\Psi_{G}
.

Conversely, assume that for a given net
(\mathcal{C}_{i})_{i\in J}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, we have convergence
\pi\mathcal{C}^{*}_{i}\to e
in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Fix a neighbourhood
V
of
e
in
G
. There exist
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\chi_{1},\dots,\chi_{n}\in G^{*}
and neighbourhoods
W_{1}\dots,W_{n}
of
1
in
\mathbb{T}^{1}
such that
V\supseteq\bigcap_{l=1}^{n}\chi_{l}^{-1}(W_{l})
. From the convergence
\pi\mathcal{C}^{*}_{i}\to e
in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
it follows that there is
i_{0}\in J
with
\chi_{l}F(\mathcal{C}_{i})=F(\chi_{l}\mathcal{C}_{i})=F(\mathcal{C}^{*}_{i}(\chi_{l}))\subseteq W_{l}
for all
i\geq i_{0}
and
l=1,\dots,n
. Consequently,
F(\mathcal{C}_{i})\subseteq\bigcap_{l=1}^{n}\chi_{l}^{-1}(W_{l})\subseteq V
for every
i\geq i_{0}
. This shows that
F(\mathcal{C}_{i})\to e
in
2^{G}
and hence
\mathcal{C}_{i}\to e
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.
∎

Corollary 4.34
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, let
h_{\mathcal{C}}
be the morphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to G
with
h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathcal{C}
. Then the map

\widetilde{\Psi}_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\ni\mathcal{C}\mapsto\left(\omega_{G}\right)^{-1}\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}(\pi^{*})^{-1}=h_{\mathcal{C}}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}),G)

is a topological isomorphism onto its image, provided
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is equipped with the ext-topology and
\mathop{\rm Hom}(F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}),G)
carries the topology of uniform convergence.

Remark 4.35
.

Similarly to Theorem
4.32
, in our definition of
\widetilde{\Psi}_{G}
we are identifying every extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with its cohomology class. This time, the abuse of notation is excused by the fact that for equivalent extensions
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the corresponding morphisms
h_{\mathcal{C}}
and
h_{\mathcal{D}}
agree on
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
. (Indeed, if
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
then
h_{\mathcal{C}}(h_{\mathcal{D}})^{-1}=h_{\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)}
and
h_{\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=F(h_{\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=F(\mathop{\rm co}(\xi))=e
.) We shall use this identification also in the proof of Corollary
4.34
below.

Proof of Corollary
4.34
.

Since both
G^{*}
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
carry the discrete topology, there is a topological isomorphism
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Hom}((\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*},G^{**})
, which associates with every morphism
k\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
its Pontryagin dual
k^{*}\colon G^{**}\to(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
. Moreover, the Pontryagin isomorphism
\omega_{G}
corresponding to
G
is a topological isomorphism
G\to G^{**}
and the dual morphism
\pi^{*}
of
\pi
is a topological isomorphism
(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}\to(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}
. Finally, since
(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}=F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
by the first part of Remark
4.8
, we have a topological isomorphism
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}),G)
, which associates with every morphism
k\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
the morphism
(\omega_{G})^{-1}k^{*}(\pi^{*})^{-1}\colon F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\to G
. Thus, since
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is a topological isomorphism onto its image by virtue of Theorem
4.32
, it follows that
\widetilde{\Psi}_{G}
is also a topological isomorphism onto its image. To finish the proof, we need to show that
(\omega_{G})^{-1}\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}(\pi^{*})^{-1}=h_{\mathcal{C}}
for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Given
\chi\in G^{*}
, consider the set

\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\mathcal{C}}=\left\{\Lambda\in\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}=(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}:\Lambda(\chi\mathcal{C})=(\chi h_{\mathcal{C}})(\Lambda)\right\}.

Clearly,
\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\mathcal{C}}
is a closed subgroup of
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Further,
\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\mathcal{C}}
contains all the values of
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
; indeed, for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
x\in X
,

\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)(\chi\mathcal{C})=(\chi\mathcal{C})(\gamma,x)=\chi(\mathcal{C}(\gamma,x))=\chi(h_{\mathcal{C}}\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x))=(\chi h_{\mathcal{C}})(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}(\gamma,x)).

These two observations lead to the inclusion
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\mathcal{C}}
.

We verify the desired identity
(\omega_{G})^{-1}\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}(\pi^{*})^{-1}=h_{\mathcal{C}}
. We accomplish this by showing that
\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}=\omega_{G}h_{\mathcal{C}}\pi^{*}
. For all
\Upsilon\in(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
and
\chi\in G^{*}
we have

\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}(\Upsilon)=\Upsilon\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})\colon\chi\mapsto\Upsilon\left(\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})(\chi)\right)=\Upsilon(\pi(\chi\mathcal{C}))=(\Upsilon\pi)(\chi\mathcal{C}),

(4.6)

and

\left(\omega_{G}h_{\mathcal{C}}\pi^{*}\right)(\Upsilon)=\omega_{G}\left(h_{\mathcal{C}}(\Upsilon\pi)\right)\colon\chi\mapsto\chi\left(h_{\mathcal{C}}(\Upsilon\pi)\right)=(\chi h_{\mathcal{C}})(\Upsilon\pi).

(4.7)

Since
\Upsilon\pi=\pi^{*}(\Upsilon)\in\mathop{\rm im}(\pi^{*})=F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\chi,\mathcal{C}}
for all
\Upsilon
and
\chi
by our discussion above, the right-hand sides of (
4.6
) and (
4.7
) are equal. This verifies the identity
\Psi_{G}(\mathcal{C})^{*}=\omega_{G}h_{\mathcal{C}}\pi^{*}
.
∎

Theorem 4.36
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow on a compact connected space
X
and let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. Then there exist

•

a morphism
\Omega\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))
, and

•

a monomorphism
\nu\colon\pi^{1}(X)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

such that the following sequence is exact:

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{G}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa_{G}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega}}{{\longrightarrow}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{e}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{e}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

(4.8)

where
\kappa_{G}=\Psi_{G}\pi_{G}
,
\nu_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\nu)
and
\pi_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi)
. If, in addition, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible then the following sequence is exact:

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{G}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa_{G}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(4.9)

Finally, if
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and
G
is connected then, under the identification
\mathop{\rm im}(\Psi_{G})\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the latter group is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
with the complementary summand isomorphic to
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))
. Thus, there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right).

(4.10)

Remark 4.37
.

It follows from the exactness of (
4.8
) and from the definition of
\kappa_{G}
that
\Psi_{G}
is an isomorphism, provided
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))=0
. This is the case in the following situations:

•

the group
\pi^{1}(X)
is divisible,

•

the group
G^{*}
is free abelian, that is,
G
is a torus,

•

\pi^{1}(X)
is finitely generated (in particular,
X
is a manifold) and
G^{*}
is a Whitehead group (that is,
G
is arc-wise connected).

Proof of Theorem
4.36
.

Consider the covariant Hom-Ext sequence derived from the sequence (
2.27
) and corresponding to the group
G^{*}
:

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{h}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{h}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{e}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{e}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(4.11)

Recall that
\mu_{h}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mu)
,
\pi_{h}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)
,
\mu_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mu)
,
\pi_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi)
and
\epsilon
is the connecting morphism. Our aim now is to show that the sequence (
4.11
) is equivalent to the sequence (
4.8
). We shall proceed by replacing all the arrows in (
4.11
) step by step. The result of this construction will be summarized in the diagrams in Figures
4.11
and
4.12
below.

By virtue of Corollary
3.8
, the isomorphism
\Phi_{G}
from Theorem
3.1
restricts to an isomorphism
\Phi_{G}^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Moreover,
\Phi_{G}\mu_{G}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mu)\Phi_{G}^{\prime}=\mu_{h}\Phi_{G}^{\prime}
. It follows that the arrow

\mu_{h}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mu)\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})

in (
4.11
) can be replaced by the arrow
\mu_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Further, the arrow

\pi_{h}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})

in (
4.11
) can be replaced by the arrow
\pi_{h}\Phi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, this is clear. Since
\pi_{h}\Phi_{G}=\Psi_{G}\pi_{G}=\kappa_{G}
by definition of
\Psi_{G}
and
\kappa_{G}
, this yields the third arrow in (
4.8
).

Fix a base point
z
for
X
. There are isomorphisms of groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)
; denote by
j
the underlying inclusion morphism
\pi^{1}(X)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. We claim that
j
induces an isomorphism of groups

j_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},j)\colon\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))\to\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}).

To see this, let
\mathop{\rm pr}_{1}
,
\mathop{\rm pr}_{2}
be the projection morphisms from
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
onto
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
,
\pi^{1}(X)
, respectively, and write
q_{1}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm pr}_{1})
,
q_{2}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm pr}_{2})
. Since
\mathop{\rm pr}_{2}j=\mathop{\rm id}_{\pi^{1}(X)}
, it follows that
q_{2}j_{e}=\mathop{\rm id}_{\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))}
and hence
j_{e}
is a monomorphism. Further, from the exactness of the sequence

0\longrightarrow\pi^{1}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathop{\rm pr}_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow 0

we obtain an exact sequence

\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{e}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))\longrightarrow 0.

(4.12)

By divisibility of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
, we have
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))=0
and so
j_{e}
is an epimorphism due to the exactness of (
4.12
). Thus, indeed,
j_{e}
is an isomorphism of groups. Consequently, the arrow
\epsilon\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in (
4.11
) can be replaced by the arrow
\Omega\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))
, where
\Omega=j_{e}^{-1}\epsilon
.

Finally, set
\nu=\mu j
and
\nu_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\nu)
. Then
\nu_{e}=\mu_{e}j_{e}
and the arrow

\mu_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mu)\colon\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})

in (
4.11
) thus turns into

\nu_{e}=\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\nu)\colon\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))\to\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}),

which is precisely the fifth arrow in (
4.8
). The observations made so far can be summarized in the form of the commutative diagrams from Figures
4.11
and
4.12
.

\begin{CD}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\mu_{h}}>{}>\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\pi_{h}}>{}>\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\\
@A{\Phi_{G}^{\prime}}A{}A@A{\Phi_{G}}A{}A\Big\|\\
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)@>{}>{\mu_{G}}>\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)@>{}>{\kappa_{G}}>\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\end{CD}

Figure 4.11:
Obtaining (
4.8
) from (
4.11
), part 1.

\begin{CD}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\epsilon}>{}>\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\mu_{e}}>{}>\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\pi_{e}}>{}>\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\\
\Big\|@A{j_{e}}A{}A\Big\|\Big\|\\
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{}>{\Omega}>\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))@>{}>{\nu_{e}}>\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{}>{\pi_{e}}>\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\end{CD}

Figure 4.12:
Obtaining (
4.8
) from (
4.11
), part 2.

Since all the vertical arrows in Figures
4.11
and
4.12
are isomorphisms, the exactness of (
4.8
) follows from the exactness of (
4.11
). The exactness of (
4.9
) follows at once from the exactness of (
4.8
), for
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
as soon as the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible.

We finish the proof of the theorem by verifying (
4.10
). First, since
\pi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is an epimorphism and
\kappa_{G}=\Psi_{G}\pi_{G}
by definition, we have
\mathop{\rm im}(\Psi_{G})=\mathop{\rm im}(\kappa_{G})
. Further, by the exactness of (
4.9
),
\mathop{\rm im}(\kappa_{G})=\ker(\Omega)
and

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\ker(\Omega)\cong\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)).

Consequently,

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\mathop{\rm im}(\Psi_{G})\\
&=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\mathop{\rm im}(\kappa_{G})\\
&=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\ker(\Omega)\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right).\end{split}

(4.13)

By virtue of Theorem
4.12
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible due to divisibility of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and connectedness of
G
, and hence its quotient group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is also divisible. It follows that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and, by virtue of (
4.13
), its complementary summand is isomorphic to
\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)
. This verifies (
4.10
).
∎

In order to formulate our next result we fix some notation. Given a minimal flow
\mathcal{F}
and a group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, we use the symbol
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
to denote the set of all extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with a totally disconnected section
F(\mathcal{C})\subseteq G
. Also, we write
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
instead of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
.

Lemma 4.38
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Then for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Moreover, if
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
is connected then
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is also divisible and hence it is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1st step.
Let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
. We recall the equivalence of the following statements:

(
\alpha
)

H
is totally disconnected,

(
\beta
)

\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{k}(H)=e
,

(
\gamma
)

for every identity neighbourhood
U
in
G
there is
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\kappa_{l}(H)\subseteq U
for every
l\in\mathbb{N}
divisible by
k
.

First, (
\alpha
) follows from (
\beta
), for if
H_{0}\neq e
then, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\kappa_{k}(H)\supseteq\kappa_{k}(H_{0})=H_{0}\neq e
. To show that (
\beta
) follows from (
\alpha
), assume that
H
is totally disconnected. Then
H
is isomorphic to a subgroup of the product of a family of finite abelian groups. Since every finite abelian group is annihilated by
\kappa_{k}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we conclude that for every identity neighbourhood
V
in
H
there is
k
with
\kappa_{k}(H)\subseteq V
. This verifies (
\beta
). The equivalence of (
\beta
) and (
\gamma
) follows from the fact that
\kappa_{l}(H)\subseteq\kappa_{k}(H)
as soon as
k
divides
l
.

2nd step.
We show that if
H,K
are closed totally disconnected subgroups of
G
and
h\in\mathbb{N}
, then both
HK
and
\kappa_{h}^{-1}(H)
are (closed and) totally disconnected. (Here,
\kappa_{h}
is the
h
-endomorphism of the whole group
G
.)

Fix an identity neighbourhood
U
in
G
. Further, choose an identity neighbourhood
V
in
G
with
VV\subseteq U
. By virtue of the equivalence (
\alpha
)
\Leftrightarrow
(
\gamma
) from the first step of the proof, there are
k,l\in\mathbb{N}
with
\kappa_{k}(H)\subseteq V
and
\kappa_{l}(K)\subseteq V
. Then

\kappa_{kl}(HK)=\kappa_{kl}(H)\kappa_{kl}(K)\subseteq\kappa_{k}(H)\kappa_{l}(K)\subseteq VV\subseteq U.

Since
U
was an arbitrary identity neighbourhood in
G
, it follows by the first step of the proof that
HK
is indeed totally disconnected. Finally, the group
\kappa_{h}^{-1}(H)
is totally disconnected by virtue of the equivalence (
\alpha
)
\Leftrightarrow
(
\beta
), for

\begin{split}\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{k}\left(\kappa_{h}^{-1}(H)\right)&\subseteq\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{kh}\left(\kappa_{h}^{-1}(H)\right)=\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{k}\left(\kappa_{h}\left(\kappa_{h}^{-1}(H)\right)\right)\\
&\subseteq\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\kappa_{k}(H)=e.\end{split}

3rd step.
Let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. We show that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Fix
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Then
F(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D})\subseteq F(\mathcal{C})F(\mathcal{D})
by Lemma
3.31
(c). Since
F(\mathcal{C})F(\mathcal{D})
is a totally disconnected subgroup of
G
by the second step of the proof, it follows that the group
F(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D})
is also totally disconnected and hence
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Also,
F(\mathcal{C}^{-1})=F(\mathcal{C})
by Lemma
3.31
(b) and so
\mathcal{C}^{-1}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. This shows that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is indeed a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

4th step.
Assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected. We show that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible.

Fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
h\in\mathbb{N}
. By the assumptions of the theorem and by virtue of Theorem
4.12
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible. Consequently, there is
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with
\mathcal{D}^{h}=\mathcal{C}
; we show that
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. We have
\kappa_{h}(F(\mathcal{D}))=F(\kappa_{h}\mathcal{D})=F(\mathcal{D}^{h})=F(\mathcal{C})
and so
F(\mathcal{D})\subseteq\kappa_{h}^{-1}(F(\mathcal{C}))
. By the second step of the proof,
\kappa_{h}^{-1}(F(\mathcal{C}))
is totally disconnected and hence so is
F(\mathcal{D})
. Thus,
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, as was to be shown.
∎

Theorem 4.39
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow on a compact connected space
X
. Assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and fix a complementary direct summand
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
to
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Given
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected, set

•

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\pi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,

•

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\Phi_{G}^{-1}\left(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})\right)
,

•

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\pi_{G}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\right)
,

•

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
.

Then the following statements hold:

(1)

F(\mathcal{C})\subseteq G
is connected for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,

(2)

there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G),

(3)

there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G),

which is topological, provided
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
carries the ext-topology,

(4)

the restriction
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is an isomorphism of groups,

(5)

under the identification
\Psi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the latter group is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and its complementary summand is isomorphic to the group
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))
; consequently, there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)).

(4.14)

Remark 4.40
.

Notice the following facts.

•

Assume that
G
has no proper non-trivial closed connected subgroups. Then, since
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
by definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, it follows from statement (1) that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a subgroup of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Recall also that for a non-trivial connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
the following conditions are equivalent:

\circ

G
has no proper non-trivial closed connected subgroups,

\circ

\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})=1
.

These conditions are satisfied, for instance, by the circle group
G=\mathbb{T}^{1}
and by the solenoids.

•

By virtue of (3) applied to the group
\mathbb{T}^{1}
, there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}).

Consequently, by virtue of (4) and (5), it is the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
that are “responsible” for the non-surjectivity of the monomorphism
\Psi_{G}
. That is,
\Psi_{G}
is an isomorphism if and only if its restriction
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is an isomorphism.

•

The splitting in statement (2), unlike the one in statement (3), need not be topological if
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
carries the topology of u.c.s. convergence. For examples of such a situation we refer to Proposition
5.45
and Remark
5.46
in Chapter
5
.

Proof of Theorem
4.39
.

Before turning to the proof of the theorem notice the following facts. First, by virtue of Lemma
4.38
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is indeed a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Second, though unique only up to an isomorphism,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is always a subgroup of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Now we turn to the proof of the theorem, proceeding in five steps.

1st step.
We verify statement (1).

Fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. By definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,
\chi\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{*}(\chi)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for every
\chi\in G^{*}
. It follows that for every
\chi\in G^{*}
,
\chi F(\mathcal{C})=F(\chi\mathcal{C})
is either
1
or
\mathbb{T}^{1}
. Consequently,
F(\mathcal{C})
is a connected subgroup of
G
and statement (1) thus follows.

2nd step.
We verify statement (2).

By definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}).

(4.15)

Under the isomorphism
\Phi_{G}
from Theorem
3.1
, the following correspondences hold:

(i)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
corresponds to
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; indeed, given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the following conditions are equivalent:

•

\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,

•

F(\mathcal{C})\subseteq G
is totally disconnected,

•

F(\chi\mathcal{C})=\chi F(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{1}
is totally disconnected for every
\chi\in G^{*}
,

•

\mathcal{C}^{*}(\chi)=\chi\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for every
\chi\in G^{*}
,

•

\Phi_{G}(\mathcal{C})=\mathcal{C}^{*}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

(ii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
corresponds to
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; this holds by definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Thus, statement (2) follows from (
4.15
), (i) and (ii).

3rd step.
We verify statement (3).

First we check that the direct sum in (3) holds algebraically. The fact that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a sum of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
follows by applying
\pi_{G}
to the direct sum in (2). We need only to show that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=e
, but this is immediate. For if
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
F(\mathcal{C})
is both connected and totally disconnected then
F(\mathcal{C})=e
and hence
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Now we show that the splitting in (3) is topological. To this end, fix a directed set
J
and nets
(\mathcal{C}_{i})_{i\in J}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
(\mathcal{D}_{i})_{i\in J}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. We need to show that
F(\mathcal{C}_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i})\to e
in
2^{G}
if and only if
F(\mathcal{C}_{i}),F(\mathcal{D}_{i})\to e
in
2^{G}
. Since all
F(\mathcal{C}_{i})
(
i\in J
) are totally disconnected and all
F(\mathcal{D}_{i})
(
i\in J
) are connected, Corollary
3.20
yields
F(\mathcal{C}_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i})=F(\mathcal{C}_{i})F(\mathcal{D}_{i})
for every
i\in J
. From this identity the desired equivalence follows at once.

4th step.
We verify statement (4).

Notice the following facts:

•

the restriction
\Phi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is an isomorphism; this follows from the definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and from the fact that
\Phi_{G}
is an isomorphism,

•

the restriction
\pi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is an isomorphism; this follows from the definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and from the identity
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=e
(the latter holds by virtue of (2)),

•

the restriction
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is an isomorphism; this follows from the fact that (by the preceding statement applied to the group
\mathbb{T}^{1}
) the restriction
\pi\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is an isomorphism,

•

the morphism
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is defined by the identity
\Psi_{G}\pi_{G}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi)\Phi_{G}
; this follows from Remark
4.33
.

The four facts listed above now yield statement (4).

5th step.
We verify statement (5).

First notice that
\Psi_{G}
maps
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
into
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, since
\Phi_{G}
maps
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
into
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
by virtue of (i) from the second step of the proof. We shall need the following facts:

•

under the isomorphism
\Psi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the latter group is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and its complementary summand is isomorphic to the group
\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)
; this follows from (
4.10
) in Theorem
4.36
,

•

under the isomorphism
\Psi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the latter group is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; indeed, by virtue of Lemma
4.38
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a divisible group and hence so is its quotient group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,

•

there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G);

this follows from statement (3),

•

there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}});

this follows from statement (3) applied to the group
\mathbb{T}^{1}
,

•

\Psi_{G}
maps
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
isomorphically onto
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; this follows from statement (4).

These five facts lead, on one hand, to a diagram in Figure
4.13
and, on the other hand, to the isomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)&\cong\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)}{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}\\
&\cong\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})}{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}\oplus\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)}{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}\\
&\cong\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})}{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}.\end{split}

\begin{CD}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}){}={}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}){}\oplus{}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})\\
@A{\Psi_{G}}A{\text{mon}}A@A{\Psi_{G}}A{\text{mon}}A@A{\Psi_{G}}A{\text{iso}}A\\
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G).\end{CD}

Figure 4.13:
Monomorphic and isomorphic restrictions of
\Psi_{G}
.

This verifies statement (5).
∎

4.6
Torsions as non-minimal extensions

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. In this section we study the torsion subgroup
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and its subgroups
\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
). We are motivated by the fact that the torsion elements of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
correspond to the non-minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, see Corollary
3.9
. We begin our investigation with the situation when
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a divisible group, which includes the case when the acting group
\Gamma
of
\mathcal{F}
is simply connected; see Theorem
4.41
. Then we embark on the case when
\Gamma
is a connected Lie group and
X
is a compact (connected) manifold; see Theorem
4.44
.

Theorem 4.41
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow on a compact connected space
X
and assume that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there are short exact sequences

0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{k})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\pi^{1}(X)/k\pi^{1}(X)\longrightarrow 0

(4.16)

and

0\longrightarrow\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{l})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\longrightarrow 0.

(4.17)

Moreover, if
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups then there are isomorphisms

\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\pi^{1}(X)/k\pi^{1}(X)

(4.18)

and

\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X),

(4.19)

and, finally, there exists a divisible subgroup
\mathfrak{D}
of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinH}_{\mathcal{F}}}
such that

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathfrak{D}\oplus\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathfrak{D}\oplus\Big((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\Big).

(4.20)

Remark 4.42
.

Notice the following facts.

•

The union
\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{l})
from (
4.17
) is to be interpreted as a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{d})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,(\mathbb{T}^{1})_{d})
, under the usual identifications
\mathbb{Z}_{l}\cong\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\subseteq\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{1}
. An elementary argument shows that if the quotient group
\Gamma/\Gamma_{0}
is finitely generated then this union coincides with
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{d})
.

•

An extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
corresponds to an element of
\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
if and only if
\mathcal{C}^{k}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. This is equivalent to the inclusion
F(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{k}
.

Proof of Theorem
4.41
.

Consider the exact sequence for tensor and torsion products derived from (
2.27
) and corresponding to the group
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
:

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\\
&\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(4.21)

Then (
4.21
) yields (
4.16
) with the help of the following observations:

•

\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
; this follows from the fact that for an arbitrary
z\in X
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
is a torsion-free group by connectedness of
X
,

•

\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{k})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{k})
; the first isomorphism and the equality are clear, the second isomorphism follows from the fact that
X
is connected and
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
is totally disconnected,

•

\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Z}_{k},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; this is clear,

•

\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\pi^{1}(X)/k\pi^{1}(X)
; this follows from the isomorphisms
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)
and from the divisibility of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
:

\begin{split}\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\big(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)\big)\\
&\cong\big(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\big)\oplus\big(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)\\
&\cong\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\cong\pi^{1}(X)/k\pi^{1}(X),\end{split}

•

\mathbb{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=0
; this follows from the fact that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible by the assumptions of the theorem.

Now consider the exact sequence for tensor and torsion products derived from (
2.27
) and corresponding to the group
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
:

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\\
&\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(4.22)

Then, similarly to the preceding step of the proof, (
4.22
) yields (
4.17
) with the help of the following observations:

•

\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
; this follows from the fact that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free by connectedness of
X
,

•

\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{l})
; this follows from the following isomorphisms by connectedness of
X
:

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\text{tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{l})\\
&\cong\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{N}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{l}),\end{split}

•

\mathop{\rm Tor}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; this is clear,

•

(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
; this follows from the divisibility of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
:

\begin{split}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\\
&\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\big(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)\big)\\
&\cong\big((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\big)\oplus\big((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)\\
&\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X),\end{split}

•

(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=0
; this follows from the fact that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a divisible group by the assumptions of the theorem and
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
is a torsion group.

Now assume that
\Gamma
possesses no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups. Then
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{l})=0
for every
l\in\mathbb{N}
and so the isomorphisms (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) follow from the exactness of the sequences (
4.16
) and (
4.17
), respectively. To finish the proof, we need to verify (
4.20
). The second isomorphism from (
4.20
) is a consequence of (
4.19
). The first isomorphism from (
4.20
) follows from the following facts.

•

The torsion subgroup
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. Indeed, by the assumptions of the theorem,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a divisible group and hence so is its quotient group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Thus,
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is divisible, being the torsion subgroup of a divisible group.

•

The group
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is contained as a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
by divisibility.

•

Every complementary summand
\mathfrak{D}
of
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. This follows from the divisibility of both
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

•

Given
\mathfrak{D}
as above, it is always a subgroup of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinH}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Indeed, we have
\mathfrak{D}\setminus 1\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm{\bf MinH}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1
.

This verifies (
4.20
).
∎

Corollary 4.43
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow on a compact connected space
X
. Assume that
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups and that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. Then the identity component
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})_{0}
of
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
is a topological direct summand in
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
. In fact, under the notation from Theorem
4.41
, there are topological isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong&F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})_{0}&\times&F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})/F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})_{0}\\
&\cong&\mathfrak{D}^{*}&\times&\mathop{\rm tor}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{*}\\
&\cong&\mathfrak{D}^{*}&\times&\left((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{*},\end{array}

where all
\mathfrak{D}
,
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and
(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
are assumed to carry the discrete topology.

Proof.

By virtue of Remark
4.8
and by virtue of (
4.20
) from Theorem
4.41
, the following topological isomorphisms hold
for an appropriate divisible subgroup
\mathfrak{D}
of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinH}_{\mathcal{F}}}
:

\begin{split}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{\perp}\cong\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{*}&\cong(\mathfrak{D}\oplus\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}))^{*}\\
&\cong\mathfrak{D}^{*}\times\mathop{\rm tor}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{*}\\
&\cong\mathfrak{D}^{*}\times\left((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{*}.\end{split}

(4.23)

Since
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is a torsion group, its dual group
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
is totally disconnected. Further, since
\mathfrak{D}
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinH}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, it is torsion-free and its dual group
\mathfrak{D}^{*}
is therefore connected. Consequently, under the isomorphisms from (
4.23
), the group
\mathfrak{D}^{*}
corresponds to
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})_{0}
and the group
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}
corresponds to
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})/F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})_{0}
. This proves the corollary.
∎

Theorem 4.44
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact manifold. Set
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
,
n+m=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(X))
and denote by
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
the elementary divisors of
H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})
in
H_{1}^{w}(X)
. If
k\geq 2
is an integer and
\delta_{i}=\gcd(d_{i},k)
(
i=1,\dots,n
) then

\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{k}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\cong\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{k}\left(H_{1}^{w}(X)/H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\right)\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{n}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}

(4.24)

and

\mathop{\rm tor}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\cong\mathop{\rm tor}\left(H_{1}^{w}(X)/H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\right)\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\right)^{m}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\right)^{m}.

(4.25)

Remark 4.45
.

We wish to add the following remarks.

•

The isomorphisms (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) are consistent with the isomorphisms (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) from Theorem
4.41
, respectively. For if
\Gamma
is simply connected then
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a divisible group and we have
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))=0
,
m=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(X))
and
\pi^{1}(X)\cong H_{1}^{w}(X)
, which leads to isomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong\pi^{1}(X)/k\pi^{1}(X)\cong H_{1}^{w}(X)/kH_{1}^{w}(X)\\
&\cong\mathbb{Z}^{m}/k\mathbb{Z}^{m}\cong\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes H_{1}^{w}(X)\\
&\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathbb{Z}^{m}\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}.\end{split}

•

The isomorphism (
4.24
) can help us understand the structure of the set
\mathop{\rm per}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
of all cohomology classes of extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
F(\mathcal{C})=\mathbb{Z}_{k}
. Indeed, an element
(z_{1},\dots,z_{n},\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{m})
of
\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{n}}\oplus(\mathbb{Z}_{k})^{m}
corresponds to an element of
\mathop{\rm per}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
under the isomorphism (
4.24
) if and only if one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

there is
1\leq i\leq n
such that
k
divides
d_{i}
(that is,
\delta_{i}=k
) and
z_{i}
generates
\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{i}}=\mathbb{Z}_{k}
,

(ii)

there is
1\leq j\leq m
such that
\zeta_{j}
generates
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
.

Proof of Theorem
4.44
.

Throughout the whole proof, both
X
and
\Gamma
will be assumed pointed. The base point of
X
will be denoted by
z
and the base point of
\Gamma
will be its identity
1
. We also recall that for abelian groups
A,C
and their subgroups
B\mathrel{\lhd}A
,
D\mathrel{\lhd}C
,
\mathop{\rm Hom}(A,B;C,D)
stands for the group of all morphisms
h\colon A\to C
with
h(B)\subseteq D
. Now we turn to the proof, proceeding in three steps.

1st step.
Fix a map
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
and an integer
k\geq 2
. We show that the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
lifts across
\kappa_{k}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

(b)

\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}
, where
\xi^{\sharp}
is the induced morphism
H_{1}^{w}(X)\to H_{1}^{w}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
and
H_{1}^{w}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
is identified with
\mathbb{Z}
.

We claim that the following conditions are equivalent:

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
lifts across
\kappa_{k}
to an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
lifts across
\kappa_{k}
to a continuous base point preserving map
\Gamma\times X\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
,

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)\subseteq\kappa_{k}^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1})=k\mathbb{Z}
,

•

\xi^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma)\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}
,

•

\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}
.

Indeed, the equivalence of the first four of these conditions follows from Lemmas
3.36
and
2.17
. The equivalence of the last two conditions follows from the following simple computation based on the commutativity of the diagram in Lemma
2.17

p_{\mathbb{T}^{1}}\left(\xi^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma)\right)=\xi^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}p_{\Gamma}(\pi_{1}(\Gamma))=\xi^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)=\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})

and from the equality
p_{\mathbb{T}^{1}}=\mathop{\rm id}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, which holds under the standard identifications
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong H_{1}^{w}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z}
.

2nd step.
We verify (
4.24
).

Given a propositional form
\mathcal{P}
defined on the set
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
, we use the symbol
[\mathcal{P}(\xi)]
to denote the set of all
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
such that the proposition
\mathcal{P}(\xi)
holds true. We shall arrive at (
4.24
) through a series of isomorphisms. Each of these isomorphisms will be dealt with in a separate item.

(i)

By virtue of the equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(b) from the first step of the proof,

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&=\left\{\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}:\mathcal{C}^{k}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right\}/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\\
&=\left\{\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)):\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}\right\}/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}},\end{split}

(4.26)

where
\kappa_{k}
stands for the
k
-endomorphism of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Observe that
\kappa_{k}
is a monomorphism by connectedness of
\Gamma
and
X
. Consequently, by virtue of (
4.26
),

\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\left\{\mathop{\rm co}(\xi):\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}\right\}/\kappa_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}).

(4.27)

(ii)

Under the usual isomorphism
\sigma\colon C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\ni\xi\mapsto\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, the subgroup
\kappa_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
corresponds to the subgroup
\kappa_{k}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1}))
of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
. The latter subgroup consists of all those maps
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
, which lift across
\kappa_{k}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an element of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
; this occurs if and only if
\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, the isomorphism (
4.27
) yields

\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\frac{[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]}{[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]}\cong\frac{[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]/[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)=0]}{[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]/[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)=0]}.

(4.28)

(iii)

Consider the map
\varphi\colon C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\ni\xi\mapsto\xi^{\sharp}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(H_{1}^{w}(X),\mathbb{Z})
. By our assumptions on
X
,
\varphi
is an epimorphism and
\ker(\varphi)=[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)=0]
. Consequently, it follows from (
4.28
) by elementary group-theoretic arguments that

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(H_{1}^{w}(X),H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F});\mathbb{Z},k\mathbb{Z})}{\mathop{\rm Hom}(H_{1}^{w}(X),H_{1}^{w}(X);\mathbb{Z},k\mathbb{Z})}\\
&\cong\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m},d_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus d_{n}\mathbb{Z};\mathbb{Z},k\mathbb{Z})}{\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m},\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m};\mathbb{Z},k\mathbb{Z})},\end{split}

(4.29)

where
d_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus d_{n}\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus 0\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}
.

(iv)

Consider the isomorphism

\psi\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m},\mathbb{Z})\ni h\mapsto(h(e_{1}),\dots,h(e_{n+m}))\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m},

where
e_{1},\dots,e_{n+m}
is the standard basis for
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. Then

\psi(\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m},d_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus d_{n}\mathbb{Z};\mathbb{Z},k\mathbb{Z}))=\frac{k}{\delta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{k}{\delta_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}

and

\psi(\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m},\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m};\mathbb{Z},k\mathbb{Z}))=k\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus k\mathbb{Z}^{m}.

Consequently, by virtue of (
4.29
),

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong\left(\frac{k}{\delta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{k}{\delta_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)/\left(k\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus k\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)\\
&\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{\delta_{n}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}})\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm tor_{k}}(H_{1}^{w}(X)/H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}.\end{split}

(4.30)

This verifies the two isomorphisms from (
4.24
) and finishes the second step of the proof.

\begin{CD}\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{k}}>{}>[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]@>{\psi\varphi}>{}>\frac{k}{\delta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{k}{\delta_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\\
{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\subseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\subseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\subseteq$}}\\
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}@>{}>{\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{k}}>[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]@>{}>{\psi\varphi}>k\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus k\mathbb{Z}^{m}\end{CD}

Figure 4.14:
Verifying (
4.24
)

Since we shall have to refer to the procedure just finished and also modify it in a certain way, we summarize its steps in the form of a diagram in Figure
4.14
. (We emphasize that all the arrows in the diagram are epimorphisms.)

3rd step.
We verify (
4.25
).

Let
k,l\geq 2
be integers,
l
divisible by
k
and
k
divisible by
d=d_{n}
. Then
\gcd(d_{i},k)=\gcd(d_{i},l)=d_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
and hence, by the second step of the proof, there are isomorphisms

\text{tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{k}\right)^{m}

(4.31)

and

\text{tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{l}\right)^{m}.

(4.32)

We need to determine what form the inclusion
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
takes after passing to the right hand sides of (
4.31
) and (
4.32
). To this end, we invoke the procedure from the second step of the proof, which is summarized in the diagram in Figure
4.14
, but we apply the monomorphism
\kappa_{l}
instead of
\kappa_{k}
. This leads to the inclusions depicted in the diagram in Figure
4.15
, as we now show. (Similarly to Figure
4.14
, all the arrows in Figure
4.15
are claimed to be epimorphisms.)

\begin{CD}\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{l}}>{}>\kappa_{l/k}[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]@>{\psi\varphi}>{}>\frac{l}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{l}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\frac{l}{k}\mathbb{Z}^{m}\\
{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\subseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\subseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\subseteq$}}\\
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}@>{\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{l}}>{}>[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(X)\subseteq l\mathbb{Z}]@>{\psi\varphi}>{}>l\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus l\mathbb{Z}^{m}\\
{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\supseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\supseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\supseteq$}}\\
\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})@>{\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{l}}>{}>[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq l\mathbb{Z}]@>{\psi\varphi}>{}>\frac{l}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{l}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\end{CD}

Figure 4.15:
Towards the inclusion
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})

The second and the third line of the diagram follow immediately from the second step of the proof. We verify the first line, proceeding in two steps.

•

Recall from the first step of the proof that

\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\left\{\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)):\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}\right\}.

By applying
\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{l}
to this equality, we obtain

\begin{split}\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{l}\left(\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\right)&=\sigma^{-1}\left(\left\{\mathop{\rm co}\left(\xi^{l/k}\right):\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}\right\}\right)\\
&=\left\{\xi^{l/k}:\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}\right\}\\
&=\kappa_{l/k}[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}].\end{split}

•

By the second step of the proof,

\begin{split}\psi\varphi[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]&=\frac{k}{\delta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{k}{\delta_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\\
&=\frac{k}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{k}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}.\end{split}

Consequently,

\begin{split}\psi\varphi\left(\kappa_{l/k}[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]\right)&=\kappa_{l/k}\left(\psi\varphi[\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq k\mathbb{Z}]\right)\\
&=\kappa_{l/k}\left(\frac{k}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{k}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)\\
&=\frac{l}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{l}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\frac{l}{k}\mathbb{Z}^{m}.\end{split}

These two observations verify the first line of the diagram in Figure
4.15
.

Set
\varrho=\psi\varphi\sigma^{-1}\kappa_{l}\colon\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. Following the results summarized in Figure
4.15
, we claim that
\varrho
induces isomorphisms

\begin{split}\text{tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&=\frac{\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})}{\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cong\frac{\varrho(\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}))}{\varrho(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})}\\
&=\left(\frac{l}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{l}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\frac{l}{k}\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)/(l\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus l\mathbb{Z}^{m})\end{split}

(4.33)

and

\begin{split}\text{tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&=\frac{\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})}{\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cong\frac{\varrho(\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}))}{\varrho(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})}\\
&=\left(\frac{l}{d_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus\frac{l}{d_{n}}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)/(l\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus l\mathbb{Z}^{m}).\end{split}

(4.34)

In order to prove this claim, it suffices to show that
\ker(\varrho)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. So let
\mathcal{C}\in\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
be the transfer function of
\kappa_{l}(\mathcal{C})
and assume that
\varrho(\mathcal{C})=0
. Then
\xi^{\sharp}=0
and hence
\xi\in\mathop{\rm Div}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1}))
. Since
\sigma\colon C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\to\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is an isomorphism, it follows that
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)=\sigma(\xi)\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Consequently,
\mathcal{C}=\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm co}(\xi))\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, as was to be shown.

Now, under the natural isomorphisms
\mathbb{Z}/l\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
and
((l/k)\mathbb{Z})/l\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathbb{Z}/k\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
, the inclusion
((l/k)\mathbb{Z})/l\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/l\mathbb{Z}
takes the form of the inclusion
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\subseteq\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
. Thus, by virtue of (
4.33
) and (
4.34
), the inclusion
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
takes the form of the right hand side of Figure
4.16
.

\begin{CD}\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}){}={}\kappa_{k}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}{}\cong{}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathbb{T}^{1})^{m}\\
{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\supseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\supseteq$}}{\rotatebox{90.0}{$\supseteq$}}\\
\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}){}={}\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}{}\cong{}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathbb{T}^{1})^{m}\end{CD}

Figure 4.16:
Another form of the inclusion
\mathop{\rm tor}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathop{\rm tor}_{l}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})

Let
(k_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of positive integers with the following properties:

•

d
divides
k_{1}
and
k_{j}
divides
k_{j+1}
for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
there is
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k
divides
k_{j}
.

Then the results of this step of the proof yield isomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})&=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{k}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{k_{j}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\text{tor}_{k_{j}}(\mathbb{T}^{1})^{m}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathbb{T}^{1})^{m}=\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\right)^{m}\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm tor}\left(H_{1}^{w}(X)/H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\right)\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}.\end{split}

This verifies (
4.25
).
∎

4.7
Torsions over topologically free flows

In this section we continue our study of the cohomology group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, focusing now on the situation when
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
is a topologically free minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact (connected) manifold. We start by determining the complementary summand of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and that of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then we express
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
as a direct sum of its torsion subgroup
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and a divisible subgroup
\mathfrak{D}
of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinH}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Theorem 4.46
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal topologically free flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact manifold. Set
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
,
n+m=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(X))
and denote by
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
the elementary divisors of
H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})
in
H_{1}^{w}(X)
. Then there are direct sums

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\cong\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}

(4.35)

and

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathop{\rm tor}\Big(H_{1}^{w}(X)/H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\Big)\cong\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}.

(4.36)

Moreover, for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, the exact sequence
(\ref {Eq:exact.sequence})
from Theorem
4.36
takes the form

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{G}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa_{G}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\longrightarrow\\
&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z})^{n+m}\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z})^{n}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}})\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(4.37)

Proof.

Before turning to the proof we fix some notation. By definition of
n
,
m
and
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
, the inclusion
H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq H_{1}^{w}(X)
can be expressed in the form
d_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus d_{n}\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus 0\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. The standard basis of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n+m}
will be denoted by
e_{1},\dots,e_{n+m}
and we shall write
\eta=d_{1}\dots d_{n}
. We assume throughout the whole proof that the space
X
is pointed and we denote its base point by
z
. Recall the notation
\mathcal{F}_{z}=\mathcal{F}(-,z)\colon\Gamma\to X
and
\mathcal{C}_{z}=\mathcal{C}(-,z)\colon\Gamma\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. We shall use freely the usual identifications
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong H_{1}^{w}(\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z}
. Finally, observe that by our assumptions on
X
, for every
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(H_{1}^{w}(X),\mathbb{Z})
there is
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
such that
\xi^{\sharp}=h
.

1st step.
We collect some useful facts about lifts of extensions from
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and about the divisible subgroup
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
and
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Then the following statements hold:

(a)

we have

\mathcal{C}^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)=\mathcal{C}_{z}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathbb{Z};

this follows from Lemma
2.17
,

(b)

we have

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)=\xi^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)=\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq\mathbb{Z};

this follows from Lemma
2.17
,

(c)

\mathcal{C}
lifts across
\kappa_{l}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
if and only if
\mathcal{C}_{z}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)\subseteq l\mathbb{Z}
(in particular,
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
if and only if
\mathcal{C}_{z}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)=0
); this follows from Lemma
3.36
and from (a),

(d)

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
lifts across
\kappa_{l}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
if and only if
\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\subseteq l\mathbb{Z}
(in particular,
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
if and only if
\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0
); this follows from Lemma
3.36
and from (b).

2nd step.
For
i=1,\dots,n
fix
\xi_{i}\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
so that
(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}(e_{j})=\delta_{ij}\eta/d_{j}
for
j=1,\dots,n
and
(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}(e_{j})=0
for
j=n+1,\dots,n+m
, and let
\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
be the lift of
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})
across
\kappa_{\eta}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
. We claim that
\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{n}
form a basis for a free abelian subgroup
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and that there is a direct sum
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathcal{S}
. This will verify (
4.35
).

First notice that by virtue of (d) from the first step of the proof, all
\mathcal{C}_{i}
are well defined. Now, in order to prove the two claims, it suffices to verify the following statements.

(
\alpha
)

For all
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
implies
k_{1}=\dots=k_{n}=0
.

(
\beta
)

The set
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cup\mathcal{S}
generates the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

We begin be verifying (
\alpha
). So fix
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Then

\mathop{\rm co}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})=\eta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\right)\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})

and hence, by virtue of (d),
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}=(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i})^{\sharp}
vanishes on
H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})
. By our choice of the maps
\xi_{i}
, this leads to
k_{1}=\dots=k_{n}=0
.

To verify (
\beta
), fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Since the flow
\mathcal{F}
is topologically free by the assumptions of the theorem, the morphism
\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}\colon H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)\to H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})
is an isomorphism. Thus, since
\eta\mathcal{C}_{z}^{\sharp}=(\eta\mathcal{C})_{z}^{\sharp}\colon H_{1}^{w}(\Gamma)\to\mathbb{Z}
takes its values in
\eta\mathbb{Z}
, it follows that so does
(\eta\mathcal{C})_{z}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp})^{-1}\colon H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\to\mathbb{Z}
. Consequently,
(\eta\mathcal{C})_{z}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp})^{-1}
extends to a morphism
\varrho\colon H_{1}^{w}(X)\to\mathbb{Z}
with
\varrho(e_{j})\in(\eta/d_{j})\mathbb{Z}
for
j=1,\dots,n
and
\varrho(e_{j})=0
for
j=n+1,\dots,n+m
. By our choice of the maps
\xi_{i}
, there are integers
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}
with
\varrho=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}=(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i})^{\sharp}
. Thus, with the help of Lemma
2.17
, we obtain

\eta\mathcal{C}_{z}^{\sharp}=(\eta\mathcal{C})_{z}^{\sharp}=\varrho\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i}\Big)^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}=\mathop{\rm co}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i}\Big)_{z}^{\sharp}=\eta\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\Big)_{z}^{\sharp},

whence it follows that

\Big(\mathcal{C}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\Big)_{z}^{\sharp}=\mathcal{C}_{z}^{\sharp}-\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\Big)_{z}^{\sharp}=0.

In view of (c) from the first step of the proof, this means that
\mathcal{C}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, which verifies (
\beta
).

3rd step.
We show that
\mathcal{S}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\langle d_{1}\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,d_{n}\mathcal{C}_{n}\rangle
.

First we verify inclusion “
\subseteq
”. So fix
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
and assume that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; we show that
d_{i}
divides
k_{i}
for every
i=1,\dots,n
. Then

\eta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})=\mathop{\rm co}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i}\right)

and hence
\mathop{\rm co}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i})
lifts across
\kappa_{\eta}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Consequently,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i}
lifts across
\kappa_{\eta}
to an element of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
, whence it follows that
(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\xi_{i})^{\sharp}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}
takes its values in
\eta\mathbb{Z}
. By our choice of the maps
\xi_{i}
it follows that
d_{i}
divides
k_{i}
for every
i=1,\dots,n
.

To verify inclusion “
\supseteq
”, it suffices to show that
d_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for every
i=1,\dots,n
. By our choice of the maps
\xi_{i}
,
(d_{i}\xi_{i})^{\sharp}(H_{1}^{w}(X))=d_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}(H_{1}^{w}(X))\subseteq\eta\mathbb{Z}
for every
i=1,\dots,n
. Consequently,
d_{i}\xi_{i}
lifts across
\kappa_{\eta}
to an element of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
and hence
\mathop{\rm co}(d_{i}\xi_{i})
lifts across
\kappa_{\eta}
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Since
\kappa_{\eta}(d_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i})=d_{i}(\kappa_{\eta}\mathcal{C}_{i})=d_{i}\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})=\mathop{\rm co}(d_{i}\xi_{i})
, it follows by uniqueness of a lift that, indeed,
d_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

4th step.
Let
\mathcal{M}
consist of the maps
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
with
\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0
. Clearly,
\mathcal{M}
is a subgroup of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
and hence
\sigma(\mathcal{M})
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, where
\sigma
is the usual isomorphism
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\to\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. We show that
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\sigma(\mathcal{M})\oplus(\mathcal{S}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\sigma(\mathcal{M})\oplus\langle d_{1}\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,d_{n}\mathcal{C}_{n}\rangle
.

To verify the desired direct sum, we need to check the following statements:

(i)

\sigma(\mathcal{M})\cap(\mathcal{S}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})=0
,

(ii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\sigma(\mathcal{M})+(\mathcal{S}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

We begin with statement (i). So fix
\xi\in\mathcal{M}
and
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}(d_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i})
; we show that
k_{1}=\dots=k_{n}=0
and
\xi=0
. From the last equality we obtain

\mathop{\rm co}(\eta\xi)=\eta\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}(\eta\mathcal{C}_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})=\mathop{\rm co}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\xi_{i}\right),

whence
\eta\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\xi_{i}
and so
\eta\xi^{\sharp}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}
. By applying the last equality to the elements
e_{1},\dots,e_{n}
and by using the definition of the maps
\xi_{1},\dots\xi_{n}
, we get
k_{1}=\dots=k_{n}=0
. This yields
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)=0
and hence also
\xi=0
.

To verify statement (ii), fix a map
\zeta\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
; we show that
\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta)\in\sigma(\mathcal{M})+(\mathcal{S}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Set
k_{i}=\zeta^{\sharp}(e_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,n
. By our choice of the maps
\xi_{i}
, the morphism
(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\xi_{i})^{\sharp}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}
takes its values in
\eta\mathbb{Z}
and so the map
\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\xi_{i}
lifts across
\kappa_{\eta}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an element
\vartheta
of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
.

Set
\xi=\zeta-\vartheta
; we show that
\xi\in\mathcal{M}
. For every
j=1,\dots,n
,

\eta\xi^{\sharp}(e_{j})=\eta\zeta^{\sharp}(e_{j})-(\eta\vartheta)^{\sharp}(e_{j})=\eta\zeta^{\sharp}(e_{j})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}(\xi_{i})^{\sharp}(e_{j})=\eta k_{j}-k_{j}d_{j}\frac{\eta}{d_{j}}=0

and hence
\xi^{\sharp}(e_{j})=0
. Consequently,
\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0
and, indeed,
\xi\in\mathcal{M}
.

Now, since
\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta)=\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)+\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta)
and
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)\in\sigma(\mathcal{M})
by our discussion above, it remains to show that
\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta)\in\mathcal{S}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. By definition of
\vartheta
,

\eta\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta)=\mathop{\rm co}(\eta\vartheta)=\mathop{\rm co}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\xi_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}d_{i}\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})=\eta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}(d_{i}\mathcal{C}_{i})\right),
