(f^{\flat})^{-1}(p^{l}\pi^{1}(X))=p^{l}\mathbb{Z}
or, equivalently, that
kf\in p^{l}\pi^{1}(X)
if and only if
p^{l}
divides
k
, where
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. The implication from right to left is clear. To verify the converse, let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
kf\in p^{l}\pi^{1}(X)
and let
0\leq t\leq l
be such that
\gcd(k,p^{l})=p^{t}
. There exist integers
\alpha,\beta
with
p^{t}=\alpha k+\beta p^{l}
. Then
p^{t}f=\alpha kf+\beta p^{l}f\in p^{l}\pi^{1}(X)
and hence
f\in p^{l-t}\pi^{1}(X)
. Since
f\in\pi^{1}(X)\setminus p\pi^{1}(X)
by the assumptions, it follows that
t=l
and hence
p^{l}
divides
k
. This verifies the claim.

Now, by virtue of Theorem
5.32
, part two of Remark
5.33
and our discussion above,

F(\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l})^{\perp}=\frac{1}{p^{l}}\kappa_{p^{l}}^{*}\left(f^{\flat}\right)^{-1}(p^{l}\pi^{1}(X))=\frac{1}{p^{l}}p^{l}(p^{l}\mathbb{Z})=p^{l}\mathbb{Z}=\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p^{l}}\right)^{\perp},

which shows that, indeed,
F(\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l})=\mathbb{Z}_{p^{l}}
.

2nd step.
Let
p
be as above. We show that
\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ucs}}{{\longrightarrow}}1
as
l\to\infty
.

For every
0<s\leq 1/2
we consider the set
W_{s}=\{\exp(i2\pi t):-s<t<s\}\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{1}
. The sets
W_{s}
form a local base at
1
in
\mathbb{T}^{1}
. Moreover, for every
s\in(0,1/2]
and every
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\kappa_{k}^{-1}(W_{s})_{0}=W_{s/k}
, where
\kappa_{k}^{-1}(W_{s})_{0}
stands for the identity component of the set
\kappa_{k}^{-1}(W_{s})
in
\mathbb{T}^{1}
.

Now fix
s\in(0,1/2]
and a compact set
F\subseteq\Gamma
. By our assumptions on
\Gamma
and by compactness of
X
, there is a connected identity neighbourhood
V
in
\Gamma
with
\mathop{\rm co}(f)(V\times X)\subseteq W_{s}
. Then for every
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
\kappa_{p^{l}}\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}(V\times X)\subseteq W_{s}
and hence, by connectedness of
V
and
X
,
\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}(V\times X)\subseteq\kappa_{p^{l}}^{-1}(W_{s})_{0}=W_{s/p^{l}}
. Now let
l_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
be large enough so that
V^{p^{l_{0}}}\supseteq F
. Then for every
l\geq l_{0}
,
V^{p^{l}}\supseteq F
and so

\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}(F\times X)\subseteq\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}(V^{p^{l}}\times X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{\subseteq}}\left(\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}(V\times X)\right)^{p^{l}}\subseteq\left(W_{s/p^{l}}\right)^{p^{l}}=W_{s},

where the inclusion (
*
) follows from the cocycle identity for
\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}
. This shows that
\mathcal{D}^{(p)}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ucs}}{{\longrightarrow}}1
as
l\to\infty
.

3rd step.
We prove the proposition.

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
. From the first and the second step of the proof it follows that there is a sequence
(\mathcal{D}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of extensions in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, which satisfies the following conditions:

•

the groups
F(\mathcal{D}_{n})
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) are non-trivial, finite and mutually group-disjoint,

•

F(\mathcal{D}_{n})\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
in
2^{\mathbb{T}^{1}}
as
n\to\infty
,

•

\mathcal{D}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ucs}}{{\longrightarrow}}1
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
as
n\to\infty
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
set

\mathcal{E}_{n}=(\mathcal{D}_{(n-1)m+1},\mathcal{D}_{(n-1)m+2},\dots,\mathcal{D}_{nm})\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{m}).

By virtue of Proposition
3.19
,

F(\mathcal{E}_{n})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}F(\mathcal{D}_{(n-1)m+i})\mathrel{\lhd}\mathbb{T}^{m}

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and so the sequence
(\mathcal{E}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies the following conditions:

•

the groups
F(\mathcal{E}_{n})
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) are mutually group-disjoint,

•

F(\mathcal{E}_{n})\to\mathbb{T}^{m}
in
2^{\mathbb{T}^{m}}
as
n\to\infty
,

•

\mathcal{E}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ucs}}{{\longrightarrow}}1
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{m})
as
n\to\infty
.

By virtue of Theorem
3.23
, we may assume that the series
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}_{n}
converges u.c.s. in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{m})
and that its sum
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{m})
is minimal. Now set
\mathcal{C}_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{i}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then the groups
F(\mathcal{C}_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}F(\mathcal{E}_{i})
are finite by Proposition
3.19
and we have
\mathcal{C}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ucs}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{C}
as
n\to\infty
. Since the extension
\mathcal{C}
is minimal, this finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.47
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Let
d\geq 2
be an integer and
d=p_{1}^{k_{1}}\dots p_{n}^{k_{n}}
be the prime decomposition of
d
. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

there is
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
F(\mathcal{C})=\mathbb{Z}_{d}
,

(2)

\pi^{1}(X)\setminus p_{i}\pi^{1}(X)\neq\emptyset
for every
i=1,\dots,n
,

(3)

\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\pi^{1}(X)\setminus p_{i}\pi^{1}(X)\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

We shall follow the notation from the first step of our proof of Proposition
5.45
. To show that (1) implies (3), fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
F(\mathcal{C})=\mathbb{Z}_{d}
and denote by
\xi
the base point preserving transfer function for
\kappa_{d}\mathcal{C}
. Set
f=\xi^{\flat}(1)
. Then
f\in\pi^{1}(X)
and
\xi^{\flat}=f^{\flat}\colon\mathbb{Z}\ni k\mapsto kf\in\pi^{1}(X)
. By virtue of Theorem
5.32
and part two of Remark
5.33
,

d\mathbb{Z}=(\mathbb{Z}_{d})^{\perp}=\frac{1}{d}\kappa_{d}^{*}(f^{\flat})^{-1}(d\pi^{1}(X))=\frac{1}{d}d(f^{\flat})^{-1}(d\pi^{1}(X))=(f^{\flat})^{-1}(d\pi^{1}(X)).

Thus, for every integer
k
,
kf\in d\pi^{1}(X)
occurs if and only if
d
divides
k
. This shows that
f\in\pi^{1}(X)\setminus l\pi^{1}(X)
for every integer
l\geq 2
dividing
d
and, in particular,
f\in\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\pi^{1}(X)\setminus p_{i}\pi^{1}(X)
. Thus, (1) implies (3).

Implication (3)
\Rightarrow
(2) is clear and so it suffices to show that (2) implies (1). So assume that
\pi^{1}(X)\setminus p_{i}\pi^{1}(X)\neq\emptyset
for every
i=1,\dots,n
and write
d_{i}=p_{i}^{k_{i}}
. By the same argument as in the first step of our proof of Proposition
5.45
, we find
\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{n}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
F(\mathcal{C}_{i})=\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}
for every
i=1,\dots,n
. Set
\mathcal{C}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then by virtue of Proposition
3.19
,

F(\mathcal{C})=F\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{C}_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}F(\mathcal{C}_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}=\mathbb{Z}_{d}.

This verifies condition (1).
∎

Theorem 5.48
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Assume that
K
is a non-trivial closed totally disconnected subgroup of a solenoid
S_{{\bf p}}
and let
(c_{j})_{j\in J}
be a generating net of torsions for
K
. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

there is an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(S_{{\bf p}})
with
F(\mathcal{C})=K
,

(2)

\pi^{1}(X)
contains a subgroup
A\cong K^{\perp}
, which is
c_{j}
-pure in
\pi^{1}(X)
for every
j\in J
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1st step.
We collect some preliminary observations that will be of use to us in the proof of the theorem.

Write
{\bf p}=(p_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
p_{0}=1
. Given
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
d_{n}
be the positive integer with
\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}=\mathop{\rm pr}_{n}(K)
. Then
\kappa_{p_{n}}(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n+1}})=\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Consequently, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
there is
q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
co-prime with
d_{n}
such that
d_{n+1}q_{n}=d_{n}p_{n}
. Moreover, since
K
is closed in
S_{\bf p}
,

K=\lim_{\longleftarrow}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa_{p_{n}}}}{{\longleftarrow}}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n+1}}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}=\left\{(z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in S_{\bf p}:z_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\,\text{for every}\,n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.

Clearly,
(d_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a generating net of torsions for
K
. Under the isomorphism
\lambda\colon\mathbb{Z}/{\bf p}\to(S_{\bf p})^{*}
defined by (
2.19
) in Subsection
2.4.13
, the annihilator
K^{\perp}
of
K
in
(S_{\bf p})^{*}
corresponds to

\lambda^{-1}(K^{\perp})=\left\{\frac{ld_{n}}{p_{0}\dots p_{n-1}}:l\in\mathbb{Z},n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{d_{n}}{p_{0}\dots p_{n-1}}\mathbb{Z}.

(5.20)

We claim that

K^{\perp}\subseteq\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}d_{n}(S_{\bf p})^{*}.

(5.21)

Indeed, the inclusion (
5.21
) is a consequence of (
5.20
) and the following facts:

•

the union on the right hand side of (
5.20
) is increasing in
n\in\mathbb{N}
; this follows from the identity
d_{n+1}q_{n}=d_{n}p_{n}
,

•

the intersection on the right hand side of (
5.21
) is decreasing in
n\in\mathbb{N}
; this follows from the fact that
d_{n}
divides
d_{n+1}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

•

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\lambda\left(\frac{d_{n}}{p_{0}\dots p_{n-1}}\mathbb{Z}\right)\subseteq d_{n}(S_{\bf p})^{*}.

2nd step.
Let
\varrho\colon K^{\perp}\to\pi^{1}(X)
be a monomorphism of groups. We show that the following conditions are equivalent for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

(a)

\mathop{\rm im}(\varrho)
is
d_{n}
-pure in
\pi^{1}(X)
,

(b)

\mathop{\rm im}(\varrho)\cap d_{n}\pi^{1}(X)\subseteq d_{n}\mathop{\rm im}(\varrho)
,

(c)

\varrho^{-1}(d_{n}\pi^{1}(X))\subseteq d_{n}K^{\perp}
,

(d)

(1/d_{n})\varrho^{-1}(d_{n}\pi^{1}(X))\subseteq K^{\perp}
.

The equivalence of (a) and (b) follows at once from the definition of
d_{n}
-purity. Further, the implication (c)
\Rightarrow
(b) is clear and the converse holds since
\varrho
is a monomorphism. Finally, the implication (c)
\Rightarrow
(d) holds since the group
(S_{\bf p})^{*}
is torsion-free and the converse follows from the inclusion (
5.21
) from the first step of the proof.

3rd step.
We prove the equivalence of conditions (1) and (2) from the theorem.

First, let us mention the following facts.

•

Condition (2) from the statement of the theorem is independent on the choice of a generating net of torsions for
K
; this follows from the second part of Remark
5.38
and from the fact that
d
-purity implies
d^{\prime}
-purity for all
d,d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
d^{\prime}
dividing
d
. As a result of this observation we shall restrict ourselves to the generating net of torsions
(d_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
for
K
that has been constructed in the first step of the proof.

•

Let
\varrho\colon K^{\perp}\to\pi^{1}(X)
be a morphism of groups. Then either
\varrho
is a monomorphism or else it is trivial. This follows from the fact that the group
\pi^{1}(X)
is torsion-free and the group
K^{\perp}
has rank
1
.

To show that (2) follows from (1), fix
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(S_{\bf p})
with
F(\mathcal{C})=K
. Consider the quotient morphism
p_{K}\colon S_{\bf p}\to S_{\bf p}/K
and denote by
\xi
the base point preserving transfer function
\xi\colon X\to S_{\bf p}/K
for
p_{K}\mathcal{C}
. By virtue of Theorem
5.40
,

K^{\perp}=F(\mathcal{C})^{\perp}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{d_{n}}\left(\xi^{\flat}\right)^{-1}(d_{n}\pi^{1}(X)).

(5.22)

Since the group
K
is non-trivial by the assumptions of the theorem, (
5.22
) and Remark
5.39
show that
\xi^{\flat}\colon K^{\perp}\to\pi^{1}(X)
is non-trivial and hence it is a monomorphism. By applying (
5.22
) once more and by using the equivalence of (a) and (d) from the second step of the proof, we conclude that the group
A:=\mathop{\rm im}(\xi^{\flat})\cong K^{\perp}
is a
d_{n}
-pure subgroup of
\pi^{1}(X)
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. This verifies condition (2).

To show that (1) follows from (2), assume that
\pi^{1}(X)
contains a subgroup
A\cong K^{\perp}
, which is
d_{n}
-pure in
\pi^{1}(X)
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Fix a monomorphism
\varrho\colon K^{\perp}\to\pi^{1}(X)
with
\mathop{\rm im}(\varrho)=A
. The equivalence of (a) and (d) from the second step of the proof yields the inclusion

\frac{1}{d_{n}}\varrho^{-1}(d_{n}\pi^{1}(X))\subseteq K^{\perp}

(5.23)

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By virtue of (
2.15
) from Subsection
2.4.10
,
\varrho=\xi^{\flat}
for an appropriate continuous base point preserving map
\xi\colon X\to S_{\bf p}/K
. Since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible by Remark
3.39
and torsion-free by connectedness of both
\Gamma
and
X
, we may apply Theorem
4.14
(f) to find
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(S_{\bf p})
with
p_{K}\mathcal{C}=\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
. Then, by Theorem
5.40
and (
5.23
),

K^{\perp}\subseteq F(\mathcal{C})^{\perp}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{d_{n}}\left(\xi^{\flat}\right)^{-1}(d_{n}\pi^{1}(X))\subseteq K^{\perp},

and hence
F(\mathcal{C})=K
. This verifies condition (1).
∎

Corollary 5.49
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Given an integer
d\geq 2
, a solenoid
S_{{\bf p}}
and a subgroup
K\cong\mathbb{Z}_{d}
of
S_{{\bf p}}
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

there is an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(S_{{\bf p}})
with
F(\mathcal{C})=K
,

(2)

\pi^{1}(X)
contains a
d
-pure subgroup isomorphic to
K^{\perp}
.

Remark 5.50
.

We wish to mention the following facts.

•

A finite group
K
is isomorphic to a subgroup of a solenoid
S_{{\bf p}}
if and only if it is isomorphic to a quotient group of
(S_{{\bf p}})^{*}
. Since
(S_{{\bf p}})^{*}
has rank
1
, it follows that the number of the elementary divisors of
K
is
1
, and hence
K
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus the corollary covers all (non-trivial) finite subgroups of all solenoids.

•

Given a solenoid
S_{{\bf p}}
and an integer
d\geq 2
, the group
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
may or may not be isomorphic to a subgroup of
S_{{\bf p}}
, depending on the sequence
{\bf p}=(p_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. As a matter of fact, the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

S_{{\bf p}}
contains a subgroup isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
,

(b)

\mathbb{Z}/{\bf p}
factors onto
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
,

(c)

there is
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\gcd(p_{n},d)=1
for every
n\geq m
.

First, the equivalence of (a) and (b) follows from the isomorphism
(S_{\bf p})^{*}\cong\mathbb{Z}/{\bf p}
. Further, the implication (a)
\Rightarrow
(c) follows from the first step of the proof of Theorem
5.48
. Finally, in proving the implication (c)
\Rightarrow
(b) one constructs the required epimorphism
\mathbb{Z}/{\bf p}\to\mathbb{Z}_{d}
by successively extending an arbitrary epimorphism
\mathbb{Z}/p_{0}\dots p_{m-1}\to\mathbb{Z}_{d}
to the groups
\mathbb{Z}/p_{0}\dots p_{n}
for
n=m,m+1,m+2,\dots
.

Proof of Corollary
5.49
.

Since the group
\mathbb{Z}_{d}
has the singleton
d
as its generating net of torsions, the corollary follows immediately from Theorem
5.48
.
∎

Proposition 5.51
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Given
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected with
\dim(G)=n\in\mathbb{N}
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains a minimal extension,

(2)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{n})
contains a minimal extension.

Proof.

In the whole proof we shall use the notation
q\colon G\to\mathbb{T}(G)
for the maximal toral quotient of
G
. Recall that the kernel
\ker(q)
of
q
is a totally disconnected subgroup of
G
. We divide the proof into two steps.

1st step.
We show that if
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
satisfies
q(H)=\mathbb{T}(G)
then
H=G
.

Since
\mathbb{T}(G)=\mathbb{T}^{\dim(G)}=\mathbb{T}^{n}
, we may identify
\mathbb{T}(G)^{*}
with
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Let
j
be the inclusion morphism
H\to G
and set
r=qj\colon H\to\mathbb{T}(G)
. By our assumptions on
H
,
r
is an epimorphism. The dual morphism
j^{*}
to
j
is an epimorphism
G^{*}\to H^{*}
with kernel
H^{\perp}
. Therefore, in order to show that
H=G
, it suffices to verify that
j^{*}
is a monomorphism.

To this end, fix
\chi\in G^{*}
with
j^{*}(\chi)=0
. Let
e_{1},\dots,e_{n}
be the standard basis for
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then
q^{*}(e_{1}),\dots,q^{*}(e_{n})
are independent in
G^{*}
and hence, since
n=\dim(G)=\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})
, there exist integers
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}
and
k\neq 0
such that
k\chi=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}q^{*}(e_{i})
. By applying the morphism
j^{*}
to the last equality, we obtain

0=kj^{*}(\chi)=j^{*}(k\chi)=j^{*}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}q^{*}(e_{i})\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}r^{*}(e_{i})=r^{*}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}e_{i}\right).

Since
r^{*}
is a monomorphism, it follows that
k_{1}=\dots=k_{n}=0
and hence
k\chi=0
. By connectedness of
G
, the group
G^{*}
is torsion-free, and so
k\chi=0
and
k\neq 0
imply
\chi=0
. Thus,
j^{*}
is a monomorphism, as was to be shown.

2nd step.
We verify the equivalence of the conditions (1) and (2). First, we recall from the first step of the proof that
\mathbb{T}(G)=\mathbb{T}^{n}
.

The implication (1)
\Rightarrow
(2) is clear, for if
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is minimal then so is
q\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}(G))
. Conversely, assume that
\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}(G))
is minimal. Since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and torsion-free by our assumptions on
\Gamma
and
X
(see Remark
3.39
and Theorem
4.10
), and since the kernel
\ker(q)
of
q
is totally disconnected, we may apply Theorem
4.14
(f) to find
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with
q\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}
. Now
F(\mathcal{C})
is a closed subgroup of
G
satisfying
qF(\mathcal{C})=F(q\mathcal{C})=F(\mathcal{D})=\mathbb{T}(G)
. Thus, by the first step of the proof,
F(\mathcal{C})=G
. This means that the extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is minimal, which verifies condition (1).
∎

Corollary 5.52
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(S_{\bf p})
contains a minimal extension for every sequence
{\bf p}
,

(ii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(S_{\bf p})
contains a minimal extension for some sequence
{\bf p}
,

(iii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains a minimal extension.

Proof.

Since
S_{\bf p}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
is connected and
\dim(S_{\bf p})=1
for every sequence
{\bf p}
, the corollary follows directly from Proposition
5.51
.
∎

Chapter 6
First Cohomology Groups

6.1
Groups of minimal extensions

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a connected group. In this section we show that under mild assumptions on
\mathcal{F}
and
G
, the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of an arbitrary torsion-free abelian group
A
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{c}
. We concentrate on the following three classes of flows
\mathcal{F}
:

•

the group
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LCGp}\setminus\mathsf{CGp}
is amenable, the space
X
is compact and the flow
\mathcal{F}
possesses a free point; see Theorem
6.3
,

•

the group
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
is simply connected, the space
X
is compact and
1\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\pi^{1}(X))<\mathfrak{c}
; see Theorem
6.5
,

•

the group
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
is connected, the space
X
is a compact connected manifold and the flow
\mathcal{F}
possesses a free cycle; see Theorem
6.6
.

One of the main tools used in this section is the following theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
\mathfrak{k}
be an infinite cardinal number. Then the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

For every torsion-free group
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{k}
and every non-trivial connected group
H\in\mathsf{CAGp}
with
\mathop{\rm w}(H)\leq\mathfrak{k}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
contains an isomorphic copy of
A
.

(2)

For every torsion-free group
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{k}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains an isomorphic copy of
A
.

(3)

For every non-trivial connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
with
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{k}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus 1\neq\emptyset
(that is,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains a minimal extension).

Remark 6.2
.

Notice the following facts.

•

The assumption of torsion-freeness of the group
A
is necessary in both (1) and (2). This follows from the fact that for every connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains no non-trivial torsion elements. Indeed, if
k\in\mathbb{N}
then
\kappa_{k}\colon G\to G
is an epimorphism by connectedness of
G
and hence
F(\mathcal{C}^{k})=F(\kappa_{k}\mathcal{C})=\kappa_{k}F(\mathcal{C})=\kappa_{k}(G)=G
for every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus 1
.

•

If
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
is torsion-free with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{k}
then the divisible hull
\mathbb{Q}\otimes A
of
A
satisfies
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(\mathbb{Q}\otimes A)=\mathop{\rm rank}(A)\leq\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{k}
. Hence
\mathbb{Q}\otimes A
can be viewed as a subgroup of
\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{k})}
, the cardinality of the latter group being
\mathop{\rm card}(\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{k})})=\mathfrak{k}
. Consequently, a groupoid
B
contains an isomorphic copy of every torsion-free group
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{k}
if and only if
B
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{k})}
. (This applies to the groupoids
B=\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
and
B=\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
in conditions (1) and (2), respectively.) In case of
\mathfrak{k}=\mathfrak{c}
we have
\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{k})}=\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\cong\mathbb{R}
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

We divide the proof into three steps. First, let us fix some notation. Let
A,B
be abelian groups and let
E
be a sub-groupoid of
B
. By a (mono)morphism
A\to E
we mean a (mono)morphism of groups
A\to B
, whose image is contained in
E
. This terminology will be applied twice in the proof. First in the case of
B=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
E=\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, and second in the case of
B=\mathop{\rm Hom}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and
E=\mathop{\rm Hom}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
with
H\in\mathsf{CAGp}
.

1st step.
We recollect some useful properties of the tensor product in
\mathsf{AbGp}
.

Let
A,C
be non-trivial torsion-free abelian groups with cardinalities at most
\mathfrak{k}
. Then the following statements hold.

(
\iota
)

If
a\in A\setminus 0
and
c\in C\setminus 0
then
a\otimes c\neq 0
; in particular,
A\otimes C
is a non-trivial abelian group. Indeed, since the groups
A,C
are torsion-free and the group
\mathbb{Q}
is divisible, there are morphisms
\varphi\colon A\to\mathbb{Q}
and
\psi\colon C\to\mathbb{Q}
with
\varphi(a)=\psi(c)=1
. Let
e\colon A\times C\to A\otimes C
be the universal bilinear map. Since the map
b\colon A\times C\ni(u,v)\mapsto\varphi(u)\psi(v)\in\mathbb{Q}
is also bilinear, there is a morphism
h\colon A\otimes C\to\mathbb{Q}
with
he=b
. Then
h(a\otimes c)=b(a,c)=\varphi(a)\psi(c)=1\neq 0
, and hence
a\otimes c\neq 0
.

(
\iota\iota
)

The group
A\otimes C
is torsion-free. This follows from the following three facts. First, both
A
and
C
are direct limits of (non-trivial) finitely generated free abelian groups. Second, the (non-trivial) finitely generated free abelian groups are closed with respect to the tensor product. Finally, the tensor product commutes with direct limits.

(
\iota\iota\iota
)

The cardinality of
A\otimes C
is at most
\mathfrak{k}
. Indeed, the group
A\otimes C
is generated by the monomial tensors
a\otimes c
(
a\in A
,
c\in C
) and these form a set with cardinality at most
\mathfrak{k}
.

2nd step.
We show that (1)
\Rightarrow
(2)
\Rightarrow
(3).

Implication (1)
\Rightarrow
(2) is immediate, since
\mathop{\rm w}(\mathbb{T}^{1})=\aleph_{0}\leq\mathfrak{k}
. To verify implication (2)
\Rightarrow
(3), let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be non-trivial connected with
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{k}
. Then
G^{*}
is a non-trivial torsion-free abelian group with
\mathop{\rm card}(G^{*})=\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{k}
and so condition (2) yields a monomorphism
\varphi\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. By virtue of Corollary
3.8
, there is
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus 1
with
\varphi=\Phi_{G}(\mathcal{C})=\mathcal{C}^{*}
. Thus,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus 1\neq\emptyset
and condition (3) hods.

3rd step.
We finish the proof by verifying implication (3)
\Rightarrow
(1).

Fix
A
and
H
as in (1). We may clearly assume that
A
is non-trivial. By the first step of the proof, the group
A\otimes H^{*}
is non-trivial torsion-free abelian with cardinality at most
\mathfrak{k}
and hence its dual group
(A\otimes H^{*})^{*}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
is non-trivial connected with weight at most
\mathfrak{k}
. Condition (3) thus yields
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}((A\otimes H^{*})^{*})\setminus 1\neq\emptyset
. By virtue of Corollary
3.8
this means that there is a monomorphism
\varphi\colon A\otimes H^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Now consider the mapping

\psi\colon A\ni a\mapsto(\chi\mapsto\varphi(a\otimes\chi))\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}).

It is a standard procedure to verify that
\psi
is well defined (that is, it takes its values in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
) and that it is a morphism from the group
A
to the groupoid
\mathop{\rm Hom}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. We show that
\psi
is in fact a monomorphism that takes its values in
\mathop{\rm Mon}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. In view of Corollary
3.8
this will secure that
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(H)
contains an isomorphic copy of
A
.

To see that
\psi
is a monomorphism, let
a\in A\setminus 0
. Fix
\chi\in H^{*}\setminus 0
. By virtue of (
\iota
), we have
a\otimes\chi\neq 0
. Since
\varphi
is a monomorphism, it follows
\psi(a)(\chi)=\varphi(a\otimes\chi)\neq 0
. Thus,
\psi(a)\neq 0
and so
\psi
is indeed a monomorphism. We finish the proof by showing that
\psi(a)\in\mathop{\rm Mon}(H^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
for every
a\in A
. If
a=0
then
\psi(a)=0
and so in this case the statement holds. If
a\neq 0
then, as above,
\psi(a)(\chi)\neq 0
for every
\chi\in H^{*}\setminus 0
, which means that
\psi(a)
is a monomorphism.
∎

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LCGp}\setminus\mathsf{CGp}
be an amenable group,
X
be a compact space and
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with a free point. If both
\Gamma
and
X
are second countable then the following statements hold:

(a)

For every torsion-free group
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{c}
and every non-trivial connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
with
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
A
.

(b)

For every non-trivial connected second countable group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of the quotient group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
.

Remark 6.4
.

In connection with statement (b) let us mention two facts. First, as observed in Remark
6.2
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains no non-trivial torsion elements. Therefore, it is necessary in (b) to factor out the torsion subgroup
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Second, if the space
X
is connected and the group
\Gamma
has no non-trivial finite abelian quotient groups then, by Theorem
4.10
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is torsion-free. In such case the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
itself.

Proof of Theorem
6.3
.

We verify statement (a). From the equivalence of conditions (1) and (3) in Theorem
6.1
it follows that it suffices to check the following statement:

(
\circ
)

for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected with
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{c}
, there is a minimal extension in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

So fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
connected with weight at most
\mathfrak{c}
. The dual group
G^{*}
of
G
is then torsion-free with cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
and so there is a monomorphism
\sigma\colon G^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
. Under the usual identifications
\mathbb{R}^{*}\cong\mathbb{R}
and
G^{**}\cong G
, the dual morphism
\sigma^{*}\colon\mathbb{R}\to G
of
\sigma
has a dense image.

By virtue of
[
9
, Theorem 6]
, there is an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
such that the flow
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X\times\mathbb{R}
is topologically transitive. Since the phase space
X\times\mathbb{R}
of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}}
is a Polish space, transitivity means that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}}
possesses a dense orbit. Now, the map
\mathop{\rm id}_{X}\times\sigma^{*}\colon X\times\mathbb{R}\to X\times G
is continuous, has a dense image and constitutes a morphism of flows
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\sigma^{*}\mathcal{C}}
. It follows that the flow
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma^{*}\mathcal{C}}
also possesses a dense orbit and hence
F(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{C})=G
. Thus,
\sigma^{*}\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is minimal, which verifies condition (
\circ
).

To verify statement (b), fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
non-trivial connected second countable. Then by our assumptions on
\Gamma
and
X
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a Polish group with the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets, and so it has cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
. Consequently, the quotient group
A=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
is torsion-free and also has cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
. Since
\mathop{\rm w}(G)=\aleph_{0}<\mathfrak{c}
, we may apply part (a) of the theorem to conclude that
A
has an isomorphic copy within
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.
∎

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{CLAC}
simply connected and with
X
compact. If
1\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(\pi^{1}(X))<\mathfrak{c}
then the following statement holds:

(i)

For every torsion-free group
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{c}
and every non-trivial connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
with
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
A
.

If, in addition, both
\Gamma
and
X
are second countable and
\Gamma
is locally compact then the following statement also holds.

(ii)

\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
for every non-trivial connected second countable group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
.

Proof.

We begin by verifying statement (i). By virtue of Theorem
6.1
, we need to show that
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus 1
is nonempty for every non-trivial connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
with weight
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{c}
. So let
G
be such a group. Denote by
q\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
the composition of the canonical quotient morphisms
\pi\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. By Remark
3.39
and Theorem
4.10
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and torsion-free and hence it is a rational linear space. Consequently,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is also a rational linear space and
q
is thus an epimorphism of rational linear spaces. It follows that
q
possesses a right inverse in the class of morphisms of rational linear spaces, that is, there is a morphism
r\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
qr=\mathop{\rm id}
.

Now, by virtue of Theorem
3.43
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
has rational linear dimension at least
\mathfrak{c}
. Since the group
G^{*}
is abelian torsion-free with rank at most
\mathfrak{c}
, there is a monomorphism
h\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Set
k=rh
. Since
h
is a monomorphism and
h=qk
, it follows that
k
is a monomorphism
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Moreover, if
\chi\in G^{*}\setminus 1
then
1\neq h(\chi)=q(k(\chi))
and so
\pi(k(\chi))\notin\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Thus
k(\chi)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is minimal for every
\chi\in G^{*}\setminus 1
and so
k\in\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})\setminus 1
. Corollary
3.8
then yields that
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is non-trivial, as was to be shown.

Now assume that both
\Gamma
and
X
are second countable and that
\Gamma
is locally compact. Fix
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
as in (ii). With the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a Polish group and hence it has cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
. Moreover, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is torsion-free by Theorem
4.10
, and so statement (ii) follows from statement (i).
∎

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact connected manifold. Assume that
\mathcal{F}
possesses a free cycle. Then the following statements hold.

(i)

For every torsion-free group
A\in\mathsf{AbGp}
with
\mathop{\rm card}(A)\leq\mathfrak{c}
and every non-trivial connected group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
with
\mathop{\rm w}(G)\leq\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
A
.

(ii)

For every non-trivial connected second countable group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps. First, let us recall and fix some notation. Write
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
and
n+m=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(X))
. By the assumptions of the theorem,
m\neq 0
. Further, let
\mathcal{G}
consist of the extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, whose induced morphisms
\mathcal{C}^{\sharp}\colon\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)\to\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
vanish. Finally, set
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
\mathcal{G}
is clearly a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is a subgroup of
\mathcal{G}
.

1st step.
We show that there is an exact sequence of abelian groups

0\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{m}\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}/(\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\longrightarrow 0.

In order to do this, two facts need a verification. First,
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Second,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathbb{Z}^{m}
in such a way that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{Z}^{m}\cong\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. The first fact follows immediately by definition of
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Now we turn to the proof of the second fact.

Let
p\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
be the covering morphism with kernel
\mathbb{Z}
. Then, by virtue of Lemma
3.36
,
\mathcal{G}
consists of those extensions
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, which lift across
p
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
. Consequently,
\widehat{p}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})\ni\mathcal{D}\to p\mathcal{D}\in\mathcal{G}
is an isomorphism of groups. Set
\mathcal{K}=\widehat{p}(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R}))
. Then
\mathcal{K}
is a subgroup of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and we have an isomorphism

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})\cong\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{K}.

Denote by
\psi
the isomorphism
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\to\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
acting by the rule
\psi(\xi)=\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
. Given
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
, the following conditions are equivalent:

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)\in\mathcal{K}
,

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)
lifts across
p
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
,

•

\xi
lifts across
p
to an element of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
.

Consequently, under the usual isomorphism
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)
, we have

\psi^{-1}(\mathcal{K})=C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}).

(6.1)

Similarly, by virtue of Lemma
2.17
, the following conditions are equivalent for every
\xi\in C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
:

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
,

•

\mathop{\rm co}(\xi)^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)=0
,

•

\xi^{\sharp}\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}\pi_{1}(\Gamma)=0
,

•

\xi^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0
.

Therefore,

\psi^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})=C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\{f\in\pi^{1}(X):f^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0\}.

(6.2)

Now, from the identities (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) it follows that the group
\mathcal{K}
is a direct summand in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and its complementary summand is isomorphic to the group
A=\{f\in\pi^{1}(X):f^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0\}
. Further, recall from Section
5.3
in Chapter
5
that there is an isomorphism of groups
\sigma\colon\pi^{1}(X)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(H_{1}^{w}(X),\mathbb{Z})
, acting by the rule
\sigma(f)=f^{\sharp}
. Also, under the natural isomorphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}(H_{1}^{w}(X),\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n+m}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}
, the group
\sigma(A)
corresponds to
\mathbb{Z}^{m}
, and so we have a splitting
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=\mathcal{K}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. It follows that there are isomorphisms of groups

\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\cong\mathcal{G}/(\mathcal{K}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m})\cong(\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{K})/\mathbb{Z}^{m}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{Z}^{m},

as was to be shown.

2nd step.
We verify statement (i) from the theorem. Since every torsion-free abelian group with cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
can be viewed as a subgroup of
\mathbb{R}
, by virtue of Theorem
6.1
it suffices to show that
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathbb{R}
.

Let
\pi
denote the quotient morphism
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. By the first step of the proof,
\mathbb{Z}^{m}
can be viewed as a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
in such a way that
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})/\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. Since
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
is a real (and hence also rational) linear space, we get inclusions
\mathbb{Z}^{m}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}^{m}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
. Clearly,
\mathbb{Q}^{m}
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
; denote its complementary summand by
L
. Since
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})\neq 0
, we have
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R}))\geq 1
and hence
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R}))\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Consequently,
L
is a rational linear space with dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(L)\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Also, by definition of
L
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\cong L\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}
.

Now, the group
\mathcal{G}
is torsion free and divisible by its definition, and
(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}
is a direct summand in
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
with a torsion-free complementary summand
L
. Consequently,
\pi^{-1}((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
is a divisible subgroup of
\mathcal{G}
and we may write
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{L}\oplus\pi^{-1}((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
, where
\mathcal{L}
is a rational linear subspace of
\mathcal{G}
. Given
l\in L
, fix
\mathcal{C}_{l}\in\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{D}_{l}\in\pi^{-1}((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
with
l=\pi(\mathcal{C}_{l}+\mathcal{D}_{l})
. An elementary argument shows that for
l_{1},l_{2}\in L
with
l_{1}\neq l_{2}
, one has
\mathcal{C}_{l_{1}}\neq\mathcal{C}_{l_{2}}
. Therefore,
\mathop{\rm card}(\mathcal{L})\geq\mathop{\rm card}(L)\geq\mathfrak{c}
, and hence
\mathcal{L}
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathbb{R}
. Now it remains only to show that
\mathcal{L}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. This follows from two facts. First, the non-minimal extensions in
\mathcal{G}
project to
\mathop{\rm tor}(\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}^{\prime})=(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}
via
\pi
. Second, by definition of
\mathcal{L}
, we have
\mathcal{L}\cap\pi^{-1}((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})=0
.

3rd step.
We finish the proof of the theorem by verifying statement (ii).

So let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a non-trivial connected second countable group. Then the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is torsion-free by connectedness of
\Gamma
and
X
, see Theorem
4.10
. Moreover,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a Polish group with the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets and hence its cardinality is at most
\mathfrak{c}
. Thus, statement (ii) follows immediately by applying statement (i) to
A=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.
∎

Our assumption on the existence of a free cycle for the flow
\mathcal{F}
in Theorem
6.6
can not be dropped, as the following example demonstrates.

Example 6.7
.

Let
G
be a compact connected Lie group and
H
be a closed subgroup of
G
with the topological dimension
\dim(H)=k
. Consider the usual transitive action
\mathcal{F}\colon G\curvearrowright G/H
and choose
z=H
as the base point for
G/H
. If
G
acts in a minimal way on a space
X
then, by compactness of
G
, the action is necessarily transitive. Consequently, the space
X
is (up to homeomorphism) a homogeneous space of
G
and hence
\dim(X)\leq\dim(G)
. It follows that for
l>k
there is no minimal
G
-flow on
G/H\times\mathbb{T}^{l}
and hence
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{T}^{l})=e
. From the point of view of Theorem
6.6
, this must be due to the fact that the flow
\mathcal{F}
does not possess a free cycle. To see this, let
\mathcal{F}_{z}=q\colon G\to G/H
be the canonical projection. Since
G
is a locally trivial fibre bundle over
G/H
with the fibre
H
and since
G
and
G/H
are arc-wise connected by our assumptions, the long exact homotopy sequence of the bundle yields exactness of the upper row of the diagram in Figure
6.18
below (see
[
20
, Section 9.8]
). It follows that there is an isomorphism of groups

\pi_{1}(G/H)/\mathop{\rm im}(q^{\sharp})=\pi_{1}(G/H)/\ker(\partial)\cong H/H_{0},

where the group
H/H_{0}=\pi_{0}(H)
of the path-components of
H
is finite (
H
being a compact Lie group). Since both
p_{G}
and
p_{G/H}
are epimorphisms and the diagram in Figure
6.18
commutes, it follows that the group

H_{1}^{w}(G/H)/H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=H_{1}^{w}(G/H)/\mathcal{F}_{z}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(G)=H_{1}^{w}(G/H)/\mathop{\rm im}(q^{\sharp})

is also finite. This means that the flow
\mathcal{F}
does not possess a free cycle indeed.

\begin{CD}\pi_{1}(G)@>{q^{\sharp}}>{}>\pi_{1}(G/H)@>{\partial}>{}>H/H_{0}@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{p_{G}}V{}V@V{p_{G/H}}V{}V\\
H_{1}^{w}(G)@>{}>{q^{\sharp}}>H_{1}^{w}(G/H)\end{CD}

Figure 6.18:
The flow
\mathcal{F}
does not possess a free cycle

6.2
First cohomology groups with integer coefficients

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. Our aim in this section is to express the inclusions
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
in terms of inclusions of familiar (topological) abelian groups, as well as to compute the first cohomology group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathcal{F}
with integer coefficients. We concentrate on the following situations:

(i)

the acting group
\Gamma
of
\mathcal{F}
is a simply connected Lie group and the phase space
X
of
\mathcal{F}
is a compact second countable space with
\pi^{1}(X)\neq 0
, see Theorem
6.12
,

(ii)

the acting group
\Gamma
of
\mathcal{F}
is a connected Lie group, the phase space
X
of
\mathcal{F}
is a compact manifold, the flow
\mathcal{F}
is topologically free and possesses a free cycle, see Theorem
6.14
.

We also know from Corollary
3.41
that, in case (i), the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is the additive topological group of a real separable Banach space. In Proposition
6.18
we show that, in case (ii), the structure of a Banach space is carried by the divisible subgroup
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, which forms an open topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Before turning to the mentioned results we prove two propositions, which indicate how the results of this section can be used to determine the possible values of the functor
F
on the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. First, recall that for a minimal flow
\mathcal{F}
we use notation
\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
for the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
and that this extension takes its values in
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}=(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}^{*}\in\mathsf{CAGp}
.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow,
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

there is
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with
F(\mathcal{C})=H
,

(b)

there is
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
with
qF(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=H
,

(c)

there is
k\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
with
k^{-1}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=H^{\perp}
.

Remark 6.9
.

Observe that the equality
k^{-1}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=H^{\perp}
from condition (c) can be reformulated in the following way:

•

k\left(H^{\perp}\right)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
k\left(G^{*}\setminus H^{\perp}\right)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Proof of Proposition
6.8
.

The equivalence of (a) and (b) follows from the following facts:

•

every
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is of the form
\mathcal{C}=q\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
for some
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
; this follows directly from the definition of the free extension of
\mathcal{F}
,

•

F(q\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=qF(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
for every
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
; this follows from Theorem
3.7
(3).

The equivalence of (b) and (c) follows from the following facts:

•

the elements of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
are in a one-to-one correspondence with the elements of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; this follows from the isomorphism
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}\cong(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}
,

•

a morphism
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
maps
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
onto
H
if and only if its dual morphism
q^{*}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*})
satisfies
\left(q^{*}\right)^{-1}\left(F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}\right)=H^{\perp}
; this is clear,

•

under the usual isomorphism
(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}})^{*}\cong(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})_{d}
,
F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})^{\perp}
corresponds to
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; this follows from Theorem
4.7
(2).

∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow,
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and
H\mathrel{\lhd}G
. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

there is
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
with
G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
and
F(\mathcal{C})=H
,

(ii)

there is an epimorphism
q\colon\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to G
with
qF(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})=H
,

(iii)

there is a monomorphism
k\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
k^{-1}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=H^{\perp}
.

Remark 6.11
.

We recall from Proposition
4.9
(d) that for a group
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
and an extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the symbol
G_{\mathcal{C}}
is used to denote the closed subgroup of
G
generated by the values of
\mathcal{C}
. Also, similarly to Remark
6.9
, condition (iii) can be reformulated in the following way:

•

the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains an isomorphic copy of
G^{*}
in such a way that
G^{*}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}=H^{\perp}
.

Proof of Proposition
6.10
.

The equivalence of (i) and (ii) is proved by the same line of reasoning as the equivalence of (a) and (b) in Proposition
6.8
, with the following additional argument:

•

given
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
and
\mathcal{C}=q\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}}
, the equality
G_{\mathcal{C}}=G
holds if and only if
q
is an epimorphism
\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}\to G
; this follows from Proposition
4.9
(
\iota
).

Likewise, the equivalence of (ii) and (iii) follows by the same argument as the equivalence of (b) and (c) in Proposition
6.8
; one uses, additionally, that
q\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},G)
is an epimorphism if and only if
k=q^{*}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is a monomorphism.
∎

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Assume that the space
X
is second countable and
\pi^{1}(X)\neq 0
. Then there are isomorphisms of groups

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\pi^{1}(X)&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\big(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\end{array}

(6.3)

and topological isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong&b\mathbb{R}&\times&1&\times&\pi^{1}(X)^{\perp}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&b\mathbb{R}&\times&b\mathbb{R}&\times&\big(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)^{*}&\cong&\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}.\end{array}

(6.4)

Also, there is an isomorphism

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\left((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right).

(6.5)

If, in addition, the group
\pi^{1}(X)
is finitely generated and
k=\mathop{\rm rank}\left(\pi^{1}(X)\right)
then the three isomorphisms above take the form

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{k}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{k}&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}},\end{array}

(6.6)

respectively,

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong&b\mathbb{R}&\times&1&\times&\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\perp}\right)^{k}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&b\mathbb{R}&\times&b\mathbb{R}&\times&\left(\mathbb{Q}^{*}\right)^{k}&\cong&\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}},\end{array}

(6.7)

and, finally,

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{k}.

(6.8)

Remark 6.13
.

We wish to mention the following facts.

•

In the topological isomorphisms (
6.4
) and (
6.7
) the group
\mathbb{R}
is equipped with its usual topology and the groups
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
,
\mathbb{Q}
are equipped with the discrete topology. All the other isomorphisms are meant in the algebraic sense.

•

The annihilator
\pi^{1}(X)^{\perp}
in (
6.4
) is taken in
\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{*}
. The group
\pi^{1}(X)
is identified here with the tensor product
\mathbb{Z}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\subseteq\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
under the usual isomorphism
\pi^{1}(X)\ni f\mapsto 1\otimes f\in\mathbb{Z}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
.

•

Finally, recall that the group
\pi^{1}(X)
is finitely generated if
X
is a compact connected manifold.

Proof of Theorem
6.12
.

Since both
\Gamma
and
X
are connected,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a torsion-free abelian group by Theorem
4.10
. Moreover, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible by virtue of Theorem
3.38
and Remark
3.39
. Also, since
\Gamma
and
X
are both locally compact and second countable,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a Polish group with the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets and it has therefore cardinality at most
\mathfrak{c}
. Finally, it follows from our assumptions on
X
that
1\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\left(\pi^{1}(X)\right)<\mathfrak{c}
. Indeed, the first inequality is secured by the assumption
\pi^{1}(X)\neq 0
and the second inequality follows since the space
X
is second countable.

If every element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is identified with its base point preserving transfer function then there are the usual isomorphisms
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)
. As observed above, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible and so it contains the divisible hull
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Moreover, there are isomorphisms

\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{Q}\otimes\left(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\pi^{1}(X)\right)\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right).

Since the group
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible by definition, it is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; its complementary summand
\mathfrak{D}
intersects
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at
0
and it is therefore a divisible subgroup of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Thus, we have

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathfrak{D}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\cong\mathfrak{D}\oplus C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right).

Now
\mathfrak{D}
is a divisible torsion-free abelian group and it is therefore a rational linear space. Write
\mathfrak{k}=\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}\left(\mathfrak{D}\right)
, we claim that
\mathfrak{k}=\mathfrak{c}
. First, since
\mathop{\rm card}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\leq\mathfrak{c}
, it follows that
\mathfrak{k}\leq\mathfrak{c}
. Further, by virtue of Theorem
6.5
, there is a monomorphism of groups
\sigma\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\mathop{\rm im}(\sigma)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Let
q
be the projection
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathfrak{D}
. Since the complementary summand
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathfrak{D}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
intersects
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at
0
, it follows that
q\sigma
is a monomorphism
\mathbb{R}\to\mathfrak{D}
. This shows that
\mathfrak{k}\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Thus, indeed,
\mathfrak{k}=\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{D}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\cong\mathbb{R}
.

Since
X
is a non-degenerate second countable continuum, we have
\mathop{\rm w}(X)=\aleph_{0}
. Consequently,
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
is a real Banach space with dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}BS}}\left(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\right)=\mathop{\rm w}(X)=\aleph_{0}
and hence its dimension as a real linear space is
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}\left(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\right)=\mathfrak{c}
(see Subsection
2.3.9
). It follows that as an abelian group,
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
.

The observations made so far may be summarized in the form of the following isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})&\oplus&\pi^{1}(X)&\cong&\\
&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\pi^{1}(X)&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\big(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)&\cong&\\
&\cong&\mathfrak{D}&\oplus&C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})&\oplus&\big(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}},\end{array}

which verify (
6.3
). Further, from (
6.3
) it follows at once that the following isomorphisms hold:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{\perp}&\cong&\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}\right)^{*}&\times&1&\times&\pi^{1}(X)^{\perp}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}\right)^{*}&\times&\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}\right)^{*}&\times&\big(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\big)^{*}&\cong&\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)_{d}^{*}.\end{array}

These isomorphisms lead to (
6.4
) with the help of the following facts:

•

\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)_{d}^{*}=\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}
; this follows from Theorem
4.5
,

•

\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)^{\perp}=F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})
; this follows from Theorem
4.7
(2),

•

\left(\mathbb{R}_{d}\right)^{*}\cong b\mathbb{R}
; this is clear.

Finally, (
6.5
) follows from (
6.3
) and from the isomorphism
(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X))/\pi^{1}(X)\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
.

Now assume that the group
\pi^{1}(X)
is finitely generated and set
k=\mathop{\rm rank}(\pi^{1}(X))
. Then the inclusion
\pi^{1}(X)\subseteq\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
takes the form
\mathbb{Z}^{k}\subseteq\mathbb{Q}^{k}
and so the isomorphisms (
6.6
)–(
6.8
) follow from the isomorphisms (
6.3
)–(
6.5
), respectively.
∎

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact connected manifold. Set
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
,
n+m=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(X))
and denote by
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
the elementary divisors of
H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})
in
H_{1}^{w}(X)
. If
\mathcal{F}
is topologically free and possesses a free cycle then there are isomorphisms of groups

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{m}&\oplus&\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{n}&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\end{array}

(6.9)

and topological isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}F(\mathop{\rm\daleth_{\mathcal{F}}})&\cong&b\mathbb{R}&\times&1&\times&\left(\mathbb{Z}^{\perp}\right)^{m}&\times&\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&b\mathbb{R}&\times&b\mathbb{R}&\times&\left(\mathbb{Q}^{*}\right)^{m}&\times&\mathbb{T}^{n}&\cong&\mathop{\rm\gimel_{\mathcal{F}}}.\end{array}

(6.10)

Also, there is an isomorphism of groups

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}.

(6.11)

Remark 6.15
.

In the isomorphisms (
6.10
) the groups
\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{T}^{n}
are equipped with their usual topologies and the group
\mathbb{Q}
carries the discrete topology. The isomorphisms (
6.9
) and (
6.11
) are meant in the algebraic sense.

Proof of Theorem
6.14
.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1st step.
We recall some facts from Theorem
4.46
and from its proof.

Write
\eta=d_{1}\dots d_{n}
. We shall use the usual identifications

H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\cong d_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus d_{n}\mathbb{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus 0\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\cong H_{1}^{w}(X),

and denote the corresponding standard basis of
H_{1}^{w}(X)
by
e_{1},\dots,e_{n+m}
. By virtue of the isomorphism (
5.6
) from the proof of Lemma
5.20
, there are maps
\xi_{i}\in\pi^{1}(X)
(
i=1,\dots,n
), whose induced morphisms
\left(\xi_{i}\right)^{\sharp}\colon H_{1}^{w}(X)\to\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
\left(\xi_{i}\right)^{\sharp}(e_{j})=\delta_{ij}(\eta/d_{j})
for
j=1,\dots,n
and
\left(\xi_{i}\right)^{\sharp}\mathbb{Z}^{m}=0
.

Given
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, the extension
\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
lifts across
\kappa_{\eta}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an extension
\mathcal{C}_{i}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. The following statements hold:

•

the extensions
\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{n}
are independent in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and so they generate a free abelian group
\mathcal{S}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{n}\rangle
with
\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathcal{S})=n
,

•

there is a direct sum
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathcal{S}
,

•

\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cap\mathcal{S}
is a free abelian group with rank
n
and the extensions
\mathcal{C}_{1}^{d_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{C}_{n}^{d_{n}}
form a basis for
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cap\mathcal{S}
.

2nd step.
We show that the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
possesses a divisible subgroup
\mathfrak{D}
such that there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathfrak{D}\oplus C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}.

(6.12)

For
i=1,\dots,n
let
\zeta_{i}
be the base point preserving transfer function for
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{d_{i}}
. Then

\mathop{\rm co}\big(\zeta_{i}^{\eta}\big)=\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta_{i})^{\eta}=\big(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{d_{i}}\big)^{\eta}=\big(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\eta}\big)^{d_{i}}=\mathop{\rm co}(\xi_{i})^{d_{i}}=\mathop{\rm co}\big(\xi_{i}^{d_{i}}\big),

and hence
\zeta_{i}^{\eta}=\xi_{i}^{d_{i}}
. It follows that the induced morphisms
(\zeta_{i})^{\sharp}\colon H_{1}^{w}(X)\to\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
(\zeta_{i})^{\sharp}(e_{j})=\delta_{ij}
for
j=1,\dots n
and
(\zeta_{i})^{\sharp}\mathbb{Z}^{m}=0
. Also, since
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\in\pi^{1}(X)
and
\pi^{1}(X)
is a direct summand in
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})
with a torsion-free complementary summand
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
, we get
\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}\in\pi^{1}(X)
. Further, by virtue of the isomorphism (
5.6
), there are maps
\vartheta_{k}\in\pi^{1}(X)
(
k=1,\dots,m
) with
(\vartheta_{k})^{\sharp}(e_{l})=\delta_{kl}
for
l=1,\dots,n+m
. By applying (
5.6
) once more we see that the maps
\vartheta_{k}
(
k=1,\dots,m
) and
\zeta_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,n
) together form a basis for the group
\pi^{1}(X)\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n+m}
. Thus, under the usual identification
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{T}^{1})\supseteq C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
, there are isomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&=C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\left\langle\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta_{1}),\dots,\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta_{m})\right\rangle\oplus\left\langle\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta_{1}),\dots,\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta_{n})\right\rangle\\
&\cong C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{m}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}.\end{split}

(6.13)

Write
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\langle\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta_{1}),\dots,\mathop{\rm co}(\vartheta_{m})\rangle
. Since
\vartheta_{k}^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0
for every
k=1,\dots,m
, it follows from Corollary
5.7
that the elements of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
lift across all
\kappa_{l}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
(
l\in\mathbb{N}
) to elements of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. In other words,
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and hence also
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{m}
, it follows that
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{m}
. Further, we have
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and, by virtue of (
6.13
), also
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\cap(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime})=0
. This means that
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is a divisible subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and hence
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathfrak{D}\oplus C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
for an appropriate divisible subgroup
\mathfrak{D}
of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Thus

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}&=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathcal{S}=\mathfrak{D}\oplus C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime})\oplus\mathcal{S}\\
&\cong\mathfrak{D}\oplus C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n},\end{split}

(6.14)

and so it remains only to show that
\mathfrak{D}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Given
0\neq\zeta\in\langle\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{n}\rangle
, we have
\zeta^{\sharp}H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})\neq 0
. By virtue of Corollary
5.7
it follows that none of the non-zero elements of
\langle\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta_{1}),\dots,\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta_{n})\rangle
is divisible in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and hence, by (
6.13
),
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cap\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
. Now let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathfrak{D}
be a non-minimal extension, we show that
\mathcal{C}
is trivial. Fix
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
\mathcal{C}^{k}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then

\mathcal{C}^{k}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cap\mathfrak{D}=(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cap\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))\cap\mathfrak{D}=(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})\oplus\mathcal{S}^{\prime})\cap\mathfrak{D}=1.

This means that
\mathcal{C}=1
, as was to be shown.

3rd step.
We determine how the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
fits into the isomorphism (
6.12
).

Recall that for every
i=1,\dots,n
,
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{d_{i}}=\mathop{\rm co}(\zeta_{i})
. Consequently, it follows from (
6.13
) and from the first line in (
6.14
) that the inclusion
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
takes the form

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})&\oplus&\mathcal{S}^{\prime}&\oplus&\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\langle\mathcal{C}_{i}^{d_{i}}\rangle&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathfrak{D}&\oplus&C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})&\oplus&(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{\prime})&\oplus&\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\langle\mathcal{C}_{i}\rangle&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}.\end{array}

This leads to the isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}&\cong&0&\oplus&C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{m}&\oplus&\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathfrak{D}&\oplus&C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{n}&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}.\end{array}

(6.15)

4th step.
We finish the proof of the theorem.

First we verify the following statements:

(
\alpha
)

\mathfrak{D}
is a rational linear space with dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(\mathfrak{D})=\mathfrak{c}
, that is,
\mathfrak{D}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\cong\mathbb{R}
,

(
\beta
)

C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
as an abelian group.

Statement (
\alpha
) follows from the following facts:

•

\mathfrak{D}
is a rational linear space; indeed, by definition, it is a divisible subgroup of a torsion-free abelian group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

•

the rational linear dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{Q}LS}}(\mathfrak{D})
of
\mathfrak{D}
is at most
\mathfrak{c}
; this follows from the fact that
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a Polish group with the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets and hence its cardinality is at most
\mathfrak{c}
,

•

the rational linear dimension of
\mathfrak{D}
is at least
\mathfrak{c}
; this follows from the following two facts:

–

first,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains a subgroup isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}
; this follows from Theorem
6.6
,

–

second, the projection
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathfrak{D}
becomes a monomorphism when restricted to any subgroup of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; this follows from the fact that the complementary summand of
\mathfrak{D}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
contains no minimal extensions whatsoever.

Statement (
\beta
) follows from the following observations:

•

C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
is a real linear space,

•

the real linear dimension
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}(C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R}))
of
C_{z}(X,\mathbb{R})
is
\mathfrak{c}
; this follows by the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
6.12
,

•

every real linear space
L
with
\mathop{\rm dim_{\mathbb{R}LS}}(L)=\mathfrak{c}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
as an abelian group.

Now the isomorphisms in (
6.9
) follow immediately from (
6.15
), (
\alpha
) and (
\beta
). The isomorphism (
6.11
) is a direct consequence of (
6.9
) and, finally, (
6.10
) follows from (
6.9
) similarly to Theorem
6.12
.
∎

For the purpose of the following corollary we fix some notation and terminology. Let

E_{1}\colon 0\longrightarrow A_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}B_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}C_{1}\longrightarrow 0\hskip 11.38109pt\text{and}\hskip 11.38109ptE_{2}\colon 0\longrightarrow A_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}B_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}C_{2}\longrightarrow 0

be extensions of abelian groups. In Subsection
2.2.8
we defined the equivalence of
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
in a strict sense which required, in particular, that
A_{1}=A_{2}
and
C_{1}=C_{2}
. We shall now relax this condition by regarding isomorphic groups as the same. Thus, we call
E_{1},E_{2}
equivalent if there exist isomorphisms of groups
h\colon A_{1}\to A_{2}
,
k\colon B_{1}\to B_{2}
and
l\colon C_{1}\to C_{2}
with
k\mu_{1}=\mu_{2}h
and
l\nu_{1}=\nu_{2}k
. Further, we call
2
-cocycles
\varphi_{1}\in Z^{2}(C_{1},A_{1})
and
\varphi_{2}\in Z^{2}(C_{2},A_{2})
equivalent if they give rise to equivalent extensions
E(\varphi_{1})
,
E(\varphi_{2})
. (Clearly, if two
2
-cocycles
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in Z^{2}(C,A)
are equivalent in a strict sense, that is, if they differ by a
2
-coboundary, then they are equivalent.) We use the same symbol
\cong
for the equivalence of extensions and
2
-cocycles. If
A,C
are abelian groups and one of them is trivial, then
\varphi_{0}(C,A)
stands for the unique element of
Z^{2}(C,A)
and
E_{0}(C,A)=E(\varphi_{0}(C,A))
stands for the extension underlying to
\varphi_{0}(C,A)
. Finally, given a minimal flow
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
, we denote by
E_{\mathcal{F}}
the usual extension

E_{\mathcal{F}}\colon 0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\longrightarrow 0

and by
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}}
a fixed element of
Z^{2}(\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
with
E(\varphi_{\mathcal{F}})\cong E_{\mathcal{F}}
.

Corollary 6.16
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.14
there are an equivalence of extensions

E_{\mathcal{F}}\cong E_{0}(\mathbb{R},0)\oplus E_{0}(0,\mathbb{R})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}E(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}E(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}})

(6.16)

and an equivalence of
2
-cocycles

\varphi_{\mathcal{F}}\cong\varphi_{0}(\mathbb{R},0)\oplus\varphi_{0}(0,\mathbb{R})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\psi\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{d_{i}},

(6.17)

where
E(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}})
,
\varphi_{d_{i}}
,
E(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
and
\psi
are given by
(
2.3
)
,
(
2.4
)
,
(
2.5
)
and
(
2.6
)
, respectively.

Remark 6.17
.

Notice that under the assumptions of Theorem
6.14
we have
m\neq 0
. This means that the extension
E_{\mathcal{F}}
does not split or, in other words, that
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is not a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. It follows also that
\varphi_{\mathcal{F}}
is not a
2
-coboundary.

Proof of Corollary
6.16
.

We recall that the notion of a direct sum of extensions and
2
-cocycles has been defined in Subsection
2.2.10
. Now, by virtue of the isomorphisms (
6.9
), there are equivalences of extensions

\begin{split}E(\varphi_{\mathcal{F}})\cong E_{\mathcal{F}}&\cong E_{0}(\mathbb{R},0)\oplus E_{0}(0,\mathbb{R})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}E(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}E(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}})\\
&\cong E(\varphi_{0}(\mathbb{R},0))\oplus E(\varphi_{0}(0,\mathbb{R}))\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}E(\psi)\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}E(\varphi_{d_{i}})\\
&\cong E\left(\varphi_{0}(\mathbb{R},0)\oplus\varphi_{0}(0,\mathbb{R})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\psi\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{d_{i}}\right).\end{split}

These equivalences yield both (
6.16
) and (
6.17
).
∎

Proposition 6.18
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal topologically free flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact connected manifold. Set
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
. If
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is equipped with the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets then there is a topological direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n},

(6.18)

where
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is the additive topological group of a real separable Banach space and the group
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
is discrete.

Proof.

By virtue of (
4.35
) from Theorem
4.46
, the direct sum (
6.18
) holds in the algebraic sense. Let the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
carry the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets. Since the group
\Gamma
is compactly generated and both
\Gamma
,
X
are locally compact second countable,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
is the additive topological group of a real separable Banach space with the norm given by (
2.32
), see Lemma
2.18
and Remark
2.19
in Subsection
2.5.11
. Let
p
be the standard covering morphism
p\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
and consider the induced morphism

\widehat{p}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})\ni\mathcal{D}\mapsto p\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}.

Then
\widehat{p}
is a morphism of topological groups. Moreover, by connectedness of both
\Gamma
and
X
,
\widehat{p}
is a monomorphism. We claim that
\widehat{p}
is in fact a topological isomorphism onto its image. So let
(\mathcal{D}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
with
p\mathcal{D}_{n}\to 1
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; we show that
\mathcal{D}_{n}\to 0
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
. To this end, fix a compact set
C\subseteq\Gamma\times X
and
0<\varepsilon<1/2
. Let
1\in K\subseteq\Gamma
be a compact connected set with
C\subseteq K\times X
. Then for all but finitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\mathcal{D}_{n}(K\times X)\subseteq p(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
. Since the sets
\mathcal{D}_{n}(K\times X)\subseteq\mathbb{R}
are all connected and contain
0
, it follows that the inclusions
\mathcal{D}_{n}(C)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{n}(K\times X)\subseteq(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
also hold for all but finitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus,
\mathcal{D}_{n}\to 0
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
, as was to be shown.

Now, given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, the following conditions are equivalent by Lemma
3.36
:

•

\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

•

for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{C}
lifts across
\kappa_{k}\colon\mathbb{T}^{1}\to\mathbb{T}^{1}
to an element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

•

the induced morphism
\mathcal{C}^{\sharp}\colon\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)\to\pi_{1}(\mathbb{T}^{1})
vanishes,

•

\mathcal{C}
lifts across
p
to and element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
.

This means that
\mathop{\rm im}(\widehat{p})=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and, since
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(\mathbb{R})
is the additive topological group of a real separable Banach space, it follows that so is the group
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

In order to finish the proof, we must show that the complementary summand
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a discrete subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and that the splitting (
6.18
) is topological. We verify both these statements at once by showing that
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is an open subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Clearly, it is sufficient to find a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
of
1
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Since
X
is a compact connected manifold and
\Gamma
is a connected Lie group, both
\pi_{1}(X)
and
\pi_{1}(\Gamma)
are finitely generated. Consequently, the group
\pi_{1}(\Gamma\times X)
is also finitely generated, say, with generators
f_{1},\dots,f_{l}
. The union of the images of
[0,1]
under the paths
f_{1},\dots,f_{l}
is a compact subset
K
of
\Gamma\times X
and hence
\mathcal{U}=[K;\mathbb{T}^{1}\setminus-1]=\{\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}:\mathcal{C}(K)\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{1}\setminus-1\}
is a neighbourhood of
1
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. If
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is an element of
\mathcal{U}
then
\mathcal{C}^{\sharp}(f_{i})=0
for every
i=1,\dots,l
, since
\mathbb{T}^{1}\setminus-1
is a contractible space, and hence
\mathcal{C}^{\sharp}=0
. Thus,
\mathcal{U}\subseteq\mathop{\rm im}(\widehat{p})=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, as was to be shown.
∎

6.3
First cohomology groups with arbitrary coefficients

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a connected group. In this section we continue our investigation from Section
6.2
. Our aim is to express the inclusions
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in terms of inclusions of familiar (topological) abelian groups and to compute the first cohomology group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
of
\mathcal{F}
with coefficients in
G^{*}
. We concentrate on the two situations (i) and (ii), which have been described at the beginning of the preceding section, see Theorems
6.23
and
6.19
, respectively. We also recall Corollary
3.41
, which states that, in case (i), the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is the additive topological group of a real separable Fréchet or Banach space. In Proposition
6.21
we show that, in case (ii), the structure of a Fréchet/Banach space is carried by the divisible subgroup
