\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, which forms a topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Before proceeding to the next theorem let us recall from Theorem
4.32
that for every
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
, the morphism
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
defined by (
4.5
) is a topological isomorphism onto its image, provided
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
are equipped with the ext-topology and
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
carries the topology of point-wise convergence.

Theorem 6.19
.

Let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be second countable and connected. Write

\mathfrak{r}=\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})\,,\hskip 8.53581pt\mathfrak{f}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}))\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\mathfrak{t}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})).

Then, under the assumptions and notation from Theorem
6.14
, the inclusion
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
takes the form

\begin{array}[]{cccccccccccc}&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{m\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&0&\oplus&(d_{1}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\dots\oplus d_{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\mathfrak{f}}&\subseteq\\
\subseteq&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}&\oplus&\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}},&\end{array}

(6.19)

and there is an isomorphism of groups

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{1}}\oplus\dots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{d_{n}}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}.

(6.20)

Moreover, the topological morphism
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
takes the form of the inclusion of topological groups

\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)&\oplus\\
\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&0&\subseteq&&\\
\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}\right)&\oplus\\
\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm tor}_{d_{i}}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G)\right),&&&\end{array}

where the direct sums and the powers are topological, the groups
\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
are equipped with the discrete topology, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
is equipped with the topology of point-wise convergence and the groups
\mathop{\rm tor}_{d_{i}}(\mathfrak{t}(G))
(
i=1,\dots,n
) carry the topology inherited from
\mathfrak{t}(G)
.

Remark 6.20
.

Notice the following facts.

•

Let us clarify the last statement of the theorem. While
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
carries the topology of point-wise convergence, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
is equipped with the subgroup topology inherited from
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
via the monomorphism

\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),q)\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})\ni h\mapsto qh\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}),

where
q\colon\mathbb{Q}^{m}\to(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}
is the canonical quotient morphism. (This is indeed a monomorphism, since the group
\ker(q)=\mathbb{Z}^{m}
is free abelian and the group
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is torsion-less.) We emphasize that this subgroup topology on
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
is in general different from the topology of point-wise convergence, see our remark following the sixth step of the proof. We also wish to mention the isomorphism (
6.27
) from the eighth step of the proof, which states that
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
is algebraically a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
with the complementary summand
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})
.

•

Assume that the group
G
is non-trivial. Then
1\leq\mathfrak{r}\leq\aleph_{0}
and we get isomorphisms of abelian groups

\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\cong\left(\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\right)^{\mathfrak{r}}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{\left(\mathfrak{c}^{\mathfrak{r}}\right)}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\cong\mathbb{R},

which apply to the isomorphisms (
6.19
) and (
6.20
). (They do not apply to the last isomorphism of the theorem, since they are not topological in general.)

•

It follows from the last statement of the theorem that
\Psi_{G}
is an isomorphism if
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})=0
, that is, if
G
is a torus. The converse implication is also true. If
\Psi_{G}
is an isomorphism then, by the first part of the remark,
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})=0
. Thus
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is a Whitehead group and, being countable, it is free abelian by the Pontryagin theorem. Since
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is also torsion-less by definition, it follows that, indeed,
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})=0
.

Proof of Theorem
6.19
.

While the isomorphisms (
6.19
) and (
6.20
) are meant only in algebraic sense, the last isomorphism describes an inclusion of topological groups. (The groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
carry the ext-topology and the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is equipped with the topology of point-wise convergence.) When dealing with topological isomorphisms, in particular, when proving this last isomorphism of the theorem, we shall follow the following rules:

•

all
\mathop{\rm Hom}
groups carry the topology of point-wise convergence,

•

all direct sums are topological,

•

all powers are topological,

•

the groups
\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{Z}
carry the discrete topology.

We divide the proof into several short steps.

1st step.
We recall some properties of the functor
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},-)
, which we shall use later.

For
k=1,\dots,n
let
A_{k}\in\mathsf{DAGp}
and
B_{k}\mathrel{\lhd}A_{k}
. Set
A=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}A_{k}
and
B=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}B_{k}
. Then
B
is a subgroup of
A
,
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B)
is a closed subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A)
and there are topological isomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B)\cong\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{k})\subseteq\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{k})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A).

Consequently, there is a topological isomorphism

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A)/\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B)\cong\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}\Big(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{k})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{k})\Big).

2nd step.
We show that there is an isomorphism of groups
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{R})\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}
.

Since the group
G^{*}
is torsion-free and the group
\mathbb{R}
is divisible, every element
h
of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{R})
extends uniquely to an element
h^{\prime}
of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q}\otimes G^{*},\mathbb{R})
. Moreover,
\mathbb{Q}\otimes G^{*}
is a rational linear space with dimension
\mathfrak{r}
and so it is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{r})}
. This leads to isomorphisms of groups

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{R}\right)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes G^{*},\mathbb{R}\right)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{r})},\mathbb{R}\right)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{R})^{\mathfrak{r}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}.

(6.21)

One verifies that all the isomorphisms in (
6.21
) are in fact topological and so the isomorphism
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{R})\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}
is also topological.

3rd step.
We describe the inclusion
\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)
up to isomorphism.

Recall the direct sum
G^{*}=\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})\oplus\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
. The group
\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})
is free abelian with rank
\mathfrak{f}
and so it is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{(\mathfrak{f})}
. The group
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is torsion-less and hence it satisfies
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})=0
. Consequently, there are isomorphisms of groups

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{m})&=\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})\oplus\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\mathfrak{f})},\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)\cong\left(\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{m\mathfrak{f}}.\end{split}

(6.22)

In a similar way (and with the help of the ideas used to verify (
6.21
)) we obtain isomorphisms of groups

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)&=\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})\oplus\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Q}\otimes\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\mathfrak{f})},\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{t})},\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)\\
&\cong\left(\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)^{\mathfrak{t}}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}.\end{split}

(6.23)

The isomorphisms (
6.22
) and (
6.23
) now yield the following form of the inclusion
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{m})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}^{m})
:

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)\cong\mathbb{Z}^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus 0\subseteq\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right).

4th step.
We describe the inclusion
\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
up to isomorphism.

By using the same ideas as in (
6.22
), one puts the considered inclusion at once into the following form:

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)\cong\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\subseteq\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},Z^{n}\right).

5th step.
We verify the isomorphisms (
6.19
) and (
6.20
).

By virtue of Corollary
3.8
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
corresponds to
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})
under the isomorphism
\Phi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Consequently, the inclusion (
6.19
) follows at once from the first four steps of the proof and from the inclusion (
6.9
) in Theorem
6.14
. The isomorphism (
6.20
) is a direct consequence of (
6.19
).

6th step.
We describe our strategy for proving the last statement of the theorem.

Under the usual identifications

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}),

the morphism
\Psi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
acts by the rule

\Psi_{G}\colon\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})}\ni\varphi+\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\mapsto\pi\varphi\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}),

(6.24)

where
\pi
is the quotient morphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\varphi\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Recall that the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
carries the discrete topology, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is equipped with the topology of point-wise convergence and
\Psi_{G}
is a topological isomorphism onto its image by virtue of Theorem
4.32
.

Our aim now is to determine what form the inclusion (
6.24
) takes, when the inclusion
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is expressed in the form of (
6.9
) and, correspondingly, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}
takes the form expressed in (
6.11
). Write
A_{1}=A_{2}=\mathbb{R}
,
A_{3}=\mathbb{Q}^{m}
,
A_{4}=\mathbb{Z}^{n}
,
B_{1}=0
,
B_{2}=\mathbb{R}
,
B_{3}=\mathbb{Z}^{m}
and
B_{4}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}
. For
1\leq i\leq 4
let
\pi_{i}
be the quotient morphism
A_{i}\to A_{i}/B_{i}
. Then by virtue of Theorem
6.14
, the morphism
\pi
takes the form of the direct sum of the morphisms
\pi_{i}
:

\pi=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\pi_{i}\colon\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}A_{i}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}A_{i}/B_{i}.

Consequently, the expression of the morphism
\Psi_{G}
from (
6.24
) changes to

\Psi_{G}\colon\frac{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i})}{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i})}{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i}/B_{i})

and
\Psi_{G}
now acts by the rule

\Psi_{G}\colon\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\left(h_{i}+\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})\right)\mapsto\bigoplus_{i=1}^{4}\pi_{i}h_{i}

for
h_{i}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i})
(
1\leq i\leq 4
). Thus
\Psi_{G}
is a direct sum of the morphisms

q_{i}\colon\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i})}{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})}\ni h_{i}+\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})\mapsto\pi_{i}h_{i}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i}/B_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq 4
and we need to determine them all. Observe that all the morphisms
q_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq 4
) are monomorphisms.

Remark.
Before proceeding further we wish to make some observations. First we recall that all the groups
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
carry the discrete topology and the quotient groups
A_{i}/B_{i}
are thus also discrete. Further, the groups
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i}/B_{i})
are equipped with the topology of point-wise convergence. Finally, since
\Psi_{G}
is a topological isomorphism onto its image, we must consider on the quotient group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})
the subgroup topology inherited from
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i}/B_{i})
via the monomorphism
q_{i}
. We would like to emphasize that this inherited topology need not coincide with the quotient topology on
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{i})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},B_{i})
. Indeed, as an example consider the morphism

q\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Z})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q})\ni h\mapsto ph\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}),

where
p
is the quotient morphism
\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
. Then
q
is not a topological isomorphism onto its image if
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q})
is equipped with the topology of point-wise convergence. For if
h_{n}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q})
is given by the rule
h_{n}(x)=(n!)x
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then the sequence
(q(h_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to zero in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
but the sequence
(h_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
does not converge to zero in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q})
.

7th step.
We determine the morphisms
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
.

Since
A_{1}=\mathbb{R}
and
B_{1}=0
, the morphism
q_{1}
turns at once into the identity on
\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{R}\right)
. Moreover, in the second step of the proof we have shown that the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{R})
is topologically isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}
. Thus, we have an equivalence

q_{1}\cong\mathop{\rm Id}\colon\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\to\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}.

(6.25)

Also, since
A_{2}=B_{2}=\mathbb{R}
,
q_{2}
is equivalent to the unique endomorphism of the trivial group
0
.

8th step.
We determine the morphism
q_{3}
.

Here
A_{3}=\mathbb{Q}^{m}
and
B_{3}=\mathbb{Z}^{m}
. From the direct sum
G^{*}=\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})\oplus\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
it follows that the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{3}/B_{3})
is topologically isomorphic to the topological direct sum
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),A_{3}/B_{3})\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),A_{3}/B_{3})
and the morphism
q_{3}
takes the form of a direct sum
q_{3}\cong q_{3}^{(f)}\oplus q_{3}^{(t)}
of the morphisms

q_{3}^{(f)}\colon\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)}{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)}\ni h+\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\mapsto\pi_{3}h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}\right)

and

q_{3}^{(t)}\colon\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m}\right)}{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)}\ni k+\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\mapsto\pi_{3}k\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}\right).

First we determine the morphism
q_{3}^{(f)}
. Similarly to the third step of the proof one verifies that the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
is topologically isomorphic to
\left((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\cong(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}
. Moreover, since
\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})
is a free abelian group, it satisfies
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})=0
. Consequently, the covariant Hom-Ext sequence derived from the sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{m}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}^{m}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m}\longrightarrow 0

(6.26)

and associated to the group
\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})
takes the form of the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})\longrightarrow 0.

This shows that
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\pi_{3})
is an epimorphism with the kernel
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})
and so
q_{3}^{(f)}
is an isomorphism. Thus, as a topological morphism,
q_{3}^{(f)}
is equivalent to the identity on
(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}
.

Now we determine the morphism
q_{3}^{(t)}
. First, since the group
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is torsion-less, we have
\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)=0
, which means that
q_{3}^{(t)}\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\pi_{3})
represents the natural inclusion
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
. Further, since
\mathbb{Q}^{m}
is a divisible group, the group of extensions
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
vanishes. Consequently, the covariant Hom-Ext sequence derived from (
6.26
) and associated to the group
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
takes the form of the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q_{3}^{(t)}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\longrightarrow 0.

It follows that the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
is an extension of
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})
by
\mathop{\rm im}(q_{3}^{(t)})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
. Since the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})\cong\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}
is divisible and hence injective, it is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
and we thus obtain the following isomorphisms of abelian groups:

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})&=\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})\oplus\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})\\
&\cong\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}\oplus\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m}).\end{split}

(6.27)

9th step.
We determine the morphism
q_{4}
.

Here we have
A_{4}=\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
B_{4}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}
. Similarly to the eighth step of the proof one verifies that the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},A_{4}/B_{4})
is topologically isomorphic to the topological direct sum
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),A_{4}/B_{4})\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),A_{4}/B_{4})
and that the morphism
q_{4}
takes the form of a direct sum
q_{4}\cong q_{4}^{(f)}\oplus q_{4}^{(t)}
of the natural morphisms

q_{4}^{(f)}\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{n})/\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)

and

q_{4}^{(t)}\colon\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{n})/\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right).

In order to determine the morphism
q_{4}^{(f)}
, one can use the same ideas as those used to determine
q_{3}^{(f)}
in the eighth step of the proof. Indeed, since the group
\mathfrak{f}(G^{*})
is free abelian, we have
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z})=0
. It follows that
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\pi_{4})
is an epimorphism onto
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}})
with the kernel
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z})
and hence
q_{4}^{(f)}
is an isomorphism. Thus, as a morphism of topological groups,
q_{4}^{(f)}
is equivalent to the identity on

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\mathfrak{f})},\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)\cong\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}.

Now we determine the morphism
q_{4}^{(t)}
. The domain of
q_{4}^{(t)}
is the trivial group
0
, since
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is a torsion-less group. The co-domain
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}})
of
q_{4}^{(t)}
is obtained from the following topological isomorphisms:

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)&\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}},\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})^{*}\right)\\
&\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{d_{i}}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})^{*})\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\text{tor}_{d_{i}}(\mathfrak{t}(G)).\end{split}

10th step.
We finish the proof of the theorem by verifying its last statement.

The last statement of the theorem follows at once from the previous steps
6-9
.
∎

Proposition 6.21
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal topologically free flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
connected and with
X
a compact manifold, and let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be connected second countable. Set
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
and
\mathfrak{f}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}))
. If
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is equipped with the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets then there is a topological direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}},

(6.28)

where
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
is the additive topological group of a real separable Fréchet space,
\mathbb{Z}
is a discrete group and
\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}}
carries the product topology. If the group
G
is additionally assumed finite-dimensional then
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
is in fact the additive topological group of a real (separable) Banach space.

Remark 6.22
.

Notice the following facts.

•

The group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is connected if and only if either
n=0
or
\mathfrak{f}=0
. The first condition means that
H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F})=0
. The second condition means that
G
is a torus-free group, that is, the maximal torus of
G
is trivial.

•

Let the group
G
be finite-dimensional and torus-free (say, a solenoid). While it is not at all apparent from the definition of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
that it is a real separable Banach space, the proposition states that this is indeed the case. In particular, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is arc-wise connected also when the group
G
is not.

Proof of Proposition
6.21
.

Before turning to the proof we wish to mention that, similarly to the proof of Theorem
6.19
, all
\mathop{\rm Hom}
groups are assumed to carry the topology of point-wise convergence. Now, in our proof of the isomorphism (
6.28
) we shall need the following facts:

(
\alpha
)

there is a topological direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n},

where
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is the additive topological group of a real separable Banach space and the group
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
is discrete; this follows from Proposition
6.18
,

(
\beta
)

the topological isomorphism
\Phi_{G}
from Theorem
3.1
restricts to an isomorphism

\Phi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}));

indeed, since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free, we have

\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})),

(
\gamma
)

there is a topological isomorphism
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}}
; this follows from the topological isomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{n})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{(\mathfrak{f})},\mathbb{Z}^{n})\cong\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}}.

From statement (
\alpha
) it follows that
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
is a topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and that
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
is its complementary topological direct summand. By virtue of (
\beta
) and (
\gamma
), this translates into the topological direct sum (
6.28
).

Further, since
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is the additive topological group of a real separable Banach space and the group
G^{*}
is countable, it follows from the first part of Lemma
2.1
that
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
is the additive topological group of a real separable Fréchet space. If the group
G
is finite-dimensional then
\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})=\dim(G)
is finite and so
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
is a real separable Banach space by the second part of Lemma
2.1
.
∎

Theorem 6.23
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow with
\Gamma\in\mathsf{LieGp}
simply connected and with
X
compact. Assume that the space
X
is second countable and
\pi^{1}(X)\neq 0
. Let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a connected group and write
\mathfrak{r}=\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})
. Then there is an isomorphism of groups

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&\cong&0&\oplus&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G).\end{array}

Further, there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)=\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X))}{\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))}\oplus\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right).

(6.29)

Finally, there are isomorphisms of groups

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)}{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\frac{\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)}{\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)}.

Remark 6.24
.

We wish to mention the following facts.

•

If the group
G
is non-trivial second countable then
1\leq\mathfrak{r}\leq\aleph_{0}
and hence, similarly to Remark
6.20
, we have an isomorphism of abelian groups
\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\cong\mathbb{R}
.

•

Assume that the group
\pi^{1}(X)
is finitely generated with rank
m
and that the group
G^{*}
is torsion-less. (This is the case, for instance, if
X
is a manifold and
G
is a solenoid.) Then
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))=0
and

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X))&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}^{m})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q}\otimes G^{*},\mathbb{Q}^{m})\\
&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{r})},\mathbb{Q}^{m})\cong(\mathbb{Q}^{m})^{\mathfrak{r}}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{r}}.\end{split}

Thus, by virtue of the last statement of the theorem, there is an isomorphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{r}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}
.

•

Let the group
\pi^{1}(X)
be divisible (say, let
X=\mathbb{Q}^{*}
be the universal metrizable solenoid). Then
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)=\pi^{1}(X)
and hence, by the last statement of the theorem,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}
.

•

Assume that
G=\mathbb{T}^{\mathfrak{r}}
is a torus. Then
G^{*}
is free abelian and so
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X))=0
. Moreover,

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X))&\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathbb{Z}^{(\mathfrak{r})},(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X))\\
&\cong\left((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{\mathfrak{r}}.\end{split}

Consequently, by the last statement of the theorem,

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\left((\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)^{\mathfrak{r}}.

Proof of Theorem
6.23
.

The first isomorphism of the theorem follows from (
6.3
) in the same way as (
6.19
) has been obtained from (
6.9
) in Theorem
6.19
.

To verify the second statement of the theorem, consider the injective resolution of
\pi^{1}(X)

0\longrightarrow\pi^{1}(X)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\longrightarrow(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\longrightarrow 0.

Since the group
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
is divisible, we have
\mathop{\rm Ext}(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X))=0
and so the derived covariant Hom-Ext sequence associated to
G^{*}
becomes

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)\longrightarrow\\
&\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\otimes\pi^{1}(X)\right)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(G^{*},\pi^{1}(X)\right)\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(6.30)

To simplify notation, write (
6.30
) as

0\longrightarrow A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\longrightarrow}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma}}{{\longrightarrow}}D\longrightarrow 0.

By exactness of (
6.30
), we have

(a)

\mathop{\rm im}(\beta)\cong B/\ker(\beta)=B/\mathop{\rm im}(\alpha)=B/A
, and

(b)

D\cong C/\ker(\gamma)=C/\mathop{\rm im}(\beta)
.

Since the group
\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X)
is divisible torsion-free, it follows that the group
B=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Q}\otimes\pi^{1}(X))
is divisible. Consequently, the group
\mathop{\rm im}(\beta)
is also divisible and hence it is a direct summand in
C
. By virtue of (b),
D
is a complementary summand of
\mathop{\rm im}(\beta)
in
C
and hence, by virtue of (a), there is a direct sum

C\cong\mathop{\rm im}(\beta)\oplus D\cong(B/A)\oplus D.

This is precisely the desired isomorphism (
6.29
). The last statement of the theorem now follows from the first one and from (
6.29
).
∎

6.4
More on first cohomology groups

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow. In this section we continue our study of the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
under the assumptions of Theorem
6.14
; that is, we assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a topologically free flow possessing a free cycle. Being inspired by our results from Theorem
4.39
, which are valid for flows
\mathcal{F}
with simply connected acting groups
\Gamma
, we focus our concentration on how the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
are situated in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, respectively. Unlike in Theorem
4.39
, neither
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
nor
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible under the assumptions of Theorem
6.14
in general, but the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
still turn out to form direct summands in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, respectively; this is proved in Theorem
6.25
. Moreover, just like in Theorem
4.39
, the complementary summands to
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
can be chosen in such a way that all their elements
\mathcal{C}
have connected sections
F(\mathcal{C})
. Finally, we close this section with Theorem
6.28
, where we show that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and that its closure
\overline{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}
is a topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
; we also determine the corresponding complementary (topological) direct summands.

Before turning to the mentioned results, we describe one necessary construction. Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
. The key starting point of our construction is the following assumption:

(a)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Having (a) at our disposal, we proceed in the following steps.

(i)

Fix a complementary summand
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
F(\mathcal{D})=\mathbb{T}^{1}
for every
1\neq\mathcal{D}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Thus, every element
\mathcal{D}
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}
has a connected section
F(\mathcal{D})
.

(ii)

Recall that
\Phi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is an isomorphism of groups. Moreover, given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the group
F(\mathcal{C})
is totally disconnected if and only if all the groups
\chi F(\mathcal{C})=F(\chi\mathcal{C})
(
\chi\in G^{*}
) are totally disconnected. Consequently,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\Phi_{G}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
.

(iii)

Set
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\Phi_{G}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
. Given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, the group
F(\mathcal{C})
is connected if and only if all the groups
\chi F(\mathcal{C})=F(\chi\mathcal{C})
(
\chi\in G^{*}
) are connected. Consequently, every extension
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
has a connected section
F(\mathcal{C})
.

(iv)

The first three steps of our construction now lead to the direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G).

(v)

Let
\pi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
be the canonical quotient morphism. Set
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\pi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\pi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
. Since
\ker(\pi_{G})=\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, it follows that the restriction
\pi_{G}\colon\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\to\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is an isomorphism and we have a direct sum

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G).

We now apply this construction to the situation of Theorem
6.14
.

Theorem 6.25
.

Let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be second countable and connected. Write

\mathfrak{r}=\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})\,,\hskip 8.53581pt\mathfrak{f}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathfrak{f}(G^{*}))\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\mathfrak{t}=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})).

Then, under the assumptions and notation from Theorem
6.14
, the following statements hold:

(a)

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

(b)

there is a direct sum

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&=&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&\oplus&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\\
&\cong&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}},\end{array}

(6.31)

(c)

there is a direct sum

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&=&\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&\oplus&\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\\
&\cong&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}.\end{array}

(6.32)

Remark 6.26
.

If the acting group
\Gamma
of a flow
\mathcal{F}
is a simply connected Lie group and the phase space
X
of
\mathcal{F}
is compact then the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is divisible. By Lemma
4.38
, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is also divisible and hence it is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Consequently, the assumption (a) above is satisfied and the procedure described in steps (i)-(v) applies (we have seen this already in Theorem
4.39
). In the situation of Theorem
6.25
one can not apply the mentioned results from Chapter
4
, for neither
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
nor
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a divisible group in general. Nevertheless, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is still a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Proof of Theorem
6.25
.

By virtue of (
6.9
), there is an isomorphism

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong 0\oplus\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}.

Thus,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
with a complementary summand

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus 0\oplus 0\oplus 0\cong\mathbb{R},

which verifies statement (a).

By using step (ii) above and by applying the isomorphism
\Phi_{G}
, the inclusion
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
translates into the inclusion
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. The isomorphism (
6.9
) and the results from the proof of Theorem
6.19
thus lead to the isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccc}\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)&\cong&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&0&\oplus&\mathbb{Z}^{m\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}&\subseteq&\\
&\subseteq&\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}&\oplus&\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{f}}&\oplus&\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}&\cong&\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G).\end{array}

This yields, on one hand,

\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n\mathfrak{f}}

(6.33)

and, on the other hand,

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{td}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)/\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathbb{Q}^{m\mathfrak{t}}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m\mathfrak{f}}\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{d_{i}}\right)^{\mathfrak{f}}.

(6.34)

Also, by virtue of steps (iii) and (v) above,

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}^{cn}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{R})\cong\mathbb{R}^{\mathfrak{r}}.

(6.35)

Now, statement (b) of the theorem follows from step (iv) and from the isomorphisms (
6.33
) and (
6.35
). Similarly, statement (c) follows from step (v) and from the isomorphisms (
6.34
) and (
6.35
).
∎

Before turning to the next auxiliary result, we wish to make some observations. Consider the injective resolution of the group
\mathbb{Z}
:

0\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\longrightarrow 0.

(6.36)

Given an abelian group
D
, we have
\mathop{\rm Ext}(D,\mathbb{Q})=0
by divisibility of
\mathbb{Q}
, and so the covariant Hom-Ext sequence derived from (
6.36
) and associated to the group
D
becomes

0\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathop{\rm Ext}(D,\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow 0,

(6.37)

where
\widehat{j}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,j)
,
\widehat{p}=\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,p)
and
\epsilon
is the connecting morphism. It follows from the exactness of (
6.37
) that there are isomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})&\supseteq\mathop{\rm im}(\widehat{p})\cong\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\ker(\widehat{p})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm im}(\widehat{j})\\
&=\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Z}).\end{split}

Thus the quotient group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Z})
is naturally identified with the subgroup
\mathop{\rm im}(\widehat{p})
of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
and we shall use this identification in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.27
.

Let
D
be a countable torsion-free abelian group. Equip the groups
\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
with the discrete topology and the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
with the topology of point-wise convergence. Then
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Z})
is a dense subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
. Moreover, the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Z})
is a closed subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
,

(b)

\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Z})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
,

(c)

\mathop{\rm Ext}(D,\mathbb{Z})=0
,

(d)

D
is free abelian, that is,
\mathfrak{t}(D)=0
.

Proof.

We begin by verifying the density of
\mathop{\rm im}(\widehat{p})
in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
. So let
0\neq F\subseteq D
be a finite set and fix
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
; we need to show that there is
k\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(D,\mathbb{Q})
such that
pk
and
h
agree on
F
. Since
D
is a torsion-free group, the subgroup
\langle F\rangle
of
D
generated by
F
is free abelian with a finite rank. Let
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
be a basis for
\langle F\rangle
. For
i=1,\dots,n
let
q_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}
be such that
p(q_{i})=h(d_{i})
and denote by
k_{0}
the morphism
\langle F\rangle\to\mathbb{Q}
with
k_{0}(d_{i})=q_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,n
). By divisibility of
\mathbb{Q}
,
k_{0}
extends to a morphism
k\colon D\to\mathbb{Q}
. Clearly,
pk
coincides with
h
on
\langle F\rangle
and hence also on
F
.

We verify the equivalence of the conditions (a)–(d). First, conditions (a), (b) are equivalent by virtue of the first statement of the theorem. Further, the equivalence of (b) and (c) follows from the exactness of the sequence (
6.37
). Finally, the equivalence of (c) and (d) follows from the Pontryagin theorem, which states that in the class of countable groups
D
, the Whitehead groups coincide with the free abelian groups.
∎

Theorem 6.28
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
6.14
be fulfilled and let
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be connected second countable. Then, under the identification
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\cong\mathop{\rm im}(\Psi_{G})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in the algebraic sense and there is a direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\cong\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\oplus\mathop{\rm Ext}\left(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m}\right)\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{d_{i}}(\mathfrak{t}(G))\right).

(6.38)

Moreover, the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

(2)

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a closed subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

(3)

\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
coincides with
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

(4)

G^{*}
is a free abelian group, that is,
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})=0
,

(5)

G
is a torus.

Finally, the closure
\overline{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}
of
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is a topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and there is a topological direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\cong\overline{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}\oplus\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\text{$\mathop{\rm tor}$}_{d_{i}}(\mathfrak{t}(G))\right).

(6.39)

Remark 6.29
.

Notice the following facts.

•

It follows from our proof of the theorem that the isomorphisms (
6.38
) and (
6.39
) are compatible in the sense that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a direct summand in
\overline{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}
in the algebraic sense and its complementary summand is
\mathop{\rm Ext}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z}^{m})
.

•

By virtue of (
6.39
),
\overline{\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)}
is a co-compact subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. It also follows from (
6.39
) that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
if and only if the group
\mathfrak{t}(G)
contains no torsion elements of orders dividing
d=d_{n}
.

Proof of Theorem
6.28
.

The first statement of the theorem and the algebraic splitting (
6.38
) follow at once from the last statement of Theorem
6.19
and from the first part of Remark
6.20
.

We verify the equivalence of conditions (1)–(5). First, the relations (3)
\Rightarrow
(1) and (4)
\Leftrightarrow
(5) are clear. Further, (2) follows from (1) at once, for a topological direct summand is necessarily closed. The implication (4)
\Rightarrow
(3) is a direct consequence of the last statement of Theorem
6.19
. Now it suffices to show that (4) follows from (2). So assume that
\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is closed in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf H}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Then, by virtue of Theorem
6.19
,
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
is closed in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
and hence
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q})/\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Z})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q})
is closed in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
. It follows from Lemma
6.27
that
\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
is a free abelian group, that is,
0=\mathfrak{t}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}))=\mathfrak{t}(G^{*})
. This verifies condition (4).

We finish the proof of the theorem by verifying the topological direct sum (
6.39
). By virtue of Lemma
6.27
,
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),\mathbb{Q}^{m})
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(\mathfrak{t}(G^{*}),(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{m})
. Thus, (
6.39
) follows at once from the last statement of Theorem
6.19
.
∎

6.5
Dense groups of minimal extensions

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a connected second countable group. In this section we study density properties of the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. First, in Theorem
6.30
we show that under mild assumptions on
\mathcal{F}
, the minimal extensions from
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
form a dense
G_{\delta}
subset of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Then, in Theorem
6.32
and Remark
6.33
we present necessary and sufficient conditions, under which a given abelian group
R
can be embedded into
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
in such a way that it forms a dense subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
or
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
.

Theorem 6.30
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a connected second countable group. Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

(1)

\Gamma
is a simply connected Lie group and
X
is a compact second countable space with
\pi^{1}(X)\neq 0
,

(2)

\Gamma
is a connected Lie group,
X
is a compact manifold, the flow
\mathcal{F}
is topologically free and possesses a free cycle.

Then the minimal elements of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
form a dense
G_{\delta}
subset of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Remark 6.31
.

We wish to add the following remarks.

•

As usual, the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is equipped with the topology of u.c.s. convergence. By our topological assumptions on
\Gamma
,
X
and
G
,
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is a Polish group and hence a Baire space. Thus, it follows from the theorem that a generic element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is minimal.

•

It follows from
[
14
]
and
[
40
]
that the minimal extensions are generic if
\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}
or
\Gamma=\mathbb{R}
. In case when the group
\Gamma
is amenable locally compact the minimal extensions are also generic, this follows from
[
9
, Theorem 6]
.

•

When proving results of this type one usually proceeds by expressing the set of minimal extensions as a countable intersection of sets, each of which one shows to be open and dense. Our results proved so far allow us to use a different approach - we proceed by verifying both the density and the
G_{\delta}
property separately. That is, first we check that the minimal extensions form a set of type
G_{\delta}
. Then, arbitrarily close to any prescribed extension in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, we construct (without using any limit argument) one concrete minimal element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Proof of Theorem
6.30
.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1st step.
We show that the set
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus e
of the minimal elements of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is of type
G_{\delta}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Fix
z\in X
, a countable local base
(U_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
at
z
in
X
and a countable basis
(V_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
for the topology of
G
. Given
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
, denote by
\mathcal{M}_{m,n}
the set of all
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
such that there is
\gamma\in\Gamma
with
T_{\gamma}z\in U_{m}
and
\mathcal{C}(\gamma,z)\in V_{n}
. Then all the sets
\mathcal{M}_{m,n}
are open in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Furthermore, given
\mathcal{C}\in\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
, we have
F(\mathcal{C})=G
if and only if
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{M}_{m,n}
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus, indeed,
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)\setminus e=\bigcap_{m,n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{M}_{m,n}
is of type
G_{\delta}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

2nd step.
We recall some useful facts that we shall make use of in the final step of the proof.

First, by virtue Theorem
3.1
and Corollary
3.8
, there is a topological isomorphism between
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, under which the minimal elements of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
correspond to the monomorphisms
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. As usual, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is assumed to carry the topology of point-wise convergence.

Second, by virtue of (
6.3
) from Theorem
6.12
and (
6.9
) from Theorem
6.14
, in both cases (1) and (2) of the theorem there is a direct sum
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=A\oplus B
such that
A
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
as an abelian group and
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq B
. We let
q
denote the projection morphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to A
.

Third, since both
\Gamma
and
X
are connected, the groups
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
are torsion-free by virtue of Theorem
4.10
.

3rd step.
We show that the monomorphisms
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
form a dense subset of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. According to the second step, this will finish the proof of the theorem.

Fix
h\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, a finite set
F\subseteq G^{*}
and, for every
\chi\in F
, a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{\chi}
of
h(\chi)
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; we need to construct
k\in\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
with the following properties:

(a)

h+k
is a monomorphism,

(b)

h+k
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

(c)

(h+k)(\chi)\in\mathcal{U}_{\chi}
for every
\chi\in F
.

Consider the divisible hull
D=\mathbb{Q}\otimes qh(G^{*})
of
qh(G^{*})
. Since the group
A\supseteq qh(G^{*})
is divisible, it follows that
D\subseteq A
and hence
D
is a direct summand in
A
. That is,
A=A^{\prime}\oplus D
for an appropriate subgroup
A^{\prime}
of
A
. Since both
A
and
D
are divisible torsion-free groups, it follows that so is
A^{\prime}
. Moreover, we have
\mathop{\rm rank}(A)=\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathop{\rm rank}(D)\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})\leq\mathop{\rm card}(G^{*})\leq\aleph_{0}
, and hence
\mathop{\rm rank}(A^{\prime})=\mathfrak{c}
. Thus,
A^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
as an abelian group. We let
q^{\prime}
stand for the projection morphism
A\to A^{\prime}
.

Let
(\Upsilon_{i})_{i\in I}
be a maximal independent family in
G^{*}
. Then there exist a finite set
J\subseteq I
and integers
l_{\chi}
,
l_{\chi,j}
(
j\in J
,
\chi\in F
) such that for every
\chi\in F
,
l_{\chi}\chi=\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi,j}\Upsilon_{j}
. By applying the morphism
h
, we get
\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi,j}h(\Upsilon_{j})=l_{\chi}h(\chi)\in l_{\chi}\mathcal{U}_{\chi}
for every
\chi\in F
. Consequently, there is an identity neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
such that for every
\chi\in F
,

\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi,j}\left(h(\Upsilon_{j})+\mathcal{U}\right)\subseteq l_{\chi}\mathcal{U}_{\chi}.

(6.40)

Now, since
\mathop{\rm card}(I)=\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})\leq\aleph_{0}
and
A^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{R}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}
, there is a direct sum
A^{\prime}=\mathbb{Q}^{(J)}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{(I\setminus J)}\oplus C
for an appropriate subgroup
C
of
A^{\prime}
. Regarding
\mathbb{Q}^{(J)}
and
\mathbb{Q}^{(I\setminus J)}
as rational linear spaces, let
(s_{j})_{j\in J}
and
(t_{i})_{i\in I\setminus J}
be their respective bases. We have clearly
\mathbb{Q}^{(J)}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, where the latter group is a separable Banach space by virtue of Corollary
3.41
and Proposition
6.18
. Thus, by finiteness of
J
, there is
n\in\mathbb{N}
with
(1/n)s_{j}\in\mathcal{U}
for every
j\in J
. Denote by
k
the morphism

k\colon G^{*}\to\mathbb{Q}^{(J)}\oplus\mathbb{Q}^{(I\setminus J)}\subseteq A^{\prime}\subseteq A\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}

with
k(\Upsilon_{j})=(1/n)s_{j}
for
j\in J
and
k(\Upsilon_{i})=t_{i}
for
i\in I\setminus J
. By our choice of
(\Upsilon_{i})_{i\in I}
, such a morphism indeed exists and is unique. In fact,
k
is a monomorphism.

3rd step continued.
We finish the proof of the theorem by showing that
k
satisfies the properties (a), (b), (c) formulated above.

First, to see that
h+k
is a monomorphism, fix
\lambda\in G^{*}
with
(h+k)(\lambda)=0
. Since
qh(\lambda)\in D
, we have
q^{\prime}qh(\lambda)=0
. Since
k(\lambda)\in A^{\prime}
, we have
q^{\prime}qk(\lambda)=k(\lambda)
. Consequently,
0=q^{\prime}q(h+k)(\lambda)=q^{\prime}qh(\lambda)+q^{\prime}qk(\lambda)=k(\lambda)
. Since
k
is a monomorphism,
\lambda=0
, as was to be shown.

Further, to see that
h+k
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, fix
\lambda\in G^{*}
and assume that
(h+k)(\lambda)
is a non-minimal element of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
; we show that
\lambda=0
. Then
d(h+k)(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for an appropriate positive integer
d
. By applying the morphism
q^{\prime}q
we obtain, in the same way as above,

dk(\lambda)=q^{\prime}q\left(d(h+k)(\lambda)\right)\in q^{\prime}q\left(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\right)\subseteq q^{\prime}q(B)=q^{\prime}(0)=0.

Since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free by the second step of the proof, it follows that
k(\lambda)=0
. This implies
\lambda=0
, for
k
is a monomorphism.

Finally, we need to check that
(h+k)(\chi)\in\mathcal{U}_{\chi}
for every
\chi\in F
. So let
\chi\in F
. Then, by virtue of (
6.40
),

\begin{split}l_{\chi}(h+k)(\chi)&=(h+k)(l_{\chi}\chi)=(h+k)\left(\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi,j}\Upsilon_{j}\right)=\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi,j}\left(h(\Upsilon_{j})+k(\Upsilon_{j})\right)\\
&=\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi_{j}}\left(h(\Upsilon_{j})+\frac{1}{n}s_{j}\right)\in\sum_{j\in J}l_{\chi,j}\left(h(\Upsilon_{j})+\mathcal{U}\right)\subseteq l_{\chi}\mathcal{U}_{\chi}.\end{split}

Since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free, this verifies condition (c).
∎

Theorem 6.32
.

Let
\mathop{\rm\mathcal{F}\colon\Gamma\curvearrowright X}
be a minimal flow and
G\in\mathsf{CAGp}
be a non-trivial connected second countable group. Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

(1)

\Gamma
is a simply connected Lie group and
X
is a compact second countable space with
\pi^{1}(X)\neq 0
,

(2)

\Gamma
is a connected Lie group,
X
is a compact manifold, the flow
\mathcal{F}
is topologically free and possesses a free cycle.

Given a torsion-free abelian group
R
with
\aleph_{0}\leq\mathop{\rm rank}(R)\leq\mathfrak{c}
, the following statements hold:

(i)

the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of the group
R
, which forms a dense subset of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
,

(ii)

the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of the group
R\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{(\aleph_{0})}
, which forms a dense subset of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.

Remark 6.33
.

We wish to mention the following facts.

•

Under the assumption (1), the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible by virtue of Remark
3.39
and Theorem
4.14
, and so every group
R
as above has an isomorphic copy within
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
in such a way that it forms a dense set in the whole group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. Under the assumption (2), the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is in general not divisible. Thus, if the group
R
is divisible then each of its isomorphic copies within
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
can be dense only in
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
. (Recall from Proposition
6.21
that
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))
is a topological direct summand in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
and hence it is a closed subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
.)

•

By virtue of statement (ii), the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
contains an isomorphic copy of
\mathbb{Z}^{(\aleph_{0})}
, which is dense in the whole group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. This is no longer true if the group
\mathbb{Z}^{(\aleph_{0})}
is replaced by a finitely generated free abelian group. To demonstrate this, take
G=\mathbb{T}^{1}
and consider case (1) of the theorem. Then
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is an infinite-dimensional Banach space by virtue of Corollary
3.41
and Remark
3.42
, and hence it does not contain any dense finitely generated group. This shows, in particular, that the assumption
\mathop{\rm rank}(R)\geq\aleph_{0}
on the group
R
is necessary. The other assumption
\mathop{\rm rank}(R)\leq\mathfrak{c}
is also necessary, for
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is a Polish group and hence its cardinality is at most
\mathfrak{c}
.

Proof of Theorem
6.32
.

In proving the theorem we proceed in four steps. To avoid a repetition of arguments, at certain points we shall invoke some of the ideas from the proof of Theorem
6.30
.

1st step.
We fix notation and collect some useful facts.

As observed in the second step of the proof of Theorem
6.30
, there is a direct sum
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=A\oplus B
, where
A
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{R}
as an abelian group and
\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}\subseteq B
. Clearly,
A
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. The projection morphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to A
is denoted by
q
.

As in the proof of Theorem
6.30
, we will work in the group of morphisms
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
rather than in the group of extensions
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
. This is possible due to the topological isomorphism
\Phi_{G}
from Theorem
3.1
. Recall that
\Phi_{G}(\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))=\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Also, by virtue of Remark
3.39
and Proposition
6.18
, in both cases (1) and (2) the group
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is open in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. In fact, in case (1) we have
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
and in case (2) there is a topological direct sum
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}=0
. We shall denote by
r
the projection morphism
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Finally, since the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free,

\Phi_{G}\left(\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G))\right)=\mathop{\rm Div}\left(\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\right)=\mathop{\rm Hom}\left(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\right).

Write
\mathfrak{r}=\mathop{\rm rank}(R)
and denote by
\xi
be the smallest ordinal with
\mathop{\rm card}(\xi)=\mathfrak{r}
. Since
\mathfrak{r}\geq\aleph_{0}
, we have
\xi\geq\omega
. Also, for every
\vartheta<\xi
, the ordinals
\eta
with
\eta<\vartheta
form a set, whose cardinality is less than
\mathop{\rm card}(\xi)=\mathfrak{r}
.

Fix finite sets
F_{n}\subseteq G^{*}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) and open sets
\mathcal{U}_{\chi,n}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
(
\chi\in F_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
) in such a way that the sets
\mathcal{V}_{n}=\bigcap_{\chi\in F_{n}}[\chi,\mathcal{U}_{\chi,n}]
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) form a basis for
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
. Since
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is open in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, all the sets
\mathcal{U}_{\chi,n}
are open in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Consequently, by virtue of Theorem
6.30
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
there is a monomorphism
k_{n}\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
such that
k_{n}(\chi)\in\mathcal{U}_{\chi,n}
for every
\chi\in F_{n}
.

Notice from Proposition
6.21
that
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
is in general not an open subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and so the morphisms
k_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
) need not take their values in
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. To overcome this difficulty, set
h_{n}=rk_{n}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
). Then
h_{n}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and we show that all
h_{n}
are monomorphisms
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

•

We claim that
h_{n}
is a monomorphism. To this end, fix
\lambda\in G^{*}
with
h_{n}(\lambda)=0
. Then
rk_{n}(\lambda)=0
. Since
k_{n}
takes its values in the groupoid
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, which intersects the complementary summand
C
of
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
only at the identity
0
, it follows that
k_{n}(\lambda)=0
. Thus,
\lambda=0
, for
k_{n}
is a monomorphism.

•

We claim that
h_{n}
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. So assume that
h_{n}\neq 0
and fix
\lambda\in G^{*}
with
h_{n}(\lambda)
non-minimal. Then
d(rk_{n}(\lambda))=dh_{n}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
. As observed above, we have
C\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}=0
. Thus, without loss of generality, we may assume that
d
satisfies
d(k_{n}(\lambda)-rk_{n}(\lambda))\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
dk_{n}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Since
k_{n}\in\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})\setminus 0
, it follows that
\lambda=0
. Thus, indeed,
\mathop{\rm im}(h_{n})\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

Finally, for
\omega\leq\eta<\xi
set
F_{\eta}=0
,
\mathcal{U}_{0,\eta}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
\mathcal{V}_{\eta}=[0,\mathcal{U}_{0,\eta}]=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
and
h_{\eta}=0\in\mathcal{V}_{\eta}
.

2nd step.
We show that there are morphisms
g_{\eta}\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
(
\eta<\xi
), such that the following conditions are satisfied for every
\vartheta<\xi
:

(
a_{\vartheta}
)

g_{\eta}\in\mathcal{V}_{\eta}
for every
\eta\leq\vartheta
,

(
b_{\vartheta}
)

(g_{\eta})_{\eta\leq\vartheta}
is an independent family in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
,

(
c_{\vartheta}
)

the morphisms
g_{\eta}
(
\eta\leq\vartheta
) generate a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

In constructing such a family of morphisms we shall proceed by transfinite induction on
\vartheta
.

Fix
\theta<\xi
and assume that a family of morphisms
g_{\eta}\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
(
\eta<\theta
) is given, satisfying conditions (
a_{\vartheta}
)–(
c_{\vartheta}
) for every
\vartheta<\theta
. We need to construct a morphism
g_{\theta}\colon G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
so that conditions (
a_{\theta}
)–(
c_{\theta}
) be satisfied. Notice that by our choice of
\xi
,
\mathop{\rm card}(\theta)<\mathop{\rm card}(\xi)=\mathfrak{r}\leq\mathfrak{c}
.

Denote by
D
the divisible hull
D=\mathbb{Q}\otimes E
of the group

E=qh_{\theta}(G^{*})+\sum_{\eta<\theta}qg_{\eta}(G^{*}).

(6.41)

Since
E\subseteq A
and
A
is divisible, we have
D\subseteq A
. Moreover, by divisibility of both
D
and
A
, there is a direct sum
A=A^{\prime}\oplus D
for an appropriate divisible subgroup
A^{\prime}
of
A
. Further, since
\mathop{\rm rank}(D)=\mathop{\rm rank}(E)\leq\mathop{\rm card}(E)\leq\aleph_{0}\mathop{\rm card}(\theta)<\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathop{\rm rank}(A)=\mathop{\rm rank}(\mathbb{R})=\mathfrak{c}
, it follows that
\mathop{\rm rank}(A^{\prime})=\mathfrak{c}
. Thus,
A^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\mathfrak{c})}\cong\mathbb{R}
. We let
q^{\prime}
stand for the projection morphism
A\to A^{\prime}
.

Now, since
\mathop{\rm rank}(G^{*})\leq\aleph_{0}<\mathfrak{c}=\mathop{\rm rank}(A^{\prime})
, we may repeat the arguments from the third step of the proof of Theorem
6.30
to obtain a monomorphism
k\colon G^{*}\to A^{\prime}
with
(h_{\theta}+k)(\chi)\in\mathcal{U}_{\chi,\theta}
for every
\chi\in F_{\theta}
. Set
g_{\theta}=h_{\theta}+k
. Since both
h_{\theta}
and
k
take their values in
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
g_{\theta}
is a morphism
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. We verify the validity of (
a_{\theta}
)–(
c_{\theta}
).

First,
g_{\theta}\in\mathcal{V}_{\theta}
by definition of
g_{\theta}
and
g_{\eta}\in\mathcal{V}_{\eta}
for
\eta<\theta
by the induction hypothesis. This verifies condition (
a_{\theta}
). To verify conditions (
b_{\theta}
) and (
c_{\theta}
), the following statements must be checked:

•

the morphisms
g_{\eta}
(
\eta\leq\theta
) are independent in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
,

•

every non-zero element of
\langle(g_{\eta})_{\eta\leq\theta}\rangle
is a monomorphism,

•

every element of
\langle(g_{\eta})_{\eta\leq\theta}\rangle
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.

We verify all these three statements at once by showing that the following condition holds.

(s)

Let
d
be a positive integer,
l_{\eta}
(
\eta\leq\theta
) be integers (all but finitely many of them zero) and
1\neq\lambda\in G^{*}
be a character with
d\sum_{\eta\leq\theta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
l_{\eta}=0
for every
\eta\leq\theta
.

So fix
d
,
l_{\eta}
(
\eta\leq\theta
) and
\lambda
as above. (Observe that
G^{*}\neq 1
by our assumptions on
G
.) By virtue of (
6.41
) and by definition of
D
,
A^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
,
q^{\prime}qh_{\theta}=q^{\prime}qg_{\eta}=0
for every
\eta<\theta
. By definition of
A^{\prime}
,
q^{\prime}
and
k
,
q^{\prime}qk=k
. Consequently,

\begin{split}0&=q^{\prime}(0)=q^{\prime}q(B)\supseteq q^{\prime}q(\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}})\ni q^{\prime}q\left(d\sum_{\eta\leq\theta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)\right)\\
&=q^{\prime}q\left(dl_{\theta}h_{\theta}(\lambda)+dl_{\theta}k(\lambda)+d\sum_{\eta<\theta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)\right)=dl_{\theta}k(\lambda).\end{split}

Since
k
is a monomorphism and the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free, it follows that
l_{\theta}=0
and hence
d\sum_{\eta<\theta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Fix
\vartheta<\theta
with
l_{\eta}=0
for every
\eta>\vartheta
. Then

d\sum_{\eta\leq\vartheta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)=d\sum_{\eta<\theta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}.

Since
\lambda\neq 1
, condition (
c_{\vartheta}
) yields
\sum_{\eta\leq\vartheta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}(\lambda)=0
. By applying (
c_{\vartheta}
) once more we obtain
\sum_{\eta\leq\vartheta}l_{\eta}g_{\eta}=0
. By virtue of (
b_{\vartheta}
), this implies
l_{\eta}=0
for every
\eta\leq\vartheta
. Thus,
l_{\eta}=0
for every
\eta\leq\theta
, which verifies statement (s).

3rd step.
We verify statement (i) of the theorem.

Let a group
R
satisfy the assumptions of the theorem. We need to find an isomorphic copy
\mathcal{R}
of
R
within
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
in such a way that
\mathcal{R}
form a dense subset of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
.

First, since
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
is a divisible torsion-free abelian group, it follows that so is
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
. Further, by the second step of the proof, the morphisms
g_{\eta}
(
\eta<\xi
) form an independent family in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
with cardinality
\mathop{\rm card}(\xi)
and the set
\{g_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
. Finally, since
\mathop{\rm card}(\xi)=\mathfrak{r}=\mathop{\rm rank}(R)
, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
contains an isomorphic copy
\mathcal{R}
of
R
, for which
(g_{\eta})_{\eta<\xi}
is a maximal independent set. In particular,
\mathcal{R}\supseteq\{g_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
. Also, by the second step of the proof, the subgroup
\mathcal{S}=\langle(g_{\eta})_{\eta<\xi}\rangle
of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
generated by the morphisms
g_{\eta}
(
\eta<\xi
) is contained in
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

To finish the proof of statement (i), we need to show that the group
\mathcal{R}
is contained in
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. So fix
h\in\mathcal{R}
. By definition of
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{R}
, there is a positive integer
d\in\mathbb{N}
with
dh\in\mathcal{S}
. Then
dh\in\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. If
dh=0
then also
h=0
, for the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is torsion-free. If
dh
is a monomorphism
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
then
h
is clearly a monomorphism
G^{*}\to\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Moreover,
h
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, for
\kappa_{d}^{-1}(\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1)\subseteq\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}\setminus 1
. This verifies the desired inclusion
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

4th step.
We verify statement (ii) of the theorem. First observe that under the assumption (1), the group
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}(G)
is divisible by virtue of Remark
3.39
and Theorem
4.14
. Thus, in this case, statement (ii) follows at once from statement (i). For this reason, we shall restrict ourselves to the case (2).

Let a group
R
satisfy the assumptions of the theorem. We need to find an isomorphic copy of
R\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{(\aleph_{0})}
within
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
that would form a dense subset of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

By the third step of the proof,
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
contains a subgroup of the form
\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Q}
such that the following conditions hold:

•

\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Q}
is contained in
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

•

the group
\mathcal{R}
(in fact, an appropriate countable independent subset of
\mathcal{R}
) is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
,

•

there are isomorphisms of groups
\mathcal{R}\cong R
and
\mathcal{Q}\cong\mathbb{Q}^{(\aleph_{0})}
.

Let
(f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a rational linear basis for
\mathcal{Q}
. Since the group
G^{*}
is countable by the assumptions of the theorem, we may assume that
f_{m}\to 0
point-wise on
G^{*}
. (This is accomplished by replacing
(f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
by
((1/k_{m})f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
with an appropriately chosen sequence of positive integers
(k_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
.)

Set
n=\mathop{\rm rank}(H_{1}^{w}(\mathcal{F}))
. By virtue of Proposition
6.18
,
there is a topological direct sum
\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Consequently, there is a topological direct sum

\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})=\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))\oplus\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n}).

(6.42)

By virtue of (
6.9
) from Theorem
6.14
,
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}=0
. Since the group
G^{*}
is countable, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
is separable. Let
(h_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
such that the set
\{h_{m}:m\geq k\}
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Now let
\mathcal{Z}
be the subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
generated by the morphisms
f_{m}+h_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
). We shall verify the following statements:

(
\alpha
)

the morphisms
f_{m}+h_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) are independent in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and hence the group
\mathcal{Z}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{(\aleph_{0})}
,

(
\beta
)

\mathcal{R}\cap\mathcal{Z}=0
,

(
\gamma
)

\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Z}
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,

(
\delta
)

\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Z}
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
.

From statements (
\alpha
)–(
\delta
) the claim of this step of the proof will follow. First, let us mention that for
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
we write
l=(l_{1},\dots,l_{m})
,
\varphi_{l}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}l_{i}f_{i}
and
\psi_{l}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}l_{i}h_{i}
. We shall use repeatedly that
\mathop{\rm im}(\varphi_{l})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and
\mathop{\rm im}(\psi_{l})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}
for all
l
.

To verify (
\alpha
), fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
with
\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l}=0
; we show that
l=0
. From the identity
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cap\mathbb{Z}^{n}=0
it follows that
\varphi_{l}=\psi_{l}=0
. Thus, since
f_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) are independent in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
,
l=0
.

To verify (
\beta
), fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
with
\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l}\in\mathcal{R}
; we show that
l=0
and hence
\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l}=0
. Since
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
and
\varphi_{l}
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, it follows that
\psi_{l}
also takes its values in
\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. Thus
\mathop{\rm im}(\psi_{l})\subseteq\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\cap\mathbb{Z}^{n}=0
and hence
\psi_{l}=0
. It follows that
\varphi_{l}\in\mathcal{R}\cap\mathcal{Q}=0
and so, as above,
l=0
.

We verify (
\gamma
). To this end, fix
g\in\mathcal{R}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
; we check that
g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l})\in\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. We proceed in two steps.

•

We show that
g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l})\in\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
. So assume that
1\neq\lambda\in G^{*}
is such that
(g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l}))(\lambda)=0
. Since
g(\lambda)+\varphi_{l}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and
\psi_{l}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we obtain
g(\lambda)+\varphi_{l}(\lambda)=\psi_{l}(\lambda)=0
. Consequently, it follows from the inclusions
g+\varphi_{l}\in\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
that
g+\varphi_{l}=0
. Since
\mathcal{R}\cap\mathcal{Q}=0
, we have
g=\varphi_{l}=0
and hence
l=0
. Thus,
g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l})=0
, as was to be shown.

•

We show that
g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l})
takes its values in
\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. So assume that
1\neq\lambda\in G^{*}
is such that
(g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l}))(\lambda)
is not minimal. Fix
d\in\mathbb{N}
with
d(g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l}))(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Since
\psi_{l}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}}=0
, we may assume that
d\psi_{l}(\lambda)\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then
d(g(\lambda)+\varphi_{l}(\lambda))\in\mathop{\rm{\bf B}_{\mathcal{F}}}
. Further,
g+\varphi_{l}\in\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathop{\rm Mon}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf MinZ}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, whence
g+\varphi_{l}=0
. Thus, as above,
g+(\varphi_{l}+\psi_{l})=0
.

We finish the proof of the theorem by verifying (
\delta
). First, the group
\mathcal{R}
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm Div}(\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}))
by definition. Second, the group
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathbb{Z}^{n})
is contained in the closure of
\mathcal{Z}
by our choice of
(f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(h_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. Thus, by virtue of (
6.42
), the direct sum
\mathcal{R}\oplus\mathcal{Z}
is dense in
\mathop{\rm Hom}(G^{*},\mathop{\rm{\bf Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
, as was to be shown.
∎
